Every plate and column and six-armed thing across forty feet turned on its own axis at once and stood parallel, and then they stopped being crystals and started being needles, elongated along the field, drawn out of the shape water makes and into the shape a field makes when the gradient runs stronger than the bonds holding the lattice square.

What came out along those open lines was the entire pressure the corotating region had been holding, released in the only direction still available to it, and it came out as a beam because an opening that narrow cannot produce anything else.

The pulse came through the ring of junctures on eleven curved paths and arrived at him.

Mu-jin barely saw it. That is the exact truth of it: the eye took the whole event in, all eleven arrivals with their geometry and their gradient and their timing, and the man behind the eye got perhaps the first two of them before the arithmetic outran him.

The other nine went in.

His body did not fall down.

It should have. He was standing on a road with a polearm through his chest and one lung and a right arm that had been dead six weeks and had frozen solid an hour ago, and eleven curved arrivals had just gone through the whole architecture, and the correct behaviour of that body was to stop.

It stayed up.

He was not doing it. That was the part he would think about afterward, on the two occasions he was able to think about it at all. There was no signal going down from him into the legs, because there was very little of him left up there to send one, and the legs held the road anyway and the spine held the legs and something adjusted the left foot four inches to keep the line under the load.

The vessel was moving itself.

*I am cargo.*

*Whatever this is, it has been carrying me since the ditch bank and I have been taking credit for it, and it is not obeying me and it never was, and I am going to be dead in about ninety seconds and I would very much like to have found out what it is first.*

He got the left hand up.

It took most of what remained. The shoulder answered late and the elbow answered later and the hand came across his chest and stopped at the sternum, and there was nothing on the right side to bring to meet it, and he brought the dead arm up with the living one, hooked under the wrist, and set the two palms together.

Blood came out of his mouth.

It left him warm and it did not stay warm. It went from liquid to solid in the air between his lips and his hands with no interval in the middle, straight through, no drops, and what landed on the backs of his fingers was a scatter of small bright crystals that had been the inside of him a quarter-second earlier.

He set his teeth in what was left and said it.

"Domain Expansion."

The valley took a breath.

"Open Crucible."

A Domain seals.

That is the whole of what a Domain is and every practitioner alive learns it the same way: you draw a boundary and inside the boundary your terms are the terms, and the strength of the thing is the strength of the wall, and everything that matters about it is exclusion. A Domain keeps the world out. That is why the great ones are described by their walls and why the counter to every one of them that has ever been catalogued is to break the wall.

His had no wall.

It went out from his joined hands to a radius of about two hundred feet and it did not exclude one thing. Everything on that road at that moment came inside it: the ice and the needles and the wreck and the dead, the eleven junctures still spindling in their ring, the pulse still arriving along its curved paths, the whole standing black and the thing at the centre of it, and Kwon Mu-jin, and Zarakal, and every drop of Mu-jin's blood on another man's plate.

An open crucible accepts the charge. All of it. That is the first step of an assay and there is no assay without it, and any assayer will tell you that the sample you exclude is the sample you have already decided the answer for.

Then it began to work.

Assay is a separation by differential affinity and nothing else. You bring the whole charge up together and you supply the one condition, and under that condition the base content reacts and the noble content does not, and that is the entire principle and it has not changed since the Bronze Age. What reacts becomes fusible and drops its surface tension and gets drawn into the porous wall by capillary action. What does not react stays on the surface, and coalesces as the rest is taken away, and at the end there is a bead.

The condition Mu-jin supplied was ownership.

Everything in that Domain was assayed against the question of whether it belonged to the thing carrying it.

The borrowed content went first.

Across two hundred feet the field began to lose the material that had been drawn into it out of somewhere else, and there was a very great deal of that, because the Ashgate valley had been the richest and most polluted Aether in four worlds since the ninth hour. Aetheric Residue off the aurora. Off a shield spending itself grey. From three charges out of a dead smith's belt. From four hundred paces of men dying with workings half made. From a leak out of Mu-jin's own seams that he had been ashamed of for an hour.

All of it went to the walls.

And the black at the centre of the ring began to come off the man wearing it.

Nothing stripped it and nothing tore it. It oxidised, in the exact sense: it met the condition, it reacted, and having reacted it stopped being able to hold surface tension against the thing it had been sitting on, and it slid. Mu-jin watched a working that had drunk an aurora slide off its bearer in sheets and go outward into the walls of a Domain, and the sliding had a play of colour on it at the trailing edge as the last of it went, and no assayer who has ever stood at a muffle would have failed to recognise the sight.

What remained on him after that was smaller and it was entirely his.

*There. That is the reading, and it is the first honest one anybody has taken off him in forty years. That is his own Ardency, at his own Depth, on his own Wellspring, without the district he has been drinking, and it is not small and it is not nothing and it would kill nine tenths of everybody breathing.*

*But it is not what he was thirty seconds ago, and he has never in forty years had to fight anybody with it.*

The walls of a crucible have to be porous and they have to be made of something that will not react with what they take.

Cupels are made of bone ash.

Mu-jin found that out at the same moment as everybody else, which is to say at the moment the absorbed material reached the boundary of his Domain and had to go somewhere, and the boundary of his Domain was not in the air.

It was in him.

Every long bone, and the Lattice through them. The femurs first because they are the largest reservoir, then the humerus on both sides including the dead one, then the pelvis and the ribs and the vault of the skull, and the sensation was not pain and had no relation to pain. It was fullness. It was the specific unbearable fullness of a vessel taking on more than it was fired to hold, and it arrived from the inside of the marrow outward, and it did not stop, because the charge was two hundred feet across and the walls were one man.

Bone ash is calcium phosphate, and calcium phosphate is chosen for a cupel because it is chemically inert to what it absorbs and porous enough to take it, and a man's skeleton is the same material in a different arrangement. The Lattice through it makes it the most absorbent structure in a body and always has, which is why every practitioner's Resilience is read off the bone and never off the flesh.

He held it open anyway.

*Nine tenths of an assay is the temperature. Too low and the litharge crusts and seals the bead in and you have learned nothing and destroyed the sample. Too high and you lose part of what you were trying to weigh.*

*I have no thermometer. I have never had one. I have the same instrument I have always had, which is that I can tell what a thing is worth by looking at it, and I have four seconds of usable judgement left in me.*

The Domain held.

And in the middle of it, on the cleared road, with the borrowed sheeting away into the bones of a schoolmaster and the ring of junctures still turning above them both, the last of the base content went off the bead.

The play of colours ran across it and finished.

Kwon Mu-jin took his hands apart, and dragged air into the one lung he had, and looked at what was standing in front of him with an eye that had gone entirely white from lid to lid.

Haruki braced herself the light in front of her carving a way around her as the Musuhi and Danshi resonated vibrantly. The Kokudan shifting as the darkness parted around her figure, the light and energy siphoning and dissoluting into a blinding light, her hand pressed up against her chest as she allowed the vital pulse of hope fill her veins.

The corruption and the impurity burning away as she held onto the warmth that was produced from her core. Her Kokudan focusing as she shifted herself, the harmonic pulse shifting and turning as the tone shifted. The darkness had been answered by **Harmonic Arts, Concordia**, Haruki's calm and yet resonate state shifting as the balance between the darkness and light had been formed. The Kokudan weaved and tied the forces into a lock, the joined pressure between each other creating a singularity. The expression mixed and bursted, but found harmony in their stability. The essence shifted as the heavy storm surrounded them formed something natural, something disastrous. 

"I have no choice... I must utilize all that I have." She said, her eyes shifting as the darkness was contained, stabilized, balanced. The Harmony created a field that expanded outward between both parties, the shared dominion forming out of the opposing forces and the distant pulses.

Haruki's essence thrived here, her soul began to shine through the vessel of her physical form, the outlines of her balanced spirit had begun to shine through the darkness and pierce through the light. The balance shifted between pulses as the light began to spread out, the counter-shock behind the void beginning to shrink, but still kept it's consistency.

Haruki's frame shifted as she placed her palm up against the surface of the air. "Harmonic Arts, Confiscating Pulse." The balance shifted as the wave washed over Kujo, his body began to reject and recoil, his own traits shifting and disassociating with his current vessel. Haruki's frame leaning forward to continue consistency-between the darkness and light. Her eyes beginning to bleed as the Kokudan worked in tandem with her present power.

Dawn Guard saw the first monster fall before it ever reached the ground. It came down through the blackened sky with too many limbs and a ribbed body plated in something that looked half-grown and half-forged, its impact cratering the street hard enough to throw stone across the ruined district. Zuberi was already moving before the dust finished rising, Valkyros in one hand and Nyxion in the other. The creature barely had time to lift its head.

Dawn Guard crossed both blades once. The first cut severed three limbs and opened the torso from shoulder to hip, while the second passed cleanly through the thing’s neck before its body understood it had been divided. Black fluid sprayed across broken stone as the pieces slid apart beneath the white mantle at Zuberi’s back. He did not look down at it.

More were falling.

They came in every shape the Void could justify: skeletal things with molten cores, hunched quadrupeds armored in black shell, floating masses wrapped in tendrils, and artificial soldiers landing already upright with weapons grown directly from their arms. Some struck rooftops and tore through them, while others landed in streets already crowded with survivors and wounded soldiers. Dawn Guard turned toward the densest cluster and moved without hesitation.

“GET AWAY FROM THE OPEN ROADS! ANYTHING THAT FALLS INSIDE THESE WALLS DIES BEFORE IT REACHES THE PEOPLE!”

A winged creature dropped toward a group of fleeing civilians, and Zuberi vanished from the ground before its talons could descend. He met it in the air with Valkyros buried through its sternum, twisted his body, and drove both of them sideways through a shattered tower wall. Before the monster could close its claws around him, Nyxion cut across its abdomen and severed the lower half cleanly away. Dawn Guard kicked the remains from the building and landed beneath the next descending shadow.

A colossal thing struck fifty yards away and folded the street beneath its weight. Zuberi planted one foot, drew both swords low, and waited until the creature committed to its first charge. The moment its enormous arm came down, he stepped inside the blow rather than away from it and cut upward through the joint. Valkyros followed directly through the center of its chest.

The creature roared.

“TOO SLOW.”

Nyxion came across horizontally and separated the monster’s upper body from the lower half. The severed torso skidded through black rain while Zuberi continued forward without breaking stride. There was no triumph in his expression, only the hard concentration of a ruler watching his kingdom become another front in a war that refused to end. Then he saw Asante.

The boy sat amid a collapsed section of stone wall twenty yards behind him, partially buried beneath rubble and dust. Blood ran from one side of Asante’s forehead, one sleeve had been torn open, and every breath visibly shook his shoulders. He was alive, but barely composed. His eyes were fixed upward.

Another swarm came through Val Nihil.

Something inside Asante tightened.

The Obsession Force around his hands and forearms appeared in dark crimson-black strands, coiling closer to his skin as his breathing became shallower. Every new creature falling from the sky looked like Kujo reaching into another place Asante cared about and deciding it should be ruined too. He could still see Vaeloris when he closed his eyes.

“Kujo…”

His voice came out low the first time.

The name barely sounded like speech. Asante’s fingers curled into the rubble beneath him, and the stone cracked under the pressure of his grip. The crimson-black strands climbed higher along his arms.

Then he snapped.

**“KUJO!”**

The scream tore through the ruined district.

The Obsession Force whipped outward around him in dark crimson arcs as his entire body shook with rage. He stared into the blackened sky as though Kujo himself were standing somewhere behind every falling monster. Tears mixed with the blood and dust on his face.

**“HE KEEPS DOING IT! HE KEEPS TAKING EVERYTHING!”**

Dawn Guard cut another creature apart before it reached the rubble. He pivoted, drove Nyxion backward through the skull of a charging Void soldier without looking, then pulled the blade free and placed himself between Asante and the next wave. His posture changed the moment he faced the boy. The battlefield could wait one second.

“Asante. Look at me.”

The boy did not answer.

Another monster landed behind Zuberi.

Dawn Guard threw Valkyros.

The blade spun end over end through the air and buried itself through the creature’s face, carrying enough force to drive the entire body backward through the side of a ruined house. Zuberi extended one hand, called the sword back, and caught it without taking his eyes off Asante. The boy finally looked at him.

His eyes were wet with rage.

**“HE TOOK VAELORIS!”**

The crimson-black strands thickened around his arms.

**“HE TOOK HER!”**

Asante slammed one fist into the rubble beside him, cracking the stone beneath his knuckles. The hatred boiling through him had become so dense that even the air around his hands seemed to tighten. His face twisted as the next scream came out of him.

**“AND NOW HE’S DOING IT AGAIN!”**

Dawn Guard’s expression hardened.

“I know.”

Asante’s head lowered for only a second before snapping back up. The Obsession Force coiled tighter around his wrists and forearms, reacting to the Name at the center of everything he felt. His breathing became ragged enough that each inhale sounded painful. Then the words erupted from him.

**“I HATE HIM!”**

Dawn Guard held his gaze.

“I know.”

Another explosion sounded somewhere beyond the district, followed by the roar of something enormous landing outside the walls. Zuberi still did not look away from Asante. He had learned already that sometimes the most dangerous thing in front of him was not the monster falling from the sky. Sometimes it was what survived inside the people beneath it.

**“I WANT HIM DEAD!”**

The words came louder.

**“I WANT KUJO DEAD!”**

Dawn Guard did not rebuke him.

“That is different.”

Asante stared at him through blood, tears, and fury. Zuberi’s expression did not soften, but neither did it condemn him. His answer came with the calm certainty Asante’s rage lacked.

“If you want him dead, survive long enough to become capable of doing something about it. But if all you do is hate him, then he still gets to decide what you become.”

Asante’s expression twisted.

**“HE DESERVES IT!”**

“Yes.”

Dawn Guard answered without hesitation.

“He does.”

That caught Asante harder than denial would have. The crimson-black strands around his arms trembled, still violent but no longer expanding blindly. Dawn Guard stepped onto a slab of broken stone and positioned himself directly between the boy and the sky full of descending shapes. Valkyros and Nyxion dripped black fluid at his sides.

“Hate him if you need to.”

Zuberi raised both swords.

“Just do not become his.”

Asante stared at him.

The Obsession Force did not disappear.

It shook around his arms, resisting the thought almost as violently as he did. Then he looked back toward the black sky and screamed with enough force to tear his throat raw.

“I’M NOT HIS!”

Dawn Guard’s eyes sharpened.

“Good.”

The monsters hit.

Dawn Guard moved.

Valkyros flashed through the first creature’s throat, Nyxion severed the second at the waist, and Zuberi drove his shoulder into the third hard enough to knock it beneath the path of a descending fourth. He cut both apart in the same motion, using their collapsing bodies as cover against a burst of black projectiles from above. The white mantle behind him snapped violently through the hot wind.

Asante watched from the rubble.

His anger did not leave.

If anything, it became clearer.

The hatred for Kujo remained boiling beneath his ribs, but Dawn Guard’s words gave it a shape that was not immediately consuming him. He pushed one hand against the broken stone beside him and forced himself onto one knee.

Another monster landed nearby.

Asante looked at it. The crimson-black strands around his hands tightened.

“I’M NOT HIS!”

He forced himself higher.

“I’LL NEVER BE HIS!”

Dawn Guard heard him and did not turn around. A massive creature dropped directly in front of Zuberi, its body wrapped in living armor with six arms ending in hooked blades. Dawn Guard stepped forward before it could fully rise and drove both swords through its chest from opposite angles. Black fluid burst across him.

“GOOD.”

He tore the blades outward.

The monster split open.

Behind him, Asante dragged himself farther from the rubble, damaged, exhausted, and still furious. The hatred remained hot enough that even thinking Kujo’s Name made the dark strands around his arms constrict, but now the anger belonged to Asante again. Kujo had caused it, but Kujo would not own it.

Dawn Guard stood between him and the rain of monsters.

More shapes descended from Val Nihil.

Zuberi looked up into the black sky and raised Valkyros and Nyxion.

“COME ON, THEN.”

His stance lowered.

“EVERY LAST ONE OF YOU.”

Asante rose from the rubble slowly at first, one hand braced against broken stone while the other curled so tightly his knuckles whitened beneath the grime and blood. The pain in his body had not left him, but it no longer mattered, because something far sharper had begun to move through him. The Obsession Force answered his will the instant he stood, dark crimson-black strands unfurling from his arms like living threads drawn out by fury and purpose alike. They did not lash wildly this time, nor did they tremble without direction, because Asante’s hatred had finally found something cleaner than panic to obey.

The monsters were still falling from the void in waves, crashing through rooftops, landing in streets, and crawling from fresh craters with shrieks that made the damaged district shudder. Asante lifted his head and looked directly into the blackened sky, and the Obsession Force reacted as though his gaze itself had become an order. Invisible pressure spread outward from him in a tightening sphere, not loud, not theatrical, but terrifying in how absolute it felt. Each creature that crossed into that range seemed to meet something denser than air and harsher than steel, their bodies halting mid-descent before beginning to buckle under the certainty of his will.

The first one died without Asante ever touching it. Its spine bent backward with a sickening crack, its limbs twisted inward, and then the entire thing collapsed into itself as the Obsession Force constricted around its existence like a judgment that had already been decided. A second followed, then a third, and soon the street before him was filled with monsters dropping from the sky only to be crushed, split, or imploded before their claws ever reached the ground. It was not simply force that was killing them, but the overwhelming refusal of Asante’s will to permit their continuation anywhere near him.

**“I SAID STAY DOWN!”**

His voice tore out of him in a raw, furious roar as the Obsession Force surged harder around his frame. **“IF YOU FALL HERE, THEN DIE HERE!”** The crimson-black strands shot upward in branching arcs, catching a cluster of descending void-beasts all at once and ripping through them midair, leaving black blood and shattered remains to rain uselessly across the ruined stone.

The more Asante stood in that rage, the more the Obsession Force seemed to understand him. It no longer moved like something he was barely holding together, but like a power that had recognized its master and chosen to sharpen around his intent. Every monster that entered his sightline was met by a suffocating pressure, an invasive certainty, and then death swift enough to seem preordained. By the time the next wave came down, they were already dying, their bodies splitting apart in the air as if the world itself had accepted Asante’s hatred as a law they could not survive.

Asante remained standing in the middle of the ruin, chest heaving, eyes burning, and the darkness around his arms coiling with a dreadful elegance that made even the surviving creatures hesitate. He was still young, still hurt, and still shaking with anger, but none of that changed what the battlefield had begun to understand about him. The Obsession Force had reacted to his will with perfect obedience, and now the monsters falling from the void were learning that getting close to Asante no longer meant a fight. It meant dying beneath a power so sure of itself that even terror had no time to arrive before the end.


The gate put him out four thousand feet over a burning city and did not slow him down on the way.

Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum fell through the black.

He had time to read it. That was what nine months in the ash had actually bought, more than the seven Fists and more than the matrix under them: he could take a system apart while it was still arriving. The cloud base over Kharven was not cloud. It was a working, laid over the whole basin, and the light that should have been coming through it was being refused rather than blocked, and there is a difference and the difference was the handhold.

He put the first of the seven into it.

Nothing in it resembled a strike. Ruin is the fist that finds the ending already on its way and makes it arrive correctly, and the working over Kharven had an ending in it, because everything does, and all Darius did was locate the place where it had already begun to fail and stop being polite about the timetable.

The sky came apart in a gradient.

It arrived as a graduation and never as a hole. Sunlight came through the black in a series of small illuminations, thousands of them, opening in a scattered arithmetic across four miles of overcast and brightening in sequence, and where each one opened the black around it thinned instead of tearing.

Then the whole thing stood open and he came down through it.

Terminal velocity on a man of his mass in that air is about a hundred and twenty miles in an hour. He was going considerably faster than that and had been since the gate, because the Fourth Work is order and nothing else and a body falling under order does not accept the drag term.

Anyone on the ground looking up saw a figure in the light. He knew that and could do nothing about it and had spent eleven years of correspondence trying to prevent exactly this, and it was happening now over a capital with sixty thousand people in it.

The filaments came off him at about two thousand feet.

Aetheric Density in the column he was falling through went from the ordinary saturation of a burning district to something no instrument in Kharven was rated for, in the space of eleven hundred feet, and it did it as a gradient with no front to it anywhere, and every practitioner in the basin felt it in the sternum before anybody saw the light.

They came out of the Lattice along the long bones and out through the skin and stood off him in a fine radiant floss, and every one of them was expressing at the Wellspring stratum without being asked. Judgement, in its unmediated form, which no living man had put into open air in three hundred years, and it had two operations and they were both running.

The first is the putrefaction of what is corrupt.

The second is the destruction of Obsession.

There were monsters in the streets.

He had read the taxonomy in a warm room in Altherion and had signed the appendix. Void constructs, assembled and never born, and the assembly is the whole of them: a working folded into a shape, and the shape does the work, and the material it is folded out of is ordinary and inert and could be anything.

And a fold can be undone without touching the material.

The light reached the first of them at the head of the Cooper's quarter and their forms began to twist.

No burning anywhere. The structures went slack. The precise geometry that had made a thing a thing came apart at the level of the fold while every particle of what it had been made from stayed exactly where it was, and what had been a construct with a function became the same quantity of matter with no function at all, malleable, running, arriving on the flags in a heap that was chemically identical to the thing that had been standing there and could not do one thing.

It is the same operation that takes the function out of an egg-white over a low flame. The chain is untouched, every unit of it accounted for, and the fold is gone and the fold was the entire performance, and no amount of cooling brings it back.

The working itself was stripped out of them.

And underneath that the second operation went to work, and the second operation is the one that killed them.

A Void construct is sustained by refusal. That is what Obsession Force is and the Accord has known it for four hundred years and has never found a use for the knowledge: the thing continues because something in it will not stop, and the refusal is load-bearing, and remove the refusal and there is nothing else holding the arrangement together.

Purity does not argue with a refusal. It removes the conditions under which refusing is possible.

They went off across the whole basin. Each one released the binding energy of its own structure at once, which is a small quantity per construct and a very large one across thousands.

Thousands of them, in the streets and on the walls and in the cisterns and in the flooded lower cloister, in a wave that moved outward at the speed of the light front, and each one came apart with a concussion of released structure that took out windows for a hundred feet. The noise of it arrived at Kharven-Seat as one long roll that did not have separate parts in it.

Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum came down through the middle of that and looked past all of it into the black abyss standing over the palace, and was not afraid.

*I have been in the ash nine months.*

*Six of the seven took something off me before they came. The fifth took my certainty about a thing I had believed since I was twenty-six and I have not replaced it. The seventh took the man who carried the other half of my office, and I never learned his face properly, and I paid for it before I knew there was a bill.*

*I have nothing left in me that is available to be frightened with. That is not courage and I will not have it written down as courage.*

*Gimbzo said it in the fourth month and I argued with him for a week. A man who has been emptied is not brave, he is cheap. The dear thing is the man who still has something to lose and comes anyway, and there are eleven hundred of those on the Ashgate road at this moment and not one of them will get a window.*

Ignatius, they gave him at nineteen, for the faith. Unbridled was the assessor's word and the assessor meant it as a caution.

Sanctus at twenty-six, for the commitment, which the Order records as exemplary and which his own file records as a man who would not stop.

And Sanctorum at thirty-five, which is a genitive plural and never a third name, and which means the holiest of the holy things, and which had been conferred four times in eleven hundred years and was not conferred again after him.

The other three are in the Long Reading. Two of them are entered with a date of death and no manner. The third has neither, and the column where the manner should be carries a mark that the Bench has declined eleven times to explain, and Darius had looked it up at thirty-five in a cold room on the night they gave him the name, and had gone back to his quarters and written a letter refusing the seat.

He had gone on refusing it for eleven years, and the Order had gone on addressing him by the whole of the name anyway, because the name is not an office. It is a statement about who the Judger's weapon is, and a man does not get to decline being the thing he is.

The ground began to answer.

Aetherquakes, and no part of that word is metaphor. Nothing about it resembled the ordinary shaking of a district under pressure. The Aether across the whole Kharven basin had been carrying stress since the ninth hour, laid on by the aurora and the ridges and the corona and everything else that had happened in that valley, and stress builds in a lattice until the lattice cracks, and when a lattice under that much strain finally moves it moves everywhere at once.

The basin cracked in slabs and mended behind the cracks.

That was the part that no witness account would agree on afterward. The ground opened, and along the same line, half a second behind, it closed. Streets carved out and laid back down in a different arrangement. Two miles of the outer wall taken to the footing and reassembled four feet to the north with its courses in the right order. Things went up and around and came back and were mended, and the mending had no charity in it at all. It was an ordering. It was the basin being set right in whatever way was cheapest, without any reference to whose house had been standing where.

And in the middle of it the voice arrived.

*Go.*

It had no sound. Darius understood that with the front of his mind while the back of it did what a mind does with weight it cannot hold, which is to produce sentences, and the sentences it produced were these.

*Go now, Light-Bearer of the realms. I have given you the Trait of Purity.*

He had carried it since thirty-five and had never once heard it named aloud by the thing that gave it.

It is not a technique and cannot be trained and appears on no ladder. It is a property of the Lattice, and it is a subtraction, and what it subtracts is coupling. Nothing external integrates into his structure. Nothing sticks. No Residue accumulates in him, no district ever gets into his bones, no working laid on him ever finds purchase, and no borrowed Aether has entered his body since he was thirty-five years old.

The Order writes this up as sanctity. Darius had been correcting them in the margins for eleven years.

*It also means I cannot be given anything.*

*No Synergia. No shared working. No healing from another practitioner's hand, ever, at any price, and I have watched two men die that I could have carried out if a single thing about me would accept a transfer.*

*And it is why they split the office across two of us and walked us out through different doors. The Hand has to receive and I cannot. So one man carries and one man spends, and the carrier was a clerk with beautiful handwriting and no faculty at all, and I met him once for eleven minutes and did not shake his hand.*

*I would like that entered.*

*Heed my call. Hammer of Justice. Come to me.*

The sky parted a second time, higher, past where sky is a word that applies.

Something came down through it. It came in at an angle out of the dark between the primordial stars, arriving along a line laid down for it instead of falling, and it took eleven seconds to cross what it crossed and it came into Darius's right hand.

It was the size of a smith's hammer and it weighed what a smith's hammer weighs and there was nothing else about it that was ordinary.

Degenerate matter. Nuclei crushed out of their arrangement and packed to the density of a star's interior, and Darius knew the number in the abstract and had never expected to be holding it. A thimble of that at a bridge would take the bridge and the river bed under it.

And a piece that small cannot exist.

That is the whole of the thing and it is the reason nobody else has ever carried it. Matter at that density is held together by the weight of the star sitting on top of it, and nothing else, and below a certain mass the gravity is gone and the neutrons are free and a free neutron does not stay a neutron. It decays. The energy sitting in a single one of them is comparable to everything holding the arrangement together, and there are more of them in that hammer head than there are grains of sand in the world.

Every one of them has been trying to leave since the moment it was made.

The Hammer of Justice is a refusal, held. It is the most obstinate object in four worlds and it does exactly one thing, which is to not come apart, and it has been doing that continuously for however long it has existed and it will do it for exactly as long as somebody uncouplable is holding it.

That is why it comes to him and to nobody else. In any other hand the containment would find a channel into the bearer within a second and the bearer would be the first thing to go.

*I do not strike with it.*

*I have never struck with it. There is nothing to swing. What I do is stop holding a measured part of it for a measured length of time and let that part do the thing it has wanted to do since before there was a Kharven.*

There is a figure for how much he is permitted to let go and it is not written down anywhere, because the only man who ever needed it worked it out by feel at thirty-five and has never had occasion to revise it. Too little and the strike is a hammer blow delivered by a man of fifty-six. Too much by any margin at all and there is no Kharven, and no basin, and no Darius, and the Order's fourth entry acquires the same mark in the same column as the third.

He came down into the courtyard of the outer bailey with the light standing off him in filaments and the primordial stars aligning across the whole width of his shoulders, and he did not slow.

He hit the ground.

And opened his hand a fraction.

The aurora went out from the point of impact across four miles of a burning city, and every construct still standing in the basin met the light front, and the fold and the refusal went out of all of them in the same instant, and what had been an army came down onto the streets of Kharven as an ordinary quantity of ordinary matter with nothing left in it that wanted anything.

The concussion of it reached the outer wall and reflected and came back and met itself over the middle of the city and stood there as a standing pressure that would not disperse for eleven minutes.

Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum closed his fist around the haft, and put his other hand flat on the flags, and got up.

With a swift movement of her blade, Haruki worked in tandem with her Danshi, the darkness and light both bisecting as they risen upward into the sky, the ruin being shown clearly through the mixture of light and dark raining down from the sky. The pure holy light of white and the dark corrupting drops of black rained down onto the ground as Haruki remained there facing the palace of Kharven. The silence across the field almost deafening as the shade casted over the desolate palace.

"I know you're here... Darius." She said, her eyes laid in front of her as the weight of her body shifted looking over towards the sky, seeing the presence of Darius as he crossed just through the field of her harmonic arts. The pulse between hums had grown much more powerful since the last time Darius saw her, the silent observation and the loud throbbing rhythm, it was all too familiar now.

"But I cannot stand still any waste any more time. The time for action starts now." She said, her posture straightening as her blade was held in her right hand. Her head was lowered slightly as the wind's direction brushed against her as her Kokudan shined softly. The pulse between the both of them was much different than when they both last saw each other, Darius could see it through perfectly. The struggle, the sorrow, the faith blurred then rebuilt firmly. 

"It hasn't been exactly smooth since you have left, so you picked a rather awkward time to return." She said, her voice was softer, much more weighted, quite similar to how she spoke to in the past, but something held on tighter this time. There was determination, hope, and perhaps a sense of duty. That was all that was needed to be told.

"I've slain thousands of monsters just to get closer to him, all in the name of peace and order. Would you care to join me? Just one more time?" She asked him, her eyes looking over now fully to Darius, her sour expression shifted to something softer, a smile, something small and get genuine. It felt-strange for someone like her, even if it was only a brief gesture.

Asante felt the absence where his Obsession Force had been before he saw it returning. Darius had destroyed its previous expression, scattered the crimson-black strands until there had been nothing left around the boy but pain, rubble, and the memory of power he had failed to hold. Yet Obsession was not merely something Asante possessed; it was recognition that refused release, and his will reached for that refusal again. The first thread formed around his bleeding fingers.

A second curled around his wrist, then ten more followed, rebuilding themselves from the fixation Darius had never actually removed. Crimson-black strands crawled over Asante’s forearms and shoulders, twisting tighter with every breath until the ruined ground beneath him began to vibrate. There was no elegance in the reconstruction, only the violent insistence of a child whose emotions had become powerful enough to demand a shape. When Asante raised his head, every strand pointed toward the same man.

Kujo.

He stood beneath the blackened sky while Val Nihil continued spilling its inhabitants across the Material World. Monsters fell behind him in shapes too numerous to classify, yet Asante saw none of them anymore; every thought had compressed around the pale figure responsible for Vaeloris, Lyria, the black feather, and every nightmare that had followed. His hatred did not need encouragement. It only needed somewhere to go.

Dawn Guard noticed immediately.

Zuberi turned from the creature he had just bisected and saw the Obsession Force gathering around Asante with a density far beyond what the boy had displayed moments earlier. Valkyros lowered slightly in his right hand while Nyxion remained ready in the other, his attention shifting from the falling monsters to the child staring directly at Kujo. He recognized that expression.

“Asante, don’t.”

The boy did not look at him.

“Do not think about him through hate right now. Kujo wants you angry enough that nothing else exists, and you are giving him exactly that.”

Asante’s breathing became ragged. He heard Zuberi, understood every word, and ignored all of it with the complete irrationality only grief and childhood could produce together. His mother’s face came first, followed by Vaeloris, then Kujo standing where all of it had ended. The Obsession Force exploded around him.

“I DON’T CARE!”

Dawn Guard stepped toward him.

“Asante…”

“I WANT HIM DEAD!”

The roar tore from Asante’s throat while the Obsession Force answered like something equally furious. Hundreds of crimson-black strands erupted from the rubble, some narrow enough to resemble threads and others thick as chains, all of them bending toward Kujo with singular purpose. The Plane of Fate tightened around that fixation as Asante’s hatred assigned one destination to everything he had rebuilt.

“KUJO!”

Asante thrust both hands forward.

“DIE!”

The Obsession Force crossed the battlefield.

It came with none of the hesitation of his earlier attacks, surging through broken streets and falling monsters while every strand reinforced the next. Creatures unfortunate enough to cross its path were crushed, perforated, or simply ripped apart beneath the metaphysical certainty driving it forward. The entire torrent converged upon Kujo.

Kujo saw it from the beginning.

He watched Asante rebuild the force, watched Dawn Guard recognize the danger, and heard the warning the boy chose to ignore. There was ample time to evade, interrupt the reconstruction, remove himself from the line of attack, or kill Asante before the first strand ever arrived. Kujo did none of those things.

He smiled.

The Obsession Force struck him.

Crimson-black violence wrapped around Kujo’s torso, shoulders, arms, and throat, tearing through the darkness around him while additional strands hammered him from every direction. Stone detonated beneath his feet and the entire region around him vanished into a storm of dust and ruptured ground. Asante poured everything he had into the impact.

For one instant, hope crossed the boy’s face.

Then Kujo stepped forward from inside the storm.

Obsession still wound around his body, still applying force, and Kujo made no attempt to pretend the attack had failed. It had landed exactly as Asante intended, enough power concentrated upon him that the surrounding world could no longer comfortably contain the exchange. Kujo merely looked through the crimson-black strands toward the child who had fired them.

“I knew you would do that.”

Asante’s eyes widened.

Kujo lifted one finger.

There was no enormous buildup.

No monstrous hand descended from the sky, no Crevice tore the battlefield apart, and no ritual announced what was coming. A razor-thin concentration of Void gathered at the tip of Kujo’s finger, so narrow that the darkness surrounding it seemed almost harmless. Its terrifying quality was not size.

It was purity.

**“Void Absolone.”**


Kujo fired.

The attack crossed the distance before Asante’s perception could properly establish that it had moved. There was no projectile traveling visibly through the air, only a black line that suddenly existed between Kujo and the boy, space along its route stripped of every unnecessary fraction of resistance. Asante’s body had not even begun obeying the instinct to dodge when Dawn Guard moved.

Zuberi saw it.

That was enough.

He abandoned every thought of counterattack and appeared directly in front of Asante with the Shield of Kush already summoned into his arm. The ancient shield came up between the boy and the approaching absence, its cleansed surface erupting with protective force as Zuberi planted both feet into the shattered earth. He had no time to wonder whether it would hold.

“ASANTE, DOWN!”

Void Absolone struck the Shield of Kush.

The shield lasted less than a heartbeat.

The Voidic edge punctured its defensive structure from front to back, passing through divine material and protective authority as though it had discovered the one line neither could afford to lose. Cracks exploded across the shield’s surface before the center collapsed inward around a perfectly narrow hole. The attack continued toward Asante without noticeably slowing.

Dawn Guard understood immediately.

There was no second shield. No sword technique. No Aspect calculation. Only the child behind him.

Something moved through Zuberi that had nothing to do with sovereignty, war, Kushara, or whether Asante technically called him cousin. It was older and simpler than every title he carried, the reflex of a man looking at a child his family had already failed too many times. For one instant, Dawn Guard did not feel like Asante’s cousin.

He felt like his father.

Zuberi dropped the ruined Shield of Kush and turned.

He wrapped his body around Asante.

The boy disappeared behind Dawn Guard’s chest at the exact instant Void Absolone arrived.

**It struck Zuberi through the heart.**

His entire body locked.

The attack passed through flesh without producing an ordinary wound, Voidic corruption flooding from the point of penetration through every internal structure connected to the heart. Zuberi’s lungs blackened first, followed by the liver, kidneys, stomach, vessels, and tissue surrounding them, organs losing their living color as absence spread through the architecture meant to sustain him. His heartbeat attempted one final contraction against something that had already made continuation impossible.

The pain was immediate.

Zuberi screamed.

Not Dawn Guard.

Not the Sovereign.

*Zuberi.*

His arms tightened around Asante reflexively while his back arched, the boy feeling the larger man convulse against him. Black blood poured from Zuberi’s mouth and began leaking from the corners of his eyes, thick streams running across his brown skin as his organs died inside him. Valkyros and Nyxion fell from suddenly useless hands.

Asante could not understand what he was seeing.

One moment Zuberi had been standing in front of him, impossible and immovable as he had always seemed, and the next his entire weight was collapsing against the boy. Asante reached up automatically and caught at his clothes, feeling the warmth disappear with horrifying speed. His mind kept waiting for Zuberi to move.

He did not.

**Dawn Guard fell backward.**

Asante went down with him.

The two struck the rubble together, Zuberi’s body taking most of the impact even in death while Asante remained partially trapped beneath one of his arms. Black blood continued spilling from Dawn Guard’s mouth, staining the gold and black of his clothing while thin trails ran from his motionless eyes. His chest never rose again.

Asante stared. His mouth opened. Nothing came out.


The monsters continued falling from Val Nihil, people continued screaming throughout the ruined district, and the worlds continued enduring Kujo’s devastation. None of it reached Asante anymore. There was only Zuberi lying beside him.

Asante touched his chest. No heartbeat. He shook him once.

*“Zuberi…?”*

Nothing.

Asante’s voice cracked around the name.

“Zuberi?”

He shook harder.

“Zuberi, get up.”

No answer came. Asante’s face folded.

“ZUBERI?!”

The scream tore itself apart halfway through.

He grabbed Zuberi’s clothing with both hands and pulled, as though enough force could make the dead man sit up and complain about the treatment. Black blood smeared across Asante’s fingers, and he stared at it with the stunned incomprehension of a child encountering something his mind fundamentally refused to accept. Dawn Guard had always gotten back up.

“NO…”

His breathing broke.

“NO, NO, NO…ZUBERI!”

Asante collapsed over him. The first sob was small. The next one shook his entire body.

Then whatever restraint remained inside the boy failed completely, and he buried his face against Zuberi’s chest while crying hard enough that breathing became difficult. His fingers clenched into the dead man’s clothing, refusing to release him even as the Obsession Force surrounding them began to react.

*“I’M SORRY!”*

The crimson-black strands rose.

*“I’M SORRY!”*

They twisted around Asante and Zuberi like a storm.

*“PLEASE GET UP!”*

The Plane of Fate convulsed.

Asante’s grief struck the Obsession Force with something hatred alone had never provided: an absolute refusal to accept the relationship Kujo had just created. Zuberi was dead, the Material Plane knew it, the Soul Plane registered the departing absence, and Fate had already begun changing every connection that depended upon Dawn Guard remaining alive. Asante’s entire being rejected that transition.

His eyes began to hurt. At first it felt like pressure behind them. Then pain tore through both sockets at once.

Asante screamed and lifted his head from Zuberi’s chest, hands flying toward his face as something inside his ocular lineage forced itself through stages he had never been prepared to reach. Blood ran from beneath his palms while his vision fractured into origins, causal beginnings, first recognitions, and the initial moments from which present structures had unfolded.

“AAAAAGHHH!”

The transformation continued.

His eyes no longer merely perceived what existed before him; they began reaching instinctively toward where things had begun. The falling monsters carried visible first points, every Void structure traced backward toward the principles that produced it, and even the Obsession Force surrounding Asante became readable at the moment where hatred first became attachment and attachment first became refusal. The world suddenly possessed beginnings everywhere he looked.

***The Origin Eye opened.***

Asante lowered his shaking hands.

Blood streaked his cheeks beneath the transformed gaze, but he barely noticed what he had awakened. There was no triumph, revelation, or excitement in the child. His first sight through the Origin Eye fell upon the beginning of the wound through Zuberi’s heart.

That destroyed him again.

“ZUBERI!”

The Obsession Force erupted.

Everything Asante had produced before became insignificant beside it.

Crimson-black power surged across the district with such density that the falling monsters nearest him simply ceased progressing toward the ground. Their bodies locked, twisted, and collapsed beneath a pressure born from a will no longer capable of separating love from grief, grief from hatred, or hatred from the single Name responsible. The force did not feel learned.

It felt inevitable. Asante screamed Kujo’s Name.

“KUJO!”

The Plane of Fate answered.

Obsession spread through it like an emotion acquiring metaphysical geography, connections trembling wherever the boy’s hatred recognized the Void Regent. Every thread of grief leading from Asante toward Vaeloris, Lyria, and now Zuberi twisted around that Name, creating a concentration so severe that even distant Fate relationships seemed to recoil from the child. His Obsession Force had become unmistakable.

Unrivaled. Terrible. Potentially absolute. But Asante did not attack again.

He looked back down at Zuberi.

All that impossible power remained around him while he crawled closer to the body and pulled Dawn Guard’s head against his chest. Asante wrapped his small arms around the man who had used his own body as a shield and held him as though warmth might somehow return if he refused to let go long enough. The Origin Eye remained open through tears he could not stop.

*“I’M SORRY…”*

His voice broke until the words became almost unintelligible.

*“I’M SORRY, ZUBERI…”*

The Obsession Force raged around them.

Monsters continued dying wherever they entered its reach, crushed beneath a will Asante was not consciously directing toward them anymore. Stone levitated, Fate trembled, crimson-black strands filled the ruined streets, and the newly awakened Origin Eye stared through beginnings that no child should have been forced to understand. Yet at the center of all that power was only Asante.

Crying.

**Holding Dawn Guard’s dead body.**

And begging someone who could no longer answer him to come back.

"I have to go."

He said it without turning round, because turning round would have cost him the four seconds he had, and because he had learned at twenty-six that a man who looks at somebody while refusing them is asking for help with the refusal.

"You cannot follow me into this. Not for any reason you are about to give me, and I have heard all of them and two of them are correct."

He went.

The Fourth Work is order and nothing else and it is the least impressive of the seven and it is the one he used now, and eleven hundred yards of ruined Vaeloris quarter passed under him in a way that had no running in it. His coat did not move. That was the difference nine months had made and he noticed it in passing and put it away: everything he had done before the ash had cost him drag, and drag is what happens to a body that has to negotiate with the air, and he had stopped negotiating.

The Hammer came with him and weighed what a smith's hammer weighs and was refusing, continuously, in his right hand.

He arrived over the boy and put himself between.

Asante Zettari was on the rubble with the body.

Darius took the whole of it in one pass because he had no choice about that any more. A man of fifty-six with the sixth Fist in him reads a scene the way a physician reads a chest, and the scene said: the child is nine or ten, the man in his arms is Zuberi Zettari, Dawn Guard, sovereign of Kushara, and he has been dead for somewhere between forty and ninety seconds. Entry at the sternum, no exit, and nothing about it that a surgeon would call a wound. There was no cavitation. A puncture at that velocity should have carried a temporary cavity out through the back and it had carried nothing, which meant nothing had passed through him in the ordinary sense and something had simply stopped being present along a line. The tissue around the track had gone the colour of absence and was going on doing it, outward through the pericardium and into the lung roots and the great vessels, at a rate that had not slowed since the heart stopped.

Void does not kill by trauma. It kills by removing the conditions under which a structure is permitted to continue, and the removal propagates along whatever the structure was joined to, which in a chest is everything.

Black in the mouth. Black at the corners of both eyes.

And the Shield of Kush, dropped, with a perfectly round hole through its centre.

*He dropped it.*

*He had it up and it failed and he dropped it and he turned round, and the turning is what killed him, and any officer in four worlds would tell you the turn was the wrong call. And I have read the Zettari succession for thirty years and I would have bet the seat that Zuberi Zettari would make exactly that call.*

The Obsession Force was standing over the whole district.

Crimson-black, in strands and chains, running out of a ten-year-old across four streets and killing things wherever it touched them without the child directing any of it. It came off him in a column and the column had a Name at the top of it and the Name was no longer being spoken, because the boy had stopped screaming and had gone to holding.

Aetheric Density in that district had no business being what it was. There is no Well under the Vaeloris quarter and the ward was cut and every standpipe on that street had been dry since the ninth hour, and the boy was producing it out of his own Crystal at a rate that would have put a Grade Six practitioner on the flags inside a minute. He had been at it for four.

*He does not know he is spending. That is the thing about Obsession and it is why the Accord will not license it. It sustains on refusal, and refusal is free at the point of use, and the bill arrives all at once and somewhere else.*

Darius stood in front of him and did the hardest thing he had done since the fourth month.

He held the Trait back.

*Every strand of this comes apart if I let go. All of it, in about a second and a half, across the entire district. It is refusal-sustained and I am what removes the conditions of refusal, and there is no aiming it and no partial application, and I have never once in twenty-one years had to hold it in.*

*And it is the only thing standing between that boy and the next thing to come down this street. And it is also the only thing he has left of what he feels about the man in his arms, because that is what Obsession is, and if I take it he will find out in about a second and a half that his grief has been quietly filed as a working and that I filed it.*

*So he keeps it. He keeps every strand of it. I will stand here with my hand shut and let a child hold the worst thing in his life in both arms, because it is his.*

It cost him more than the fall had. Purity is not a working and cannot be modulated and has no dial on it; it is a property of the Lattice and holding it in is holding a structural fact in abeyance, and the strain of that went into the same bones the Hammer was already standing on.

"Finally back."

The voice came up the street behind him and Darius knew it before he had finished hearing the first word, and there was one man in four worlds whose voice arrived that way and it had been arriving that way since Darius was nineteen.

He turned his head.

Aurelian Prudentius came up the rubble at a walk with his hands loose at his sides and no vestment on him worth the word and his feet leaving prints in the ash that filled with black water behind him. Young. That was the first thing and it went into Darius like a splinter, and the second thing was that the man cast a shadow and threw no Residue, and after six hours in a city on fire there was not one mark on him that an instrument would have read. The hands were young and there was no chalk and no ink on them, the thigh under the ruined black was whole, the jaw was somebody else's, and the eyes were the same silver they had always been.

"Darius." A small, warm, entirely successful smile. "I suppose we can fight this Void Regent together."

"You look well."

"I am told it is disconcerting."

"It is." Darius did not lower the Hammer. "Who paid for it."

Aurelian's smile did not falter at any point, and the ground under him went down the width of a coin and stayed down.

"Verinus."

"Where is he."

"Gone. The crossing shows no arrival, which means it took him entirely." He said it pleasantly, in a flat plain voice with no drop at the end of the sentence. "I looked for him among the traffic. He is not in it."

*He is wrong.*

*He is wrong about that and I know he is wrong about that because I have felt the wall standing since the ninth hour and I know what is holding it, and I am not going to tell him in this street.*

*And the wrongness is not the thing I have noticed.*

Darius looked at him properly for the first time.

"How long since you came up out of the ground."

"Six hours. A little less."

"And how much of what you are saying to me now would you have said six hours ago."

Aurelian considered that with visible interest.

"None of it," he said. "You have got there faster than the archbishop did and he has known me since he was fifteen."

"Charles is dead."

He put it down between them flat and without a run-up, because there was no arrangement of it that was kind and because he had been carrying it for eleven minutes with nobody to hand it to.

"I know," said Aurelian.

"How."

"He came apart on the Ashgate road at about the ninth hour, and I felt the office go, and it went in a way I had not been told to expect, and I have been thinking about it since." He put his hands behind his back. "You are the other half. I put the chain over his head in the lower chapel and you signed second and I have wondered for ten years which of you would go first."

"You watched him."

Aurelian said nothing.

"That is not a question," Darius said. "There is a man in that column with a wax tablet who was on the wall at the Seat this morning and he has already told three people. The halberdier came for Charles and you were four yards away with two fingers and you did not move, and you found it interesting."

"I did."

"Say the rest of it."

"I held twelve futures in the front of my head." The smile had gone somewhere, and what was underneath it was worse, because it was pleasant too. "In nine of eleven interventions the archbishop lives. I chose the one where I did nothing, and I chose it in the time it takes to notice a smell, and I did not experience the choosing as a decision. I have gone back over it four times since. It comes out the same way each time and it does not bother me, and the fact that it does not bother me is the only part I have been able to identify as a problem."

Darius stood in a ruined street with a hammer that could not be put down and a boy behind him holding a body.

"You are not the man who ordained me."

"No."

"The man who ordained me was frightened every day of his life and got up and did it anyway, and that was the entire content of him, and it was worth more than my Trait and my name and the whole of the apparatus I built for that Order." Darius did not raise his voice for this part. "I have read forty years of your correspondence. There are eleven letters in it that I keep in my own quarters and I have never told anybody which eleven. Not one of them could have been written by whatever is standing in this street."

"I have read them too." Aurelian's head came round, mildly. "Since this morning. I went and looked, because I could not remember why I had written the fourth one, and the file told me what I had done and could not tell me what it had been like. That is the condition. It is not that I have lost the record. The record is intact. It is that the record is now the whole of it."

"Then you know what you are."

"I know what I am no longer." He said it without any weight at all. "I have not decided whether the difference matters, and I notice that I do not appear to be in a hurry to find out, and I am telling you that plainly because you are the only man alive whose office obliges him to weigh me and I would rather you had the true reading than a flattering one."

"Then why are you here."

"Because I am still the Primate, and because I have seen every fate in four worlds at once and I have looked at what happens to this city and at what happens to me, and I am not afraid of any of it." Aurelian brought his hands round to the front again. "You have spent eleven years telling that Curia the office does not require the man to be good. I have not come to argue with you. I have come to be the instrument, which is what you told them the office was."

"I said that at thirty-five and I was showing off."

"You were correct."

"I have been correct about a great many things and it has never once helped anybody." Darius turned back toward the black. "Stay on my left. I want no part of you there and I want you there anyway, because whatever is left of you is a very great deal, and I would rather have it where I can see it, and I am too old to pretend that is not what I meant."

"That is the most honest thing anyone has said to me in six hours."

"It is the tenth hour of the worst day in the history of the North and you have made a joke."

"I have made four since I came up out of the ground. Nobody has laughed and I have kept count." A pause. "That is a thing I would not have done either."

"Verinus made one at his investiture." Aurelian looked out at the abyss standing over the palace. "Nobody minuted it. I have thought about that more than I have thought about most things."

Darius put the Hammer out at arm's length toward the black.

"I am going to enjoy this."

The filaments came up off the Lattice along both arms and stood out in the light and the light was still coming down through the gradient he had opened in the overcast four minutes ago.

"You thought you were rid of me. Did you not. You put me inside Fate and you shut it and you went about your business, and every account you have of me since is nine months old and every one of them is wrong."

His voice had gone up and he let it. He had not raised it in front of another living soul since the year he was twenty-six and had regarded that as a discipline, and he had spent nine months in an ash pit learning that some things are supposed to be said at volume by a man who means them.

"I have been waiting. Eagerly. In the dark, in a place with no hours in it, with an old man taking me apart one Work at a time and putting me back together wrong, and I have thought about this specific street and this specific minute every day of it."

The Hammer refused in his hand, continuously, as it had since it was made.

"I am going to kill you."

Beside him, Aurelian Prudentius put both hands on his sides.

Then he took them off and put them behind his back, which is the posture, and which he had been taught at nineteen in a room with four men in it and had used at eleven hundred offices of the dead.

He smiled at the black.

"It is nice to meet you again," said the Primate of the Holy Sea.

The ground under him went down another coin's width.

"You are the creature. The strong one. The reason behind the whole of it, and I have read the file and there is nobody else in it." He tilted his head very slightly. "I am going to enjoy giving you the word of the Judger."

Behind them a ten-year-old boy held a dead man's head against his chest and the Obsession Force went on standing over four streets, and Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum kept his hand shut and did not let go of the one thing he had that could have stopped it, and started walking.

Kujo stood amid the blackened ruin with Val Nihil churning across the heavens behind him, his attention resting first upon the body in Asante’s arms. Dawn Guard no longer moved, black blood staining his mouth and eyes while the boy clung to him beneath an Obsession Force violent enough to distort the Fate surrounding four entire streets. Kujo regarded the fallen sovereign for several seconds, then slowly lifted one hand toward the sky as if presenting the corpse to the worlds watching. His smile widened.

“DOWN WITH THE SOVEREIGN! The throne of Kushara has lost the man who made it frightening. Let every crown still pretending it can resist me look carefully at what remains of him.”

His laughter carried through the ruined district while monsters continued descending from Val Nihil far overhead. Zuberi Zettari had frustrated him repeatedly, endured devastation that should have broken lesser rulers, severed Kujo’s head once, and continued placing himself between catastrophe and anyone unfortunate enough to stand behind him. In the end, that same instinct had decided the method of his death. Kujo found the symmetry exquisite.

“He died exactly as he lived. Not because the attack was meant for him, but because there was somebody smaller standing behind him. What a remarkably consistent man.”

Then Kujo felt the refusal coming toward him.

His head turned before Darius finished crossing the ruined quarter, the expression on Kujo’s face changing from triumph into something far more interested. That signature had been absent for nine months, altered enough that the old measurements no longer described it properly, yet Kujo recognized the underlying architecture immediately. Beside it came something stranger still, Aurelian Prudentius wearing his own history with none of the emotional residue that history should have carried.

Kujo began laughing.

“DARIUS!”

The name exploded across the street with enough force to carry over the shrieks descending from Val Nihil. Kujo spread his arms openly, almost welcoming the man who had once sacrificed himself to imprison him within Fate, while the dense Aether surrounding his body pressed cracks deeper into the ruined ground. There was genuine delight in his expression.

“GLAD YOU’RE BACK! ARE YOU GOING TO SACRIFICE YOURSELF AGAIN FOR ANOTHER DEFEAT? Because I remember the last one very clearly, even if you were hoping nine months in darkness might make the ending seem more flattering.”

Kujo stepped forward once, his bare presence forcing loose debris away from his feet. The Fifth Layer no longer covered him, yet The Unwinding remained quietly nested within his personal architecture, and the Crevice beneath his sternum pulsed black-gold through the darkness moving around his frame. He studied Darius with considerably more care than his mockery suggested. Whatever had returned from those nine months was not the same structure Kujo had once deposited into Fate.

“You do look better, though. Less ceremonial, less concerned with behaving like the respectable little refusal your Order needed you to be. Someone finally took you apart thoroughly enough that you stopped wasting strength pretending restraint and virtue were the same thing.”

His gaze then moved toward Aurelian.

Kujo became quieter.

There was no fear around the Primate anymore, no familiar emotional drag connecting memory to meaning, and no ordinary residue explaining how forty years of lived history continued occupying the same man. Kujo had already noticed the transformation once, but seeing Aurelian beside Darius now made the alteration even more pronounced. Something had survived while much of the reason that survival should matter had been scraped away.

Kujo smiled again.

“Or perhaps that is not why you returned at all.”

He tilted his head toward Aurelian, eyes narrowing with malicious curiosity. The Primate stood there carrying the language of judgment and the shape of a man the old world still recognized, but Kujo looked beyond both things toward the larger theological current gathering around him. His smile sharpened when the possibility pleased him.

“HAVE YOU COME TO BRING ME URION’S PARAGON?”

The question thundered over the district.

Kujo laughed so violently that the darkness around him shuddered with it, the black-gold fracture beneath his sternum flashing through his frame. Somewhere above, enormous Val Nihil swirls continued releasing monsters into every horizon while the world beneath them struggled to accommodate an ecology that had never belonged there. Kujo pointed toward Aurelian as though Darius had brought him a gift.

“Is that what this is, Darius? You spent nine months rebuilding yourself so you could walk Urion’s precious answer directly into the center of my Devastation. How considerate.”

His hand lowered slowly.

“I have heard enough about Paragons. Children built by suffering, instruments sharpened by faith, answers assembled from everything people like Verinus insist devastation accidentally creates. If that is what you have brought me, then place him in front of me properly.”

Kujo’s grin became almost feral.

“I WOULD LOVE TO KILL HIM.”

The words shook through the air while Kujo’s Aether continued pressing against the district, but he did not attack yet. He wanted Darius close enough to understand how much had changed since their previous encounter, and he wanted Aurelian standing beside him long enough for whatever he had become to reveal itself without being forced prematurely. Kujo had spent too much time learning that survivors were more informative than corpses to waste two interesting ones before they had spoken through violence.

His gaze returned briefly to Dawn Guard.

Then to Asante.

Then finally back to Darius.

“Look behind you before you begin promising my death. Zuberi promised something similar every time he stood back up, and now his heart is black in a child’s arms. The boy has inherited his grief, the world has inherited Val Nihil, and you have arrived just in time to pretend this is still a battle.”

Kujo opened both hands.

“This is not the street you remember, Darius. This is not the Kujo you locked inside Fate, and this is certainly not a world waiting for one magnificent sacrifice to restore the shape it had yesterday. Phase Three has already begun.”

The heavens behind him churned.

More monsters fell.

Kujo smiled at the two men approaching him through the ruins.

“So come on, old friend. Bring your Hammer, bring your seven Works, bring your reconstructed courage, and bring Urion’s Paragon if that is truly what stands beside you. This time, when you sacrifice yourself, I want you awake long enough to understand why it changed nothing.”

"You will have to kill me first."

The filaments came up off him before the sentence finished, gold, out of the Lattice along both arms and both collarbones and the whole length of the spine, and this time they did not stand off him in floss. They set. They stood out rigid in every direction, and the light in them was the light he had opened in the overcast and it had been coming down on this street for six minutes without interruption.

Beside him, Aurelian Prudentius put both hands out palm up.

He began to weave.

It went into the air in front of him in a way Darius could read and could not have done, threads pulled out of his own Essence and laid across each other in a lattice that was not a shape and was a distribution, and the Primate's voice came out of him flat and level in the reading register, at the volume he had used at eleven hundred offices of the dead.

"Veni, voluntas Libratoris."

Come, will of the Balancer.

"Adsit statera."

Let the Scale be present.

The ground under Aurelian went down a coin's width and then went down again and kept going, an inch, two, and the flags around him tilted inward toward the place where he was standing.

"Addatur pondus sociis meis. Crescant."

Let weight be added to my allies. Let them grow.

And then the clause that made it a working instead of a wish, and Darius heard it and understood in the same instant what the Primate had just done and what it was going to cost him to keep doing it.

"Ego lanx altera."

I am the other pan.

"Quod illis accedit, mihi decedat."

What accrues to them, let it be taken from me.

"Descendam ut ascendant."

Let me descend so that they may rise.

A balance does not make weight. That is the entire principle and the Church has been putting it on walls for four hundred years without one bishop in fifty following it to the end. A Scale moves what is already there. Every ounce that arrives on one pan is an ounce that has left the other, and there is no version in which both go up.

Darius felt it arrive.

It came in at the Attraction Layer, which is the only thing that transmits between two people who are not touching, and for one moment it did not take, because nothing external couples to him and never has. Then it found the single channel a bond gets and went in through that, and his Ardency came up by a figure he would not be able to name afterward, and the fatigue went out of his legs, and the strain of holding the Trait in went with it.

Thirty feet away, Aurelian Prudentius sank another inch into the flags and smiled at the black and said nothing about any of it.

The Scale was under the whole street by then. Darius could see where it had been laid, because a working of that class leaves the ground itself sorted: every loose thing in the district had begun drifting toward one of two places, and the two places were the two pans, and the light pan was under his own feet.

*He is spending himself into me.*

*He has nothing left to feel about it, which is the entire problem with him, and it means he will not stop, because the thing that stops a man in the middle of that is the moment it begins to hurt, and he has been carefully relieved of the mechanism.*

*I have been in this street four minutes and I have already been handed a second person to keep alive.*

Darius had had enough of the talking.

He recognised that in himself and let it stand. Twenty-one years of correspondence, eleven years of a Curia, four hundred letters in a dry hand at the foot of the Order's register, a whole life spent putting the argument in writing so that nobody would have to be hit, and he had come out of nine months in an ash pit with the entire apparatus intact and no further use for any of it.

Behind them both, at the far end of the street, Val Nihil went on emptying itself into the Material and the things coming out of it were landing in the rubble at a rate of eleven or twelve a second and getting up.

Darius did not look at any of them. That was Aurelian's half of the arrangement and neither of them had discussed it, and thirty feet back the Primate had already put a hand out sideways without turning his head, and whatever came down that street on the left flank was going to meet a man who had seen every fate in four worlds at once and had found the experience interesting.

He went forward.

Slowly, and the slowness was the point. The ground between him and the black was under a gradient that would have put any practitioner in four worlds on their face at forty feet, and Darius walked into it at a pace and it did nothing to him whatsoever.

That is the Trait, and it is the only part of it he has ever enjoyed. Pressure works by coupling. It arrives at a Crystal, finds purchase in the Shell, and bears down through the structure, and that is why a Band gap changes local weather and why men go to their knees without being touched.

Nothing external couples to him.

So he walked through a field that was cracking flagstones on both sides of him, at fifty-six years of age, in a coat, with his chin down and his shoulders down, and the only thing that happened to him on the way in was that his hair moved.

At eleven feet the Hammer became useless.

He had known that before he arrived, because measure is the first item on any technical floor and he has been reading measure since he was fifteen. Inside eleven feet there is no arc. A hammer needs a radius and a radius needs a room, and the correct answer to being inside somebody's measure holding a hammer is to stop trying to use the hammer.

So he used his head.

He came past the guard with his hips going first, dropped his weight, and drove up off the back foot with the whole of his mass behind it, and put the front of his own skull into the middle of what was standing there.

A man of his mass moving at the speed the Fourth Work allows arrives with a kinetic quantity no muscle in a body could produce, and all of it goes through four square inches of the hardest bone in a body, and the transfer takes under two milliseconds because there is no give anywhere in the chain.

The frontal bone is the thickest plate in a man. It is the one part of the head built to be hit with, and it has been used that way in every fighting tradition on the Material for as long as there have been fighting traditions, and there is nothing brutish in it. It is a short weapon delivered at a distance where nothing longer will fit.

His eyes went gold on the way in.

The shock came back down through his own neck into his hips and out through the flags and cracked four of them.
