He felt his own cervical column take it in three places and hold, and one of the discs went, and he registered that the way he registered everything now, as an entry, and kept going. There is a reason nobody past forty does that, and Darius had used it eleven times in his career and had been on the ground afterward for two of them.

At that range he could hear the Crevice.

No sound in it anywhere. A rate. Something under the black keeping a slow interval, slower than the pulse of anything that has a body, and it came up through the flags into the soles of his boots and into the bones of both feet and did not couple to a single thing in him and was audible to him anyway.

He got the shaft back.

The Hammer came up and over his right shoulder to the length of his arm, and by the time it reached the top of the arc he was already opening the first two Fists into the same motion, because there is no separation between them and never has been, and that is the whole content of the matrix Gimbzo spent nine months putting into him.

**Ruin first.**

Ruin does not break things. Ruin finds the ending that is already on its way and makes it arrive correctly, and there is no structure in four worlds without an ending in it, and the darkness standing around him had a great many. Every fold. Every joint where one part of the arrangement had been made to agree with another. Darius laid the first Fist across the whole standing volume and pushed on none of it, and stopped the timetable being polite.

The dark began to come apart along its own faults, and it did it the way anything under load does, which is to say at the joins and never in the middle of a good piece.

**Then Purity, out of the middle of Ruin, before the first was finished.**

Because a thing coming apart can reassemble, and that is what refusal is for. An arrangement sustained by Obsession continues on refusal alone and has no requirement to be intact. It goes on refusing, and it will drag its own pieces back together out of nothing but insistence, and that is why the Order's doctrine on Void constructs was wrong for three hundred years and why Darius rewrote it between twenty-six and thirty-five.

So he let the Trait out.

Some fraction of it, held short of the whole. He kept the boy's four streets and everything standing in them outside the volume by the width of his own judgement, which is the same instrument he uses for the Hammer and the same one he has never been able to write down.

Purity festered out of the ending that Ruin had found, into every fault opening across that darkness, and removed the conditions under which any of it was permitted to refuse.

The black shuddered and did not close.

Four minutes ago that would have taken the Trait forty seconds to accomplish across a volume that size. It took eleven, because a Scale had just been put under the whole street and one pan of it was standing thirty feet behind him going into the flags an inch at a time.

The Hammer came up.

There is no downstroke available at eleven inches and there was no time to make room, so he took it up, in a rising arc off the back foot with his whole body opening behind it, and he took it up at the chin.

That is the strike, and every fighting man alive knows why it is the strike. The jaw is a lever. Load applied at the point of it does not travel down through the frame, it turns the head, and rotational acceleration is the one thing a skull cannot armour against, because what is inside it is not attached to it at the speed the argument is being made.

There was blood in his left ear from the headbutt and it had already stopped, and his vision had gone narrow at the sides and come back, and the disc in his neck had begun to report at a level he would be unable to ignore in about forty minutes.

He had one arc. That was the reading and he made it on the way up: an uppercut of that length gives a single window between the point where the head clears the shoulder and the point where the wrist runs out of travel, and the window is about a hand's width of arc, and there is nothing after it except a man with a hammer over his own head and both ribs open.

And at the moment of contact, Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum opened his right hand a fraction.

For an interval he would afterward put at less than a thousandth of a second, a measured portion of the most obstinate object in four worlds was permitted to stop being obstinate, and did the thing it had been trying to do since before there was a Kharven.

The concussion went out from the head of the Hammer in a sphere, and the sphere was a sphere and not a cone, because nothing in the release is aimed and nothing about it can be.

It took the Vaeloris quarter down to the footings for a hundred and forty feet in every direction. The street went. The frontages went. The gable of the chapter hold on the corner came off in one piece and was gone before it had finished leaving. The front reached the outer wall and reflected and came back and met itself over the top of him, and Darius stood in the middle of that with his own skeleton taking all of it, because the containment gives him nothing and asks him for everything.

Behind him the boy on the rubble had not looked up, and the Obsession Force was still standing over four streets, and Darius had kept every strand of it out of the volume by the width of a judgement he could not have explained to anybody at the Order.

He drove the arc upward with everything he had.

"RAAAGH."

It tore out of him and he did not shape it and did not want to. He had not raised his voice in front of another living soul since the year he was twenty-six. He had regarded that as a discipline, and had written a paragraph about it in the Order's conduct guidance that was still in force, and every word of that paragraph had been composed by a man who had never been in an ash pit.

"FIGHT ME."

The gold went up both arms past the wrists and into the haft.

"YOU WANT THE PARAGON. YOU WILL HAVE TO DO A GREAT DEAL MORE THAN IMPRISON ME."

*And you will not get his name out of either of us.*

*The Primate will not give it. Whatever is left in that man is still holding that one, and I watched him decline to answer you and say nothing at all in a street where saying nothing is the only thing left that costs him anything.*

*And I cannot give it. I have never seen the child. That was the whole of the design and I hated it for eleven years and I understand it now.*

"KUJO."

The Hammer went up through the top of the arc with a hundred and forty feet of city coming apart underneath it.

"YOU WILL HAVE TO KILL ME."

She sighed, seeing as how the two of them were willing to throw themselves away to simply just kill Kujo. Her eyes rolled almost annoyed by their confidence, but part of it amused her despite this. Her figure shifted as the light particles shifted, within moments the thread was traced finely across the line as she appeared in front of the both of them. Gunning straight for Kujo, her figure illuminating as the tension between them broke almost immediately. 

Within moments the light surged into an explosion, the distance between them were built as the battlefield shifted to something much more spacious, Haruki landing right in front of Darius and Aurelian, her blade shifting in hand as she assumed stance almost immediately.

"I hope they remember your name, the next time it is spoken at your funeral." She said, looking at Kujo her light shining through the both of them as the Harmonic Arts rejuvenated once more, the pulse changing the outline of the battle as the terrain began to shift. Her blade remaining aimed at Kujo, the noise suddenly disrupted as her Kokudan locked onto him.

"Stand against us, bring forth your greatest of soldiers. It does not change the results, you stand before three judges, and the Prince of Kushara. This is no longer a trial, no, that would insist that a verdict has not been decided, but we have already spoken." She said, the palace walls crumbled revealing the sun shining across the horizon, the harmonic field adjusting to allow it's brightness to shine down onto them.

"It marks this point in history Kujo, that you die... TODAY!" She adjusted her blade as the pillar of light risen upward, the Saishiki resonating brightly between Darius and Aurelian. The wind blew heavily between the palace and them, the rubble ascending upward before shattering into dust underneath the pressure. 

*'It's no longer that I hold back my restraints, it is time I allowed them to see... the depth that I had been concealing from them.'* The heat pressing-against them as her eyes narrowed onto him, the tension reaching it's height at the moment of her arrival.

The Hammer reached Kujo’s chin.

It struck exactly where Darius intended, with the Fourth Work driving the entire mass of the man behind a distance too short for ordinary strength to matter. The impact propagated downward through the street, fractured the flags beneath Kujo, and threw a concussive report across the ruined quarter hard enough to shake what remained of the surrounding structures. Kujo’s head moved perhaps half an inch.

Then it stopped.

He looked down at Darius.

There was no blood at Kujo’s mouth, no fracture beneath the skin, no loosened tooth, and no disturbance in the pale geometry of his face. The headbutt had transferred its force; Kujo had simply possessed enough structural density to receive it without producing the failure Darius had calculated. His expression was not surprised.

“Good.”

Ruin spread through the darkness around him next.

Kujo felt the Work searching for endings already present within structure, finding seams, transitions, folds, agreements, and every point where one arrangement had consented to become another. The black surrounding his body began separating exactly as Darius intended, fault after fault opening beneath the accelerated timetable of its own collapse. Kujo watched it happen without attempting to stop him.

Then Purity entered the fractures.

The surrounding Void lost the conditions by which several of its temporary arrangements had been sustaining themselves, and portions of the darkness refused to close because refusal itself had been stripped from those formations. Black matter peeled away from Kujo’s shoulders and scattered through the air in dead fragments while the working continued consuming its designated volume. Kujo remained standing inside the dismantling field.

“You are still making the same mistake.”

His voice remained conversational.

“You keep attacking what I am wearing around myself.”

The Crevice beneath Kujo’s sternum pulsed once.

Ruin did not discover some hidden terminal weakness inside it because collapse was not merely something the Crevice suffered; collapse was part of the lawful function it embodied. Darius could accelerate endings through structures around Kujo, but the black-gold fracture beneath his chest did not recoil from that principle as though exposed to an alien authority. It answered with a slow internal beat.

“And you are trying to frighten collapse with Ruin.”

Kujo smiled.

“That is adorable.”

The Hammer rose.

Darius opened the weapon for less than a thousandth of a second, and the result devoured the street around them. Stone disappeared into pulverized violence, buildings came apart down to their foundations, the front reflected from the outer wall, and the returning pressure met itself over the battlefield in a sphere of destruction broad enough to erase a hundred and forty feet of city. Kujo stood at the center of it.

The Hammer struck his chin.

The entire ruined quarter detonated around him.

Kujo did not move.

Dust erased him from sight for several seconds while masonry screamed through the air and the ground collapsed beneath the force of Darius’s release. When the cloud finally opened, Kujo was still standing in precisely the same place, one foot upon a surviving fragment of stone that should not reasonably have remained beneath him. His face had not changed.

No blood.

No fracture.

No damage.

Kujo slowly brought one hand to his jaw.

He moved it experimentally from one side to the other.

Then he laughed.

“DARIUS!”

The laugh became louder.

“YOU SPENT NINE MONTHS BEING REMADE FOR THAT?”

His voice rolled over the destroyed quarter.

The pressure of his Aether surged outward, not as an attack but as the natural response of something enormous becoming entertained. Loose fragments suspended by the competing fields began trembling around him while his long black hair lifted gently away from his shoulders. Kujo looked directly into Darius’s golden eyes.

“You were correct about one thing.”

He lowered his hand.

“You will have to be killed first.”

Aurelian’s Scale had already settled beneath the street.

Kujo could feel the Attraction structure dividing the battlefield into its pans, redistributing what existed rather than manufacturing what did not. Power flowed toward Darius through the lawful bond that had permitted entry, while Aurelian descended incrementally under the price of every increase he granted his ally. Kujo’s gaze shifted toward the Primate.

“You are spending yourself into him.”

His smile disappeared for a moment.

“And you do not care enough about yourself to stop.”

Kujo studied Aurelian with genuine fascination.

“That is not courage.”

His head tilted slightly.

“That is an instrument with the part that fears breaking removed.”

Then Haruki arrived.

Light traced its path through the battlefield before her body fully established itself, Saishiki filling the region while the Harmonic Arts changed the relationship between the surviving terrain, the three combatants, and the brightness breaking through the ruined palace. The field expanded, rubble lifted, pressure rearranged itself, and her blade settled upon Kujo through Kokudan’s lock. Kujo finally turned his attention toward her.

He listened. He allowed every word. Three judges. The Prince of Kushara. A verdict already decided.

Death today.

Kujo looked from Haruki to Darius, from Darius to Aurelian, and then briefly toward the place where Asante remained with Dawn Guard’s corpse beneath the violent canopy of Obsession. There was something almost peaceful in the way he considered all of them together. Then his mouth curved upward.

“Three judges.”

He repeated it quietly.

“And a grieving child.”

The smile grew.

“What an extraordinary tribunal.”

The pillar of light rose.

Kujo did not shield his eyes.

Saishiki washed across his body, Harmonic pressure climbed around him, and the field attempted to establish the significance of what stood opposite it. Light traveled across his skin and the darkness around his frame, illuminating every line of him against the devastated city. Nothing burned. Nothing cracked. Nothing was purified away.

Kujo looked down at his own hand while the light passed over it.

“You have all become so enamored with the language of judgment.”

His fingers opened slowly.

“Verdicts.”

One finger folded.

“Scales.”

Another.

“Purity.”

Another.

“History.”

Another.

“Fate.”

His fist closed.

“And every one of you continues arriving at the same childish conclusion.”

Kujo looked at Haruki.

“That because you have named what I deserve…” His gaze moved to Aurelian. “…because you have measured it…” Then Darius. “…because you have found a mechanism that should answer it…” Finally Asante. “…because you hate me enough…” Kujo’s eyes narrowed. “…the world is obligated to produce my death.”

Silence settled briefly around him. Then Kujo laughed again.

“No.”

The word was small. It carried farther than the shouting had.

“You have confused moral certainty with mechanical authority.”

Kujo stepped forward.

The ruined stone beneath his foot compacted rather than cracked, forced downward by the density surrounding his body. Haruki’s light remained across him, Aurelian’s Scale remained active, Darius’s Works remained present in the battlefield, and Asante’s Obsession still towered over the streets. Kujo continued walking through all of it.

“Darius can refuse.”

Another step.

“Aurelian can weigh.”

Another.

“Haruki can judge.”

Another.

“Asante can hate.”

Kujo stopped.

“And none of those verbs mean kill.”

His eyes settled upon Darius.

“You told me to fight you.”

Kujo’s smile vanished.

“So stop talking.”

The black-gold fracture beneath his sternum opened wider. Not physically. Structurally.

The Crevice became visible through Kujo’s pale chest like a vertical wound cut into the agreement that his body was one continuous thing, black at its center and rimmed in molten gold. The slow interval Darius had heard through the ground deepened.

Then Kujo changed.

His shoulders broadened first.

The shift came slowly enough for every person in front of him to watch it happen.

Pale skin along Kujo’s collarbones darkened into charcoal-gray tissue as something armored began pushing upward from beneath it. Segmented plates emerged without tearing him open, replacing human anatomy rather than merely covering it, each interlocking piece ribbed with black mineral ridges and narrow channels of crimson light. His human frame grew taller.

Ten feet.

Then more.

His spine lengthened with a succession of heavy internal clicks.

Black tendrils emerged from his back one at a time.

The first was thin.

The second thicker.

Then dozens followed.

They unfolded from along the transformed spine like segmented appendages grown from some ancient execution organism, slick black armor wrapped around pulsing crimson interiors. Some ended in hooked points, others in articulated grasping structures, and several simply continued lengthening until they dragged over the ruined ground behind him.

Kujo inhaled.

His ribcage changed.

The human chest widened into a layered armored structure where black-gray sinew moved between plates resembling the ribs of something too large to have evolved naturally. Crimson channels ran through the gaps, carrying energy rather than blood, while the Crevice remained visible at the center of everything like the wound from which the new anatomy had been permitted to exist. His abdomen narrowed beneath it into corded bands of black muscle.

One arm became enormous. The hand disappeared first. Then the wrist.

Bone, armor, and Voidic material extended forward until the entire limb transformed into a colossal bladed execution appendage, its edge irregular only in appearance and impossibly precise along the actual cutting surface. The weapon was not held by Kujo.

It was Kujo.

His other arm remained articulate.

Long black claws extended from the fingertips.

Armored segments grew along the forearm and shoulder while crimson tissue remained visible beneath the joints, allowing the limb a disturbingly human range of movement despite what it had become. Kujo flexed the claw once.

His neck lengthened.

The human face persisted longer than everything else.

That made the transformation worse.

Kujo continued smiling with the same beautiful pale features while the body beneath his jaw became increasingly monstrous, as though courtesy itself were refusing to leave until the final possible moment. Then black ridges appeared beneath his cheekbones.

His jaw changed.

His eyes became harsher.

A crown of horned, bladed structures pushed backward from the skull.

The final remnants of human softness vanished as Kujo’s face reorganized into the severe predatory geometry of the Void Regent beneath the mask. Dark armor framed the skull while crimson channels traced the temples and throat. His long black hair spilled between the new crown structures like shadow caught between blades.

Then the crystal emerged.

Jagged red-white growths pushed from the upper portion of his transformed body, mineral structures somewhere between crystal, bone, and condensed catastrophe. They caught Haruki’s light and broke it into violent fragments across the battlefield. Red motes began drifting from them like burning blood suspended in air.

Kujo’s wings did not appear. He did not need them.

The tendrils behind him spread instead.

They rose across the ruined street until the monstrous silhouette obscured whole sections of the blackened sky, each appendage moving independently while the enormous execution limb lowered toward the ground. Kujo’s transformed height and width made the humans before him suddenly look painfully small.

The pressure changed. His Aether had already been immense. Now the body containing it stopped pretending to be human.

The Material Plane groaned beneath the amount of presence suddenly being permitted into one location, and the remaining fragments of the palace sank deeper into their foundations. Kujo’s true form stood where the beautiful man had been.

No courtesy. No disguise. No attempt at civilization.

Only Kujo.

The Void Regent rolled his transformed neck. The horned crown moved with it.

He looked first at Darius.

“You wanted the creature.”

Then Aurelian.

“You wanted something worthy of judgment.”

Then Haruki.

“You wanted history to remember my funeral.”

Finally his eyes traveled past them toward Asante.

“And you wanted something to hate.”

The enormous bladed limb rose.

Kujo spread the claw of his remaining hand.

Red motes circled his frame.

“Then look carefully.”

His voice had changed.

It carried several depths simultaneously, the familiar intelligence of Kujo still unmistakable beneath something vastly older and more physically terrible. Every word seemed to vibrate through the ruined structures surrounding them.

“This is what you came to kill.”

He lowered his body slightly. The tendrils spread. The execution limb turned edge-forward.

Kujo smiled.

“COME.”

The word struck the battlefield.

“DARIUS.”

One tendril stabbed into the ground.

“AURELIAN.”

Another unfolded overhead.

“HARUKI.”

The crimson channels throughout his body brightened.

“ASANTE.”

The Crevice pulsed.

Kujo opened both arms as far as his transformed anatomy allowed.

“YOU HAVE YOUR VERDICT.”

His monstrous grin widened.

“YOU HAVE YOUR JUDGES.”

The air shook.

“YOU HAVE YOUR LIGHT.”

The execution blade scraped once across the ground.

“YOU HAVE YOUR HATRED.”

Kujo leaned forward.

“AND NOW YOU HAVE ME.”

The Void Regent laughed.

“COME KILL ME.”

His claws curled inward.

“IF YOU CAN.”

The Attraction Force came down out of the Plane of Fate.

It arrived as blooms. Not a beam and not a fall: it opened in the air over the ruined quarter in a scattered sequence of expanding flowers of light, dozens and then hundreds, each one blossoming at its own rate and separating as it opened into its component wavelengths, so that the whole vault over the street stood full of shells of colour nested inside each other with the red furthest out and the violet at the core.

They were not lighting anything. That was the tell and Darius had it in under a second: an object under a light of that intensity throws a shadow, and nothing in that street was throwing one, because the blooms were not photons and had never been. They were Attraction Force presenting at a wavelength the eye had decided to accept, and the separation into colours was the Plane resolving into its own components on the way through the boundary between strata.

The common tongue calls that the smell of it. Trackers use the word for Residue and it is the same phenomenon at a different order of magnitude, and there was enough of it standing over the Vaeloris quarter to be readable from the coast.

Then they turned.

Every bloom in four hundred feet twisted inward at the same moment and ran down the same line, and the line ended in the head of the Hammer in Darius's right hand, and the light went into it and did not come out anywhere.

*That is the pan.*

*He is not making light. He is not making anything. He has laid a Scale under a street and every good thing left in this quarter is being redistributed toward the lighter side of it, and the lighter side is me.*

His own body began to tessellate.

He watched it happen on the back of his own left hand and had no framework for it whatsoever. The Lattice inside him was expressing outward through the flesh, and it was doing it as a tiling, plane-filling, a regular partition of the whole surface of him into cells that met edge to edge with no gap and no overlap anywhere. Gold at the boundaries. The skin between them still skin. It ran up the forearm and across the chest and over the shoulders and down into the coat, and where the pattern reached the coat the coat tessellated too.

*A lattice under uniform load does that. It has to. Load a crystalline structure evenly and it will express its own periodicity, because periodicity is the cheapest way for a structure under stress to be, and the pattern you see on the outside is the arrangement that was always inside.*

*I have never been loaded uniformly before. Nothing has ever been able to load me.*

*And the Aetheric Density in this street is past the point where the entry stops giving numbers and starts giving warnings. Saturation floods a region faster than it can disperse and it produces Fracture Events in everyone standing in it, including whoever caused it, and there is a child ninety feet behind me.*

Haruki came past him on the left.

He got an arm out. He had it up and across before she was level with him and it was already too late by a considerable margin, and what closed on the space where she had been was a fistful of nothing and a great deal of light.

"Do not."

It came out of him with more in it than he had intended, and it did no good, and he did not have the tempo to spend on doing it twice.

*Nobody in this street is going to be told anything by me today. I have been in it eleven minutes and I have given three instructions and every one of them has been correct and every one of them has been ignored, and I am beginning to understand what my whole life in that Order was actually made of.*


Aurelian Prudentius had not stopped speaking.

"Sit verax. Sit parcus."

Let it be truthful. Let it be frugal.

He was six inches into the flags by then. The stone had closed around both boots and was still taking him, and he had not looked down at it once, and the Scale under the street had sorted every loose thing in the quarter into two drifts and the drift on his own side was rising.

"Spolietur omni praesidio."

Let him be stripped of every defence.

*And that is the Direction role,* Darius thought, because the four roles are the first thing anybody at the Order learns and the last thing most of them think about. *Authorization was Urion, and he took it in the first clause. Boundary is this street and he set it when he laid the pans. Direction is now, and it is aimed, and it is aimed correctly.*

"Veni, lux Urionis. Librator, restitue stateram."

Come, light of Urion. Balancer, restore the balance.

And then the Primate of the Holy Sea said the fourth thing, and Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum, who had spent twenty-one years writing the Order's Glyphica standards, understood what he was listening to about half a second before the working proved it.

"Nihil sigilletur."

Let nothing be sealed.

*No.*

*No, you cannot leave it open. Sealing is not decoration. Authorization failing, Boundary failing, Direction failing all produce a working that does not happen, and every one of those is survivable and I have written the recovery procedure for all three.*

*Sealing failing produces a working that does not stop.*

*The law goes on obeying itself after the operator has left the room. It is in the standards under my own name, at paragraph nine, and I put it there.*

He knew why the man had done it. That was the worst part and it arrived immediately and without any effort at all. A Scale that seals stops balancing. Fixatio closes a working and holds it in the state it was closed in, and a closed Scale is a Scale that has stopped weighing, and Aurelian Prudentius had opened the Direction toward his allies and then declined to close the sentence, because closing it would have set a limit on how much he was permitted to give.

There is no limit now. There is only the far pan and how far it can go down.

And there is one further consequence and it took Darius another second to reach it, and when he reached it he stopped being angry with the man and started being frightened for him. An unsealed working leaves an open wound in the local Aether field, and the Continuum does not leave those. It closes them on its own terms, at its own hour, by whatever route it finds cheapest, and nobody has ever been able to predict which route that will be, and the route is very often the practitioner.

The darkness answered.

It came off the transformed shape at the end of the street in a single expanding front and it was not aimed at the Hammer or at the Works or at the light coming down out of the Plane. It arrived at Darius as weight, and weight does not care what a man is made of.

He went back.

Sixty feet, through the ruin of two frontages that had already been taken to the footings once, and the tessellation across his chest broke at the impact and reassembled while he was still travelling. He came off the far wall and got a boot down and skidded eleven yards through rubble on the heel and the side of his knee, and the disc in his neck reported at a level he was no longer able to file.

He twisted.

That was all of it and it was not a technique. A body coming out of a slide with a mass in its right hand and a bad neck has one honest option, which is to let the mass keep going and put the whole spine into the rotation and get the head of the thing into the ground before the ground stops being available.

He slammed the Hammer down.

And he opened his hand a fraction on the way in.

The street did not shatter.

The light came up out of it.

Every ounce of Attraction Force that had gone down into the Hammer since the blooms opened, everything Aurelian's Scale had moved into his pan, all of it went out of the head at the moment of contact and into the substrate, and the substrate could not hold it.

Fragments of light came up out of the flags in sheets.

They shovelled up along the fault lines the first release had already made, tearing the paving upward in slabs and then through the slabs, and where they came through, the stone burned. Actually burned. Material combustion, in a street where nothing combustible had been standing for six minutes, because the arriving flux crossed the ignition threshold of every mineral in the road and there is no substance that does not burn when the flux is high enough.

The quarter went white from the ground up, and the burning came with a smell Darius had met exactly twice, both times in a laboratory, both times behind glass: the smell of stone taken past the point where it can go on being stone, which has no honest name in the common tongue and which the alchemists call the fourth stage and will not describe further to anyone outside the Work.

And then the light began to travel.

It came out of the burning ground in lines. Coherent, collimated, four dozen of them at first and then a hundred, laid flat across the ruined street at every height, and they did not fan and did not scatter and did not obey the ordinary rules by which light leaves a fire.

They went to Aurelian.

They arrived at him, along paths that had already been decided before the light left the ground, converging on one point in a street four hundred feet wide with the accuracy of water finding a drain.

*The pan.*

*Of course they go to him. It is a balance and I have just put a very great deal on my own side of it and a balance does not accept that. The Scale is doing exactly what a Scale does, which is to bring the two sides back level, and the only route it has is through the man holding it.*

Every one of those lines arrived at the Primate of the Holy Sea, in the chest and the arms and the face and the open hands, and Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum watched a hundred collimated lines of burning ground converge on a man who was already six inches into the flags, and could not reach him, and was sixty feet away with a bad neck.

Aurelian did not shield.

He turned his palms up into it.

And Darius understood, watching, that the man was not enduring it. He was *taking* it. The lines went into him and did not go through him and did not come out the other side, and the Primate's whole frame lit from the inside, and he stood in the middle of the convergence with his head up and his hands open and did the one thing that had been the entire content of him for forty years before this morning, which was to decide.

Will. That is the whole of what a Turgia working runs on once the syntax is laid. The glyphs establish the boundary condition and the law does the work and the practitioner supplies the one thing no glyph can carry, which is the sustained intention that the sentence go on meaning what it meant when it was spoken.

Aurelian Prudentius had more of that than any man alive and had been relieved of every reason to spend it carefully.

That was the thing Darius had understood in the street six minutes ago and had put down because there was no time for it. The Order had spent four hundred years selecting for men whose will could hold a sentence open under load, and had built the entire structure of its offices around the assumption that a man like that would eventually stop, because eventually it would hurt him. Every safeguard the Order owns is downstream of that assumption. Every one of them.

There is no article, in eleven hundred years of Statute, written to govern a man who will not stop.

He wove.

The light came into him at a hundred points and left him in three, and Darius could see the three and could see where each of them ended, and the sight of the third one put a cold through him that the ice at the narrows had not managed.

The first went to Darius. The tessellation across his body took it and the load went up and the pattern tightened and the cells got smaller.

The second went across the street to where Haruki had gone, and Darius did not follow it and had no business following it.

The third went behind them both, low across the rubble, into four streets of standing crimson-black, and into a ten-year-old boy holding a dead man's head against his chest.

*Synergia requires Attraction Layer compatibility.*

*That is the first line of the entry. You cannot combine with a practitioner whose Attraction Layer will not recognise yours, and there is no version in which that child's Layer recognises anything at all this morning, because there is nothing left on it that has not curdled.*

*He is not feeding an ally.*

*He is feeding Obsession Force, and Obsession Force is Attraction Force with the recognition gone out of it, and it is close enough to the real thing to accept a transfer and it will not use it the way the real thing would.*

*And he has left the Sealing open, so it will not stop.*

He had time for one further reading and he took it and wished immediately that he had not. The lines going into Aurelian and the three going out of him were not the same quantity. The difference was going somewhere, and it was not going into the ground, and it was not going into the air, and the only remaining place for it was down whatever the man was anchored to.

*He is anchored to something. He does not know it and I can see it and there is no version of this street in which I get to tell him.*

Darius got his boot under him in the burning street and came up out of the rubble with the Hammer in both hands and the whole quarter alight behind him, and started back up the road toward the two of them.


Haruki shifted herself as the Kokudan locked onto Kujo, the bonds between him, the darkness, the tendrils, all of such had been bounded to him. The line was aligned up for them as the point was directed, her allies saw clearly through the light of what exactly was being set up. Her blade lifted, striking the point of where the thread had marked exactly where the bond was most weakest. Kujo's darkness folded inward, his tendrils scattered and expulsed, The light searing and burning where it was directed.

*'Align, connect the thread, sever, that is the motion you were taught. But lets see how he deals with it when the thread doesn't guide simply through where it is directed.'* She smirked as she shifted her position her stance shifting as the harmony aligned as her Kokudan began to perceive through it, her blade tracing against the point as Kujo's Xevorith began to see it before the action had fully carried through. The blur between the distance that the event played through, the action determined before the cause had been played. 

"Temporal Harvest." Within moments the currents shifted dramatically, her movements seemed to move in heavily increased projections, her figure moving almost impossibly fast for her current form of power. The blade shifting as the slash cleaved through the sky between Kujo and Haruki, the point of her edge drawing out the energy as the darkness expulsed, crushed, distilled underneath the weight of her pressure. The darkness, the corruption, it had been crushed before it could fully realize, the polarity shifting between void to the purity and white presence of absolution.

Her harmony shifted the ground as the pure energy shifted towards her, empowering her, absorbing the portions she were able to destroy. The presence around her began to project itself as space and time corrected itself completely, the shockwave was the first thing that hit them. The rubble shooting upward as Kujo's body was sent backwards from the shock. The clouds-parting out of results from the previous attack. Her movements shifted as the projected figure of herself would've dove into the ground, the field strengthening as Haruki lifted her hands up to collide them together.

The iron and metal of the surroundings closed in around Kujo, forming a magnetic field that chose to limit movement, rather than crush or confine. The layout was determined and selected to reduce battle performance, and it did so effortlessly. The bolts of electricity and lightning conducting off each rod and metal surface.

Kujo watched the Attraction blooms descend without moving from the center of the ruined quarter. His true form stood beneath their impossible colors, black-gray armor and crimson channels illuminated by light that cast no shadow, while the Crevice beneath his sternum maintained its slow black-gold rhythm. He recognized the Scale immediately, recognized the redistribution, and more importantly recognized the decision not to seal it. His monstrous mouth curved upward.

“You have left the sentence open, Aurelian. That means you believe infinity favors the generous. It does not.”

Kujo raised one claw and drew a circle no wider than his palm. Nothing appeared inside it, because appearance would have meant there was something there to perceive; instead, Kujo removed the local condition of receipt, creating a destination that could be reached but could never become fuller. It was not negative energy, a barrier, or another pan of the Scale. It was simply a place where possession had no valid state.

The redistribution law found it.

Kujo did not seize Aurelian’s Attraction Force and did not overpower the Scale. He merely permitted the open working to recognize an imbalance that could never be corrected, because whatever entered that tiny absence ceased to qualify as something that destination possessed. The Scale could continue transferring forever and the discrepancy would remain unchanged. Kujo had given an unsealed law an inexhaustible problem.

“You wanted the Scale to keep weighing. Then weigh nothing.”

The black point multiplied.

One became seven, seven became forty-nine, and each existed at a different depth of the battlefield without carrying enough presence to qualify as a conventional construct. They did not consume the entire working; Kujo was far more interested in making it inefficient, forcing portions of every redistribution to choose between intended recipients and impossible deficits. An open system suddenly possessed more mouths than Aurelian had authored.

Then Kujo turned his attention toward the Boundary.

He extended two claws and removed several microscopic sections of spatial continuity along the edge of Aurelian’s declared battlefield. The boundary itself remained intact everywhere else, and Kujo never attempted to cancel the working currently sustaining it. He simply made a closed perimeter topologically incapable of being closed.

“Authorization remains. Direction remains. Your Scale remains.”

Kujo’s head tilted.

“But your room has holes in it.”

The unsealed law now possessed paths beyond the original battlefield, and Kujo allowed those paths to remain available without dictating what the Scale chose to do with them. He had not rewritten Aurelian’s command, because that would have required contesting its authority directly. He had altered the geometry in which that command was expected to remain meaningful.

His attention shifted toward the Hammer.

The gold tessellation across Darius’s form did not concern him nearly as much as what it revealed about the load being placed into that structure. Kujo could feel the accumulated Attraction Force becoming usable strength, could see the Hammer becoming a reservoir for what the Scale had transferred, and understood why ordinary pressure would be an inelegant answer. So he removed the pressure around Darius completely.

A narrow corridor of perfect environmental absence appeared around the advancing man.

No crushing Aether leaned into that corridor, no atmospheric resistance pressed against it, and no hostile external field attempted to couple to anything within. Kujo did not challenge refusal with greater insistence. He gave refusal nothing to refuse.

“That is your favorite miracle, is it not?”

Kujo lowered his execution limb.

“Nothing external couples to you.”

His grin widened.

“So I will stop being external.”

The Hammer arrived.

Kujo allowed the headbutt, the rising motion, the accumulated force, and the completed contact to occur without intercepting any of them. His transformed skull accepted the collision while the structural density of his true form distributed the load throughout an anatomy built to contain pressures his human presentation never displayed. There was no fracture, no blood, and no visible injury.

Then the strike became past.

Kujo’s Unwinding moved without golden eyes, without Xevorith, and without any borrowed Layer surrounding him. The completed sequence no longer maintained an active causal hold upon his present body, so Kujo withdrew its standing as an explanation for his current state. The impact remained historical, the battlefield retained whatever independent consequences had already propagated outward, but Kujo himself owed the completed strike nothing.

His head settled exactly where it had begun.

“Better.”

The Void Regent slowly rolled his monstrous neck.

“But still finished.”

The released Hammer force erupted through the ruined quarter, and Kujo remained within it rather than attempting to outrun the detonation. He surrounded his body with hundreds of discontinuous voidic gaps, each one smaller than dust and positioned between successive layers of his armor, flesh, internal Aether, and the surrounding shock medium. Force reached one layer and discovered that the next layer was not continuously adjacent to it.

The explosion still existed.

Kujo simply refused to provide it an uninterrupted transmission chain through his body.

His monstrous silhouette remained standing inside the destruction while the surrounding city bore the consequences that actually belonged to it. When the release completed and its direct causal claim upon him ended, The Unwinding quietly removed whatever residual relationship the finished event still attempted to carry into Kujo’s present. There was nothing dramatic about the technique now.

It had become habit.

“Physical force assumes continuity.”

Kujo opened his articulate claw.

“That assumption is becoming very expensive around me.”

Ruin entered next.

Kujo felt it identify approaching endings throughout the darkness, the tendrils, the joints of his transformed anatomy, and the surrounding structures carrying temporary arrangements. He did not fight the timetable Darius had accelerated. He completed it himself.

Several tendrils detached.

Sections of external darkness collapsed.

Temporary plates along Kujo’s shoulders reached their endings and dropped from his body before Ruin could convert their failure into a widening structural chain. Kujo discarded each compromised arrangement the moment its conclusion became inevitable.

None of them reassembled.

Fresh structures grew elsewhere.

New tendrils emerged from different portions of his back, new armored segments formed along altered load paths, and new Void expressions appeared without attempting to restore the exact things Purity had been prepared to deny. Kujo’s true form did not insist that yesterday’s anatomy deserved another chance.

“You accelerate endings.”

Kujo flexed the newly formed clawed structures.

“I replace the sentence after the period.”

Purity followed through the openings Ruin had created.

Kujo watched the Work remove the conditions under which several Void formations could continue through refusal, and he permitted each targeted formation to die permanently. He did not regenerate them, recall them, or force them to contradict the judgment applied against them. He simply stopped needing them.

Then Kujo created absences instead of constructs.

Hair-thin voidic discontinuities appeared throughout the volume surrounding his body, possessing no sustained anatomy, no refusal, and no internal phenomenon requiring permission to continue. Purity could reach their borders, but there was nothing within the missing intervals to cleanse. Kujo had transformed a contest of powers into a question of whether absence itself counted as an arrangement.

“Your Purity is excellent.”

His execution limb scraped slowly across the ruined stone.

“It kills things.”

His eyes narrowed.

“So I stopped giving it things.”

Haruki’s Kokudan found him.

Kujo felt the harmonic identification settle across his darkness, tendrils, external Void structures, and the weakest relationships joining those expressions to his transformed frame. He did not obscure the bonds or falsify their locations. He allowed her to read them perfectly.

Her severance reached the correct point.

Kujo released the bond an instant before the strike could force him to defend it.

The surrounding darkness separated from him, tendrils associated with that particular external structure scattered away, and the connection Haruki had identified ceased because Kujo himself had chosen not to maintain it. Her cut could not injure him through a relationship he had already abandoned. His true body remained beneath it.

“Accurate.”

Kujo’s eyes followed the completed line.

“You severed exactly what you saw.”

He slowly spread his claws.

“I was never obligated to remain attached to it.”

Temporal Harvest changed the character of the next attack.

Kujo watched cause and execution compress toward one another until the practical distinction between intention and arrival became dangerously thin. He made no attempt to race the acceleration, because speed was merely a measure applied across a route. Kujo removed the route.

A gap appeared between successive locations along Haruki’s projected line of attack.

It possessed no measurable distance because distance itself had been omitted from that interval, leaving no continuous pathway through which increased velocity could gain advantage. Temporal Harvest could reduce the time required to cross existent space as brutally as it wished. Kujo had made part of that space nonexistent.

“Faster.”

His monstrous smile widened.

“Faster still.”

The Crevice pulsed.

“You cannot outrun a missing road.”

The slash nevertheless completed wherever continuity still permitted it to arrive.

Kujo allowed the edge to pass through an external armored layer rather than interrupting the still-active attack. Only after the blade had finished, momentum had carried the event beyond its contact interval, and the severance had become a completed sequence did his Unwinding answer. The finished cut lost its causal standing over his present.

Nothing regenerated.

Nothing reversed.

The past remained perfectly visible to anyone capable of observing it, including the exact instant in which the strike had landed. Kujo simply stood afterward without allowing that completed past to determine his present anatomy. That distinction pleased him more every time someone forced him to demonstrate it.

“Watch it again if you like.”

His voice deepened.

“You will see yourself succeed.”

The execution limb lifted.

“And then you will see how little success means after it is finished.”

The magnetic field formed around him.

Kujo immediately recognized the difference and did not attempt The Unwinding, because the field was still actively constraining the present. He allowed the rods, metal, electrical relationships, and magnetic geometry to establish themselves completely around his position. Then a second skin formed one millimeter away from his true body.

It was made of absence.

The magnetic field remained functional outside that shell, but the medium required to communicate its influence across the final millimeter no longer existed continuously. Field lines bent around the missing layer and closed upon themselves while Kujo remained physically untouched inside the voidic envelope. He had not destroyed the cage.

He had removed the prisoner’s address from inside it.

“Much better, Haruki.”

Kujo took one slow step.

“This one is still happening.”

Another step followed.

“So I cannot Unwind it.”

His grin sharpened.

“I simply do not have to live where you put it.”

Kokudan’s continuing lock remained more troublesome than the magnetic restraint.

Kujo answered it with microscopic discontinuities inserted into the final portions of every traced relationship, never severing enough of the bond to make the lock lose recognition entirely. It could identify him, follow him, map him, and determine where its authority ought to arrive. The last fraction simply never became continuous with its destination.

Kujo wanted Haruki to keep seeing him.

Blindness would have been mercy.

He wanted the lock to remain correct all the way until the instant correctness required access. At that final interval, Void replaced continuity with absence.

“A map can be perfect.”

Kujo’s true form straightened.

“And still lead to a bridge that is no longer there.”

His attention finally passed toward the crimson-black Obsession towering behind the battlefield.

Kujo did not touch Asante’s force.

He did not sever it, suppress it, or attempt to erase the child’s hatred, because those answers would have turned the Obsession itself into the primary contest. Instead, Kujo looked at the Attraction transfer already being offered toward that force and opened a frictionless voidic channel around its route.

He contributed nothing.

He merely removed resistance.

The path became unnaturally efficient, allowing whatever was already moving through it to travel without the ordinary losses imposed by a contested battlefield. Kujo had not attacked Asante, altered Asante, or commanded his Obsession Force. He had simply made generosity easier.

His laughter returned.

“Aurelian gives.”

The tendrils behind Kujo opened like a grotesque crown.

“Asante refuses to let go.”

His claws spread.

“And none of you thought to ask what happens when I remove the friction between those two virtues.”

Kujo looked across all of them. The Scale remained active. The Harmonic field remained active. Purity and Ruin still existed. Obsession still stood over the district.

Kujo had not needed to erase a single one. That was the point.

“You keep trying to defeat the Void as though it were another element.”

Black-gold light climbed through the channels of his true form.

“Fire burns.”

His execution limb lowered.

“Light illuminates.”

The Crevice opened brighter beneath his sternum.

“Force moves.”

Kujo’s voice became quieter.

“Void removes the assumptions that let those verbs reach their objects.”

He began walking forward.

Tiny spatial discontinuities opened and closed around each footstep, allowing the enormous body to move without submitting to the geometry the battlefield had arranged for him. His personal Unwinding waited beneath everything, silent and unused until completed causality gave it something lawful to discard. The Void around him did not roar.

It edited.

Kujo’s monstrous face twisted into a delighted smile.

“Come.”

His execution limb rose beside him.

“Keep your Scale open.”

The tendrils spread behind his shoulders.

“Keep your Purity absolute.”

The crimson channels across his body brightened.

“Keep cutting faster.”

The Crevice pulsed once.

“Keep hating.”

Kujo opened his arms to the entire ruined quarter.

“I want all of it.”

His voice crashed outward through Val Nihil’s blackened heavens.

“BECAUSE EVERY POWER YOU BRING ME TEACHES ME ANOTHER CONDITION I CAN REMOVE.”

The Void Regent lowered himself into stance.

His true form had not acquired a wound. Not one plate had remained broken. Not one drop of blood had touched the ground.

Kujo smiled at the assembled judgment before him.

“NOW TRY AGAIN.”

His claws tightened.

“AND THIS TIME…” The Crevice gave one slow, terrible beat.

“MAKE ME NEED TO DEFEND MYSELF.”

Aurelian Prudentius had heard the Regent say *weigh nothing* and had taken it as an instruction.

"Fini faciem."

Limit the face.

Darius heard the clause go out over the burning street and understood the target before the sentence finished, because he could see the forty-nine points the Regent had opened, each one a place where possession had no valid state, each one a mouth the Scale could pour into forever and never fill.

"Detrahe formam."

Draw down its form.

*He is bounding them.*

*A nothing with no edge cannot be weighed. That is the entire trick and it is a good one. But put a boundary around a nothing and the nothing inside it is no longer the same as the nothing outside, and the difference between the two has a magnitude, and a magnitude is a thing a pan can hold.*

"Quidquid e nihilo tractum est, in aliquid componatur."

Whatever has been drawn from nothing, let it be assembled into something.

The forty-nine points acquired faces.

Darius watched it happen and had to revise a thing he had believed since he was twenty-six. Each absence in the field, which had been a hole in possession, got a surface put on it. Turgia. Creation, lasting change, the Plane of Fate, and the oldest Method in the register and the one every practitioner underestimates because it is slow. The Primate did not fill the holes. He gave every one of them a limit, and a limited nothing has a quantity, and forty-nine quantities went onto the far pan at once.

The Scale moved.

A change in the energy of a bounded vacuum has a mass. That is not metaphor and it has been measured, and the Primate had just given forty-nine absences a boundary each and thereby forty-nine masses, and the Regent's inexhaustible problem had become an exhaustible one at the exact moment it acquired a shape.

The impossible deficit stopped being impossible. It was still a deficit and it was still large and the Scale could now measure it, and a thing that can be measured can be paid, and Aurelian went down another two inches into the flags paying it and did not stop weaving.

Then the working came back up the street and into Darius.

He shifted.

Purity out. It had been running in every fault Ruin opened and it had nothing left to find, because the Regent had stopped giving it things, and a Work with no object is a Work being spent for nothing.

Ruin out. Same reason, worse: the man had started completing his own endings ahead of the timetable, and a Work that finds endings has no purchase on a thing that is already finishing itself.

*He answered both of the first two by giving them nothing. So I stop offering them the things he can discard.*

**Order.**

The Fourth Work. It is the least impressive of the seven and Gimbzo had made him hold it for a month longer than any of the others because it refuses to be impressive, and what it does is continuity. It does not create and it does not destroy. It insists that a road which was there a moment ago is still there, and it makes the insistence stick, and it costs the practitioner nothing except the requirement that he himself be continuous, which is the one thing Darius has never had any trouble with.

He laid it across the gaps.

The Fourth Work costs almost nothing to run and it is the only one of the seven that has a hard prerequisite, which is that the practitioner has to be the kind of thing that does not have gaps in it. Every other Work can be run by a man who is falling apart. Order cannot. The Order had known that for a thousand years and had put it in the entrance examination as a riddle without ever once explaining the answer, and Darius had passed the examination at nineteen and had not understood the answer until the fourth month in the ash.

The missing roads. Every hair-thin discontinuity the Regent had opened in the volume around himself, every interval from which distance had been omitted, every removed millimetre of the medium between a rod and a prisoner. Order went into the volume and found the places where the geometry had been edited and put the geometry back, and the geometry stayed back, because a road that Order has declared continuous does not consult anybody about whether it is.

*That is the first thing he has had to hold, and holding is a different verb from defending. He has to keep those gaps open against a Work whose entire content is that things stay where they were, and every one of them he keeps open is a thing he is now spending on.*

**Spirit.**

The Third Work. The traffic. And the Crevice under that sternum was a rate before it was anything else, a slow interval he had heard through his boots, and a rate is traffic, and traffic is the one Work the Order had never thought to point at anything that was not a soul.

He pointed it at the Crevice.

He could hear it. That was the reading and it was the only one that mattered: through the flags, through the soles of both boots, in the long bones, a slow interval that coupled to nothing in him and arrived anyway, slower than any pulse a body has. Everything else at the end of the street had been replaced at least once in the last minute. The interval had not moved.

*You discard tendrils. You discard plates. You discard the dark and grow it back somewhere else and none of it costs you because none of it is you.*

*That is you. You put it into the relationship between yourself and the Void and you cannot take it out again, and it has a rhythm, and I can hear the rhythm, and Spirit is how I take hold of a rhythm.*

*Keep drawing it out. Keep pulling.*

Two Works at once, held in one Lattice, with the Attraction Force from the Scale loading both. Conjunctia, and gated high, and the Order's own register lists it as the last Method a practitioner learns and the first one that kills him. He had never run two. He ran two now, Order holding the roads and Spirit pulling on the beat, and the tessellation across his body tightened until the cells were the size of grains and the gold at their boundaries went white.

The claw came first.

He went in with the left hand open and the fingers hooked and the whole tessellated arm loaded to the point where the Attraction Force in it had nowhere left to be stored, and the air in front of the hand broke down.

Dielectric breakdown. Air is an insulator up to a threshold and above that threshold it stops being one, all at once, along whatever path the field finds cheapest, and the field standing off Darius's forearm was past that threshold by a margin no instrument in Kharven was rated to express. The air went conductive. Light sparked off the fingertips in branching lines and the lines did not scatter. They followed the hand. Electricity dragged forward through the breakdown channel ahead of the claw at the speed a discharge propagates, which is faster than any hand, and it reached the side of the thing in front of him a full interval before the fingers did.

The fingers went for the side.

Below the lowest rib on the left, where the plates of the transformed anatomy had to leave a gap for the frame to bend, because every armoured thing that bends has a gap there, and the gap was where the crimson channels ran closest to the surface.

The claw met the missing millimetre.

Order had put the roads back everywhere in the volume except the one place that mattered, the skin of absence one millimetre off the true body, and the fingers arrived at it and found no medium to close through, and the discharge ran round the outside of the shell like water round a stone and grounded in the flags.

*Held. He held that one. He let the rest go and held the one that keeps my hand out.*

*Then I have found out what he cannot afford to lose.*

He was already spinning, because the alternative was to be standing still at eleven inches with a spent arm, and the Aetheric Density around that shell of absence had gone past anything a Lattice can stand in for longer than the length of a breath.

A body that has just missed with its left hand, at full commitment, at eleven inches, is a body about to be killed, and there is exactly one thing it can do that does not involve stopping. It can use the miss. The whole loaded mass of the arm was still going forward and the hips were still behind it and Darius put his right shoulder down and let the momentum take him over.

A revolving shoulder roll. Taught in the Order's third-year manual to men who would never need it, as the thing you do when the parry has failed and the opponent is too close to run from. The shoulder comes over and the spine follows and the body goes through the space below the strike instead of away from it, and it comes up on the far side facing the wrong way with the weapon hand at the bottom of its swing.

He came up behind the left flank with the Hammer at full extension below his hip, and the shoulder that had taken the roll had gone in a way he would find out about later, and the tessellation across the back of it had broken into irregular cells and was trying to re-tile.

"Divine Hammer."

He had never once said its name aloud. He had held it twenty-one years and had called it the Hammer in his own head and in every letter and had refused the Order's name for it in writing on four occasions, and he said it now because a name spoken at the top of a swing is a Direction glyph and he needed the direction to be exact.

"Ignis."

Fire. His own first name is built on the same root and he had never let anyone remark on it.

He opened his hand.

Further than a fraction. Further than he had ever gone, the tolerance he had worked out by feel at thirty-five and had never revised, exceeded on purpose for the first time in his life, and a portion of the most obstinate object in four worlds was permitted to stop being obstinate, and this time the portion was large enough to have mass in the sense that matters.

The head of the Hammer went to the density of a star's interior.

It did not detonate. That was the release he had made every other time and it was not what he wanted now. He held the decompression back, held every neutron in that measured portion at the point of decay and refused to let a single one go, and what he had at the end of his arm for one second was a piece of degenerate matter with the full mass of that density and none of the explosion.

Mass gravitates.

*You have a centre. Everything in this street has been drawn toward it since the ninth hour, and you have discarded every other thing you own and you have kept that, because you cannot put it down.*

*I have a centre now too.*

The Hammer pulled.

It ignored the flesh and the plates and the tendrils, which he had stopped caring about, and pulled on the beat. On the thing under the sternum that had a rhythm and a location, the one object in four hundred feet that had been holding still while everything around it was edited, and a compact mass close to an extended body does not pull on the body evenly. It pulls on the near side harder than the far side, and the difference across the body is the tidal load, and the tidal load is what tears things that have never been torn.

Spirit had hold of the rhythm. Order was holding the road between the Hammer and the Crevice continuous. And the mass at the end of Darius's arm dragged on the one structure in the street that could not be discarded, and pulled it toward a single location, and the location was the head of Ignis.

Then he brought it down.

Through the pulled thing rather than the body, into the ground, with the whole of the drawn-in mass going down ahead of it, and the release at the end of the arc was the decompression he had held back for one full second, let go all at once, expanding, into whatever he had dragged into its path.

The Vaeloris quarter went down for two hundred feet in every direction, and the reflection off the outer wall came back and met itself over the crater, and Darius's own skeleton took the whole of it, because the containment gives him nothing and asks him for everything and he had just asked it for more than it had ever been asked.

The disc in his neck stopped reporting. He registered that as an entry and did not have time to read what it meant.

Aurelian did not stop weaving through any of it.

Darius could see him at the edge of the release, nine inches into the flags now, with the Scale under the street and the forty-nine bounded absences on the far pan, and the Primate had turned his hands over.

"Pondera eum."

Weigh him.

That was the second working and it was not aimed at the deficit and it was not aimed at the allies. It was aimed at the Regent, and it put the transformed thing at the end of the street on the pan, and a thing on a pan has a weight, and a weight is a fact that the Scale then holds against everything else in the field.

*He is measuring him. In the middle of this, with the Residual Strain of an unsealed working climbing behind his sternum and nowhere to put it. He has put the Void Regent on the Scale and he is going to read the number, because that is what the office does and there is nothing left in him that would think not to.*

The ground under Aurelian went down four inches in one motion.

*And the Rigidity Effect has him. A soul's reduced capacity to contradict a law it carries, scaling with how absolute the law is. He has made the Scale absolute. He cannot now stop weighing any more than the Hammer can stop refusing, and I have known that for six minutes and there is nothing I can do about it that does not involve leaving this street.*

Darius came up out of the crater with the neck he had and the tessellation white and Ignis refusing in his right hand, and the whole of the quarter burning behind him, and turned back toward the dark.

Haruki's frame shifted, the harmony altering as the field as she heard the words come cleanly. The challenge, the boldness in his voice, she was almost amused by it's confidence, considering how little she had only just started to use.

"You assumed that was the depth, but that truly was only the surface. Perhaps you'll learn better with examples instead." She said, a snap from her fingers as the action and defiance shifted into ignition. Kujo found himself casted ablaze as the electricity around him began to coil inward. The electricity striking around him, prickling and paralyzing as it carved into his plating. Underneath it began the heating of his body, the silent sizzling of the direct opposition of the cold touch from the void to the sudden warmth he felt.

Haruki exploited the weakness between the plates as the crystal aligned up close to the center between the plates and the vulnerability she crafted. With a swift slash of her blade the bond was severed, which in return, resulted in the collapse. Around him came the rapid barrage of explosions, but most importantly was the armor's durability rapidly sheading off piece by piece. The environment shifted as the void's influence on this land began to shift and turn. The harmony between the void and the layer of this area began to lose congruency, the alignment shifted and began to turn as the field around began to alter with the rapid bursts.

The Kokudan locked onto the bond between his connection here, the source of where he began to draw the lines and where she remained locked. The tracing among the threads, her light revealing the darkness within the silence of the void's dark shade. There she found the forbidden thread, the one Kujo did not intend for her to find. 

"Danshi!" She called out, the thread severing as the fate was in return redirected back towards it's source, rejoining and rerouting the connection between them and the material realm behind in the void. Her pulse sending out as the local-wellspring began to shift and stir, her palm released outward. She projected out the cold and yet punishing ice of Kharven around Kujo, the bonds between his movements stagnating as his frame began to be encapsulated in ice and frost.

The first golden eye opened across Kujo’s shoulder.

Then another opened along the black armor of his ribs, another inside the curve of a tendril, and then dozens more appeared throughout the architecture of his true form until gold stared outward from every direction. They were not Xevorith returning to possess him, nor had Kujo crawled back into the Fifth Layer he had discarded. What Xevorith had once supplied externally, Kujo had finally learned to reproduce through his own Unwinding.

The eyes were different now.

Before, they had looked backward through completed sequence and given Kujo extraordinary access to the past. Now half of them remained fixed upon what had happened, while the others watched what was still happening, distinguishing completed causality from active, load-bearing present conditions with an accuracy he had never possessed before. Past and present no longer blurred together around him.

Kujo slowly smiled.

“So that was the missing piece.”

Gold rotated through the eyes covering his transformed body as countless intervals separated themselves before his perception. An attack could now be marked while active, followed through every Material, Soul, and Fate dependency sustaining it, and Unwound the precise instant its final present-tense claim ceased. Kujo no longer needed to recognize completion afterward.

He could wait at the boundary.

The first forty-nine void points acquired faces.

Kujo watched Aurelian’s Turgia force boundaries around the null destinations, watched Fate differentiate internal absence from external reality, and understood immediately what the Primate had accomplished. A bounded vacuum possessed measure, and measure gave the Scale something finite enough to place upon a pan. Aurelian had converted Kujo’s immeasurable deficit into forty-nine payable quantities.

“Very good.”

Kujo did not remove the boundaries.

He changed their histories.

The golden eyes looking backward found the completed moments in which each void point had been opened before Aurelian imposed form upon it, while the eyes watching the present tracked the active Turgia currently maintaining those faces. Kujo could not Unwind the active boundaries while their law still sustained them, so he attacked the older causal foundation beneath them instead.

The Unwinding moved.

Each void point retained the boundary Aurelian had imposed in the present, but Kujo withdrew the completed sequence establishing that the absence inside had originally possessed a stable location relative to the surrounding Material Plane. A boundary now enclosed something whose historical coordinates no longer had standing with Kujo’s current spatial architecture. The faces existed.

Their contents no longer agreed about where “inside” was.

The forty-nine bounded deficits began presenting contradictory measures.

Not because Kujo had broken the Scale, but because the objects being weighed possessed present surfaces surrounding historically unanchored interiors. Material geometry reported finite volume, Fate recognized the imposed distinction, and the Void inside refused continuity with the prior positional sequence used to define its quantity. Kujo had turned forty-nine numbers into forty-nine arguments.

“You gave nothing a face.”

His monstrous voice rolled through the burning quarter.

“I removed the childhood photographs.”

Then Order entered the battlefield.

Kujo felt the Fourth Work settle across every discontinuity he had created and insist upon Continuity with an almost offensively simple doctrine: the road existed, therefore the road remained. Hair-thin gaps closed, missing millimeters regained geometry, and the local Material Plane began restoring the spatial adjacency Kujo had removed. The golden eyes across his body narrowed together.

“Order.”

Kujo tasted the word.

“That one matters.”

He did not attempt to erase the Work.

Instead, Kujo allowed every restored road to remain restored and created new endpoints.

Order insisted that a road continue between the positions it recognized, but Kujo’s Void began shifting the destinations attached to those roads without removing the roads themselves. Continuity survived.

Arrival changed.

A restored millimeter between Kujo and the surrounding field remained perfectly continuous, but its final coordinate terminated one millimeter beside him instead of upon him. Another road remained intact from beginning to end, except Kujo removed the proposition that its endpoint and his surface occupied the same local address. Order had preserved the bridges.

Kujo moved the doors.

“Continuity is not identity.”

The golden eyes rotated.

“You preserved the road.”

His grin widened.

“You forgot to preserve what it reaches.”

The restored magnetic pathways remained continuous.

The electromagnetic field remained real.

The air remained conductive.

Yet when influence crossed the final interval, it arrived at coordinates Kujo’s active Voidic topology had displaced from his true body by fractions too small for ordinary perception to register. He had stopped fighting Continuity and begun exploiting Spatium inside it.

Then Spirit touched the Crevice.

For the first time, several of Kujo’s golden eyes stopped moving.

The Third Work had not grabbed armor, tendrils, darkness, or temporary Void structures. It had recognized the slow interval beneath Kujo’s sternum, the pulse that had remained unchanged while everything else around him was discarded and replaced, and it had reached toward that rhythm as traffic. The clawed fingers of Kujo’s articulate hand slowly curled.

“There you are.”

The Crevice pulsed.

Spirit pulled again.

Kujo did not hide the rhythm, because hiding it would have admitted that the reading itself was dangerous enough to deserve concealment. Instead, he opened the Void immediately beside the Crevice and created a second interval with precisely the same observable cadence. Then a third appeared.

Then twelve.

The false rhythms were not illusions.

Each was a genuine local collapse-rate generated through tiny Crevice-derived failures in surrounding reality, all beating at the exact interval Darius had correctly identified. Spirit now possessed multiple authentic traffic patterns sharing one frequency.

Kujo laughed.

“You found my pulse.”

The golden eyes brightened.

“So I gave the room a heartbeat.”

The false intervals spread outward through the rubble, walls, atmosphere, and fragments of dead Void matter.

Each carried the same measurable rate but not the same structural significance, forcing any Spirit-based grasp operating through rhythm recognition to distinguish identity rather than merely cadence. Kujo’s true Crevice remained one member inside a choir of valid identical beats. He had not falsified the signal.

He had destroyed its exclusivity.

Then Kujo felt the incoming hand.

Electricity arrived before flesh through dielectric breakdown, the surrounding Aetheric Density driving the atmosphere past its insulating threshold while the strike pursued the plate gap below his lowest rib. The attack was physically intelligent, targeting articulation rather than armor and using Fulguria through a conductive channel established ahead of the body. Kujo watched it with several present-facing eyes.

He did not make another missing millimeter.

Order was waiting for that.

Instead, Kujo removed charge separation from the air touching his body.

The conductive channel still formed everywhere else, branching light racing faithfully toward him, but the final layer surrounding Kujo possessed no permitted distinction between the electrical states required to produce useful potential difference. Electricity arrived at the boundary and found a region in which “higher” and “lower” electrical potential had been locally flattened into the same condition. The discharge crawled sideways across his armor and abandoned him for easier ground.

“Fulguria needs difference.”

Kujo looked down toward the point of attack.

“Void does not always remove matter.”

His claw opened.

“Sometimes I remove the reason current moves.”

The physical strike followed.

Kujo allowed the contact sequence to begin and tracked it through the eyes governing his present. The instant skin, force, and intended target relationship became active, those eyes marked every dependency sustaining the exchange. He did not Unwind it.

Not yet.

The miss became movement.

The movement became rotation.

The rotation became another attack.

Kujo’s past-facing eyes watched the earlier physical sequence cross the threshold into completion while the present-facing eyes remained fixed upon the developing Hammer strike. The moment the earlier contact no longer carried an active claim, Unwinding removed every residual effect it might have left upon Kujo.

Then the Divine Hammer changed.

Kujo felt the mass before the downstroke.

The head entered a degenerate state approaching stellar interior density, and the battlefield around it began responding gravitationally rather than merely mechanically. The crucial point was obvious: this was no longer simply a weapon strike.

It had become a local mass event.

Kujo’s golden eyes contracted.

“That…” His monstrous grin widened. “…is finally interesting.”

The Hammer pulled on the Crevice. Order maintained the road. Spirit maintained the rhythm.

The stellar-density mass established an enormous gravitational gradient across Kujo’s transformed body, tidal acceleration seeking to drag the near side harder than the far while the Spirit Work maintained recognition of the rhythm beneath his sternum. Kujo did not attempt to overpower gravity.

He changed what gravity was allowed to recognize as his center.

The Crevice remained where it was.

Kujo did not move it.

Instead, he spread his present-tense Material acknowledgement across every golden eye covering his body, granting each one a fraction of his recognized gravitational address through a Voidic distribution of positional identity. The Hammer suddenly pulled not upon one center of Kujo, but upon scores of locally valid manifestations of where his mass relationship was permitted to resolve.
