“I am not stealing your power,” Kujo said, almost pleasantly. “You may keep every point the Continuum ever awarded you and every technique you earned honestly.” The golden eyes followed the imagined fractures through the Shell architecture. “I am only damaging the pipes.”

The next assault moved toward the Attraction Layer.

Kujo used [Lei] Binding to identify those precise Fate relationships through which allies recognized each other as compatible partners for Synergia, then brought [Uur] Disruption, [Vorn] End, and [Ir] Continuum through only those mechanical bonds. He left friendship, loyalty, memory, affection, and simple recognition untouched. “NIHIL PARU ONE-HUNDRED-AND-TWENTY-SIX, SOLITARY OFFICE.”

What Kujo intended to end was the narrower metaphysical certainty that said, your working may safely become part of mine. Two allies could survive, speak afterward, remember every battle they had ever fought together, and still find their Attraction Layers refusing clean cooperative entry when Synergia was attempted again. Their emotional relationship would remain, which meant they would understand exactly what had been taken from them. “Some wounds wait until you need another person before they begin hurting,” Kujo said.

Then came perception.

Black-gold [Ie] Perception identified the complete path from sensory reception through recognition into meaningful interpretation, and [Thru] Hollow opened only in selected portions of that path. Kujo deliberately avoided crude blindness. “NIHIL PARU ONE-HUNDRED-AND-THIRTY-THREE, THE MISSING IMAGE.”

An eye could remain healthy, transmit light perfectly, and deliver intact information through the nervous and spiritual pathways behind it. The Hollow would instead remove selected significance after arrival, allowing someone to see a blade without immediately recognizing threat, see a familiar glyph without converting shape into meaning, or perceive a known combat pattern while losing the intuitive recognition that once made it useful. Kujo preferred damaging interpretation because replacement organs did not automatically repair it. “Eyes are remarkably replaceable,” he said, “meaning is not.”

Finally, the cruelest number emerged.

Black-gold Parun rose behind Kujo in a vertical structure crossing Material sight, Soul perception, and Fate recognition simultaneously. [Vorn] End, [Ir] Continuum, [Lei] Binding, and [Thru] Hollow locked together until the technique looked less like an attack than a medical conclusion written by something that hated recovery. “NIHIL PARU ONE-HUNDRED-AND-FORTY, HEALED WRONG.”


[Vorn] defined the injury-phase as already complete, [Ir] carried the resulting structure forward, and [Lei] integrated that condition into the surviving person rather than leaving it as foreign damage. [Thru] then hollowed the recognition by which restoration would normally identify the missing function as an unresolved wound demanding correction. Flesh could close, the Soul could stabilize, Fate could recognize continued life, and regeneration could perform flawlessly around the altered architecture. Healing itself would become the mechanism that finalized the maiming.

Kujo opened his arms beneath the golden eyes.

“I learned something from all of you,” he said, while the black-gold Paru surrounding his higher form changed from defensive inscriptions into surgical targeting marks. “Every one of you is extraordinarily good at repairing damage, reversing trauma, rebuilding structure, and refusing an ending.” His smile appeared beneath the horned crown.

“So I stopped making damage.”

The Crevice pulsed again.

“I make outcomes now.”

Kujo’s claws lowered toward the selected functions rather than vital organs, every attack calculated to leave the owner alive long enough for the altered architecture to settle into Continuum. He wanted movement lost without amputation, casting ruined without emptied reserves, cooperation severed without hatred, perception damaged without blindness, and recovery tricked into treating catastrophe as health. The cruelty was not that their bodies might fail to heal.

The cruelty was that they might heal perfectly.

Kujo’s golden eyes narrowed together as Nihil Paru One-Hundred-and-Forty waited to recognize the moment each maiming became incorporated rather than injured. Once that threshold passed, ordinary restoration would no longer be repairing a wound but attempting to rewrite what the survivor had become across Material, Soul, and Fate. Kujo smiled at that distinction. “Now survive me long enough to discover which parts of you survival decided to keep.”

The whole of it had been decided when the boundary went up and neither of them said so.

Darius understood it standing in the equalised field with the Hammer at the bottom of its arc, watching black-gold numerals from a register nobody in the Concord had ever indexed open one after another across a seven-foot-four frame, and the understanding was very simple and he took no pleasure in it.

Every one of those entries described a removal. Hollow. End. Null. Disruption. The Aeloran register runs on Nihiloth and on Fractura, which is Limina and Vectoria, and it is the finest instrument for taking things away that has ever been assembled by anybody.

And a removal is still an act, and an act on the physical still has to become physical.

*Formed Boundary is Coagulatio. Materia family. Material Science: crystalline lattice, phase state, molecular bonding. It is a statement about what is permitted to bond to what, and it is closed across this crater.*

*A glyph made of nihility is still a glyph. It has a Material expression somewhere in its three-plane grammar or it does not reach anything, and he cannot ensoul, he cannot mend Aether, and he cannot formulate. He is speaking a perfect language into a room where the air will not carry it.*

*Every number he has raised since the Pillar closed has been correct and has been recited into a boundary that will not let it actualise.*

Aetheric Density inside the Pillar had stopped climbing at the moment the boundary closed and had begun, very slowly, to fall, because saturation requires bleed and bleed was the thing Coagulatio had just declared impermissible. Every entry the Regent raised now was being recited into a field that was quietly emptying around him.

He was wise about the method. He was wrong about the task.

[Thru] Hollow had gone to the Heartflare first, and that was the error, and Darius watched it happen and had to keep his face still.

The glyph is Uurgath's root and Nihiloth carries it and its entry is four words on function: *hollows targets, removes purpose and binding.* Aimed at the Fate-position of the thing the longing expected to satisfy it, it leaves the want intact and takes away the clean recognition of what fulfilment would mean.

Which is correct, and which does not weaken the flare.

*Paru Five's stability is one hour for every point of sustained emotional intensity. Sustained. The entry says sustained.*

*A want decays when it is fed. That is the whole reason the sheet warns against fresh whims, because a whim resolves and a resolved want stops burning. He has taken the terminal object out of Aurelian's longing and made it unfulfillable, permanently, and an unfulfillable want does not decay. It cannot. There is nothing left for it to arrive at.*

*He has made the creation take longer, and taking longer is the only variable that makes it stronger, and he has done it to a man who spent forty years wanting to know what his own life had felt like.*

*And Hollow's own entry says it plainly. Removes purpose and binding. He took the binding off the object and left the purpose standing, because purpose was never seated in the object. It was seated in the man.*

The flare in the Primate's core went up.

Then Aurelian began the sequence, and it was long, and it was accurate, and every entry stood on the one before it while the one before it went on running.

**[Th] Foundation.**

Thalen's root, Petralon under it, Vectoria, Mechanics. *Imposes geometry; reinforces boundaries and load paths.* He laid it into the Pillar and the Pillar took it, and then he laid it into the same boundary as it existed in the other register, the nihil expression, the shape the wall had where the Aeloran arts would have to come through it. Load paths in both. A wall reinforced on the real face and on the face that is made of nothing, and Mechanics does not care which side of a boundary a load arrives from.

**[Vorn] End.**

Uurgath again, and this is the part Darius would keep for the rest of his life. *Forces a termination state; collapses instability.* It is one of the Regent's own roots, off Fractura, and Aurelian took it and did not point it at the Regent.

He pointed it at the argument.

The state itself, the whole contested proposition standing over that crater, forced into a perpetual condition of terminating, which is a different thing from having ended. Terminating, continuously, as its permanent state, so that every fresh assertion entering the field arrived into something already in the act of concluding and had to spend itself getting to a beginning.

**[Lh] Frequency.**

Selhar's, and the Index gives it no Wellspring and no Family at all, which is a thing Darius had noticed at nineteen and had asked about twice and had never had answered. *Modulates wavelength; smooths discord.* Aurelian took the threads of the non-real and the threads of the real and brought them onto one wavelength, and once two things share a wavelength they can interfere, and once they can interfere the one can be used on the other.

**[Et] Mirage.**

Zhaeren's register, bends perception layers, disguises truth, and everybody who has ever been taught it has been taught it as a concealment.

He ran it backwards.

A layer that can disguise the true can present the untrue, and what Aurelian needed was for the nihil to become perceivable, to be dragged up into a layer where a thing with no Material expression could be looked at and therefore handled. He put the Regent's own absences into a perception layer and made them visible.

**[Len] Line.**

Valen's root, Lethegraal beneath, Limina, Abstract and Entropic Systems. *Defines a clean sequence between two events; prevents causal tangling.*

And a defined line appeared through the whole of it, and Darius, who had spent twenty-one years writing about causal tangling and had never once seen it undone at scale, felt something in his chest go over.

*That is the anti-Unwinding and he may not know it.*

*The eyes wait at the border where present becomes past and take the completed sequence's claim. A clean line between two events is a sequence with no ambiguity about where one thing ends and the next begins, and it prevents tangling, and the border he waits at is a tangle. Aurelian has just made every one of our workings a single defined causal run from origin to arrival with no completion boundary in the middle to sit on.*

**[Vor] Return.** Auren's, Somnalis beneath it, Fluxia, Fluid Dynamics, which means it does not gather and it does not fetch. It establishes a flow and lets a fluid do what a fluid does at a pressure difference. *Recycles Essence; prevents restless dead.*

Every ounce of Essence spent on that road since the ninth hour. The Bulwark grey. The three charges. The aurora. The lines Aurelian had taken into himself and put out in three directions. The whole spent inventory of a burning quarter, gathered up as a flow and turned.

**[Ser] Renewal.** Wyther's, no Wellspring listed, no Family. *Catalyzes transformation; sheds old states.*

And it came back into all of them. Darius felt his own reservoir fill, from empty, which he had been since the roll, and the disc in his neck reported again which meant there was enough of him left for it to report to. It went to Aurelian, and to the light at the far end of the street, and low across the rubble into four streets of standing crimson-black.

**[Sil] Silence.**

Auren's, a Synonym derivative, and the Index lists it against no Wellspring at all, which is a property Darius had spent four years of his life trying to get an explanation for. *Mutes hostile resonance; dignified stillness.*

This was the long part and Aurelian did not hurry it. Silence went outward across the whole crater and into every glyph standing in it, one at a time, and it did not cancel any of them. It muted their true nature. A glyph's nature is what it announces to the field, and a muted glyph goes on functioning while ceasing to declare what it is, and a register that reads by matching every hostile glyph to the assumption beneath it needs the glyph to be saying something.

**[Vt] Vector.** Wyther's compound, Ignivale, Caloria, Thermodynamics. *Directs motion or force along a plotted line.*

Force along the lines [Len] had defined, and then diverged, out of the straight and into

**[Cy] Cycle.** Valen's synonym, Rebirthine, Vitalia, Biochemistry. *Phased rest that refreshes without full sleep.*

Expanding outward across the whole crater as a turning thing, and the turning fed [Vor] and [Ser], so the recycling and the renewal stopped being a single act and became a circuit, and a circuit does not have to be recast.

There was a moment between the Cycle and the next entry where Darius could hear the whole thing running, and it did not sound like a working. It sounded like a mill. Eleven glyphs turning against each other in an order somebody had thought about for a long time, each one taking its feed from the one before it, and the only comparable thing he had ever stood inside was the pump-house at Altherion in the year they got the ward-main working, and he had been twenty-two and had cried about it and had never told anyone why.

**[Flm] Judic Flame.**

Judicium. Fulguria. Electromagnetism. A Synonym derivative and the entry is nine words. *Marks verdicts; burns only falsehood and imbalance.*

It went up across the crater and it did not burn stone and did not burn men and did not burn the dark. It burned the imbalance. It burned the falsehood. And it burned the marbled reality that had been laid over the quarter by

**[Oth] Path.** Zhaeren's root, Eclipseron, Spatium, Spatial Geometry. *Folds space; the foundation of all Gates.*

Every fold the Regent had put into that crater, every moved door and edited road and displaced address, every place the geometry had been marbled so a road would arrive at the wrong gate first. The Judic Flame does not undo a fold. It burns a falsehood, and a road that says it arrives at one place and arrives at another is a falsehood expressed in Spatial Geometry.

And each of them actualised down into equal measure with the previous still running, which is a thing the Index warns against on eleven separate entries and which the Order's own Glyphica standards forbid outright at paragraph fourteen, in a paragraph Darius wrote, for reasons that were correct for every practitioner who has ever lived except the one standing nine inches into the flags, because the field had been brought to one potential ninety seconds earlier and nothing in that crater could cancel or overwrite anything else.

Then the Flux.

[Flx], controlled unpredictability, breaks rigidity, and it had been under everything the Aeloran register did since the first black ring appeared, because a method built on removal needs the field to stay loose enough to be removed from.

**[Tp] Topology.** Thalen's compound, Fixatio, Materia, Material Science. *Freezes a local outcome grid for a brief window.*

The grid froze. Locally, and on the Plane of Fate, which is the part the entry does not mention and which a true Mahuo can do because the organ reaches the stratum directly. Every outcome in that crater stopped being available to be shuffled. The window is brief. The entry says so and Aurelian knew it and had built everything to happen inside it.

**[Nl] Null.**

Uurgath's. Nihiloth.

*Cancels or nullifies active glyph-work; leaves no signature.*

Darius heard the Primate say it and understood the whole shape of the last five minutes at once.

*Nihiloth. It is his own Wellspring. Hollow is Nihiloth and Null is Nihiloth and they are the same current, and the Regent has spent this entire engagement demonstrating that nothing in four worlds can be used against him because he can remove the conditions under which it reaches him.*

*He cannot remove Nihiloth. It is what he is made of. A boundary that stopped Nihiloth from actualising would have stopped his own register from working an hour ago and it would have stopped this one too, and Aurelian built the Pillar knowing exactly which current he was leaving a door open for.*

Null went through the black-gold entries hanging around that frame, and it did not fight one of them, and it cancelled them in the non-existence, rupturing each order at the level where it was still only a glyphic proposition, and it found every one of them by running the search through the Plane of Fate, where a true Mahuo does not need a Material expression to reach a thing.

And it left no signature. That is the last clause on the entry and it is four words and Darius had read it a hundred times as a courtesy to the caster, a note that the work would not be traced back, the sort of line a Division writes for practitioners worried about the Bench.

It was never a courtesy. It is the entire weapon.

*That is the second half and it is the better half.*

*The eyes wait at the border for a working to complete so that the Unwinding can take its claim. A cancellation that leaves no signature does not complete. There is nothing to mark. There is no finished sequence for a past-facing eye to open on, no residual causal claim to withdraw, and no history in which the cancellation occurred to be visited afterward.*

*He has not defeated the Unwinding. He has handed it a working it cannot find.*

He looked across at the man who had done it.

Nine inches into the flags. A Scale under the street, an unsealed Turgia running since the tenth hour, a Heartflare rooted in a core that could not feel the hook, a Pillar standing on a dead friend's seam, and eleven glyphs turning in a circuit that would not need recasting. Aurelian Prudentius had built the finest single piece of Glyphica Darius had seen in fifty-six years of life, and had done it in about ninety seconds, and had not once looked as though any of it mattered to him.

*He will not survive the accounting, and the load will have very little to do with it. There is nothing left in him that will decide to stop, and the Continuum closes an unsealed working by whatever route it finds cheapest, and the cheapest route out of this crater is going to be him.*

Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum stood in a crater with a full reservoir for the first time since the gate, inside a frozen outcome grid, in a field where every glyph had gone quiet and every fold was burning, and brought the Hammer up out of the bottom of its arc.

"Paru Number Eleven, Excision of Rot." Haruki lifted up her fingers, the threads infected by the connection of his barrage of parunes collected against her grace and harmony, but with the focus and amplified pulse, Haruki used the harmony to adjust and amplify the glyphs, their misalignment and backlash hitting across her as the opposing law pressed against her own, but due to the effects of **grace** and **exile**, the tandem worked in force to keep the stability as well as hold the law between the fabric of each of the threads connected to the attempt to overtake.

Haruki severed the connection between Kujo and the parunic glyphs of **grace** and **exile**, as they were as representative to order as were law and justice. Instead, Kujo was met with the bitter edge of the sword, the flame burning his soul at a much accelerated pace, the flames filtering and burning away the contents purely based on the amount of corruption placed against the challenged authority.

The Edict was challenged, but the grace is what allowed her to win. The flame acted in reaction, the verdict spoken clear and the edict assigned.

"Ah... but that is where you are mistaken... you assumed by destruction you could remove my authority, but as long as there is grace... there will always be a spot destruction cannot penetrate." She spoke, the law taking shape as she extended her palm, thrusting it forward to press against his center mass.

Within a moment Kujo felt his temperance shift, all of his previous states within his life brushed past against him, each taking a manifested shape as they all aligned up against each other. These stages were separated from the body, the Saishiki determining the arrangement and how they should be ordered. 

Using the Saishiki with the combination of Parunes of Grace and Edict, Haruki adjusted the order and adjusted the distortion between the state Kujo had preseted before, and his current state. Kujo's body was pushed through, his true form pushed back-

the form shifted, his true state reverted and returned to the point where he had not awoken his true power. His vessel weakened back to a stage where preparations were still being made before the arrival. He felt the power absorbed by the Stark Monarch push out through him, the conjoined power separated from the user as simple as cutting out a tumor.

Haruki's eyes shifted as her palm drew back against the figure of Kujo. "And now look upon yourself, weakened, shattered, your but a shadow of what you truly were capable of. It was that pathetic confidence that made you sure... and it was the same that had brought you to your knees. Truly a poetic defeat, stand proud Kujo... you achieved something most men would desire of having." She said, her eyes looking down at him with a neutral, and yet indifferent gaze.

Kujo stopped answering the glyphs one at a time.

The golden eyes across his seven-foot-four Crevice-form all turned toward Aurelian at once, watching the immense mechanism of Foundation, End, Frequency, Mirage, Line, Return, Renewal, Silence, Vector, Cycle, Flame, Path, Topology, and Null interlock inside the frozen outcome grid. What interested him was no longer which glyph countered which, but the premise beneath the entire construction: every prediction, causal line, and prepared counter assumed that Kujo would continue to possess a next moment connected ordinarily to the one before it. The Crevice beneath his sternum opened another fraction, and Kujo smiled.

“So that is where you have been looking.”

Black-gold Void spread through Kujo’s silhouette without expanding into a construct, touching his Material presence, his Soul-recognition, and his Fate-position simultaneously. He did not accelerate time, reverse it, stop it, or enter another timeline; instead, he removed the requirement that his state at one instant be the singular lawful continuation of the state immediately preceding it. Linear sequence still existed around him. Kujo simply ceased operating according to it.

The golden eyes changed.

Those watching the present no longer looked toward one approaching future, because from Kujo’s position there was no privileged next for precognition to inspect. Every viable continuation of his current movement existed as an unresolved successor until Kujo selected one by acting, and the discarded possibilities never became events that required Unwinding. Aurelian could look ahead and see Kujo kick, evade, attack, remain still, step left, step right, or appear behind him, but none of those readings possessed authority over which one Kujo would actually promote into the present.

“Precognition is only frightening when tomorrow has agreed to stand in line.”

The black mantle hanging from Kujo’s shoulders tightened against his smaller frame. “You keep looking ahead because you assume the future is waiting in front of you.” Gold burned across the Crevice as his feet settled lightly into the rubble. “Mine is waiting everywhere.”

He called the state Void Interval.

It did not make Kujo omnipresent, and it did not give him infinite actions, because his body still had to perform whatever continuation he ultimately selected. What it removed was the predictive value of watching the causally most likely successor before that successor became present. Aurelian’s sight could remain perfect and still be useless, because perfect knowledge of possibilities was not the same as knowing which possibility Kujo would grant actuality.

Then [Len] Line attempted to preserve the clean causal runs Aurelian had built.

Kujo did not cut the Line.

He stepped outside the assumption that he had to travel along it.

The causal sequence between origin and consequence remained beautifully clean for every working already inscribed inside the crater, but Kujo’s Void Interval made his own participation discontinuous with the linear index used to define their arrival. The workings could have perfectly preserved beginnings, middles, and ends. Kujo no longer supplied a guaranteed temporal coordinate at which their ends would find him.

“Keep your Line.”

One golden eye followed it from beginning to conclusion. “I am not tangling causality.” Another eye opened beside it. “I am refusing to stand at the appointment.”

[Sil] Silence muted the nature of every glyph in the field, denying Kujo the easy register-reading by which he had previously matched symbols to the assumptions beneath them. Kujo’s response was almost dismissive, because he stopped reading the glyphs by what they announced and read their effects on the environment instead. Pressure changed, Essence routed, Aetheric Density redistributed, boundaries hardened, Fate-recognition shifted, and all of those consequences betrayed function even when the symbols themselves refused to identify what they were.

“You silenced the language.”

Kujo’s golden eyes moved from inscription to consequence rather than inscription to meaning. “Excellent.” His head tilted toward the altered geometry. “I will read the accent it leaves on reality.”

[Tp] Topology froze the outcome grid before him.

Kujo allowed the available outcomes inside the grid to remain frozen.

Void Interval attacked something earlier.

Instead of shuffling the outcomes contained by the grid, Kujo removed the identification that said Kujo’s next state was required to be selected from this grid at all, leaving the frozen topology perfectly intact around a participant whose continuity no longer indexed itself to its inventory of permitted successors. The cage still contained every door it had frozen. Kujo ceased being registered as something that required one of those doors to leave.

“That is the difference between imprisoning possibility…” His foot shifted half an inch. “…and imprisoning me.”

Then [Nl] Null moved through the Plane of Fate and cancelled the black-gold glyphic propositions Kujo had left standing.

The cancellation succeeded.

Kujo watched his own prior Parunic expressions disappear without signature, without residue, and without the finished causal footprint his past-facing eyes normally used to identify completed hostile interactions. He did not attempt to Unwind it.

Instead, he laughed.

“You finally learned to leave nothing behind.”

Kujo let the cancelled glyphs remain cancelled. “So I will stop giving you glyphs.” The black-gold inscriptions surrounding him died completely. “You have been fighting my handwriting for several minutes.”

The Crevice pulsed.

“Fight my hands.”

Kujo vanished from the position Aurelian’s prediction had been observing.

There was no spatial fold to burn with Judic Flame, no moved gate, no Path expression, and no fresh glyph to Null. Kujo simply selected a different successor from the unresolved possibilities carried inside Void Interval, and his enormous speed handled the Material distance that remained. The first thing Darius received was not Void at all.

It was a teep.

Kujo’s lead foot drove straight into Darius’s centerline with the compact mechanics of Muay Thai rather than a sprawling supernatural strike. Hip, planted heel, posterior chain, and transferred bodyweight aligned through a short piston motion whose purpose was less to launch than to destroy posture. Kujo aimed through the solar plexus and lower sternum, using enough force to collapse Darius’s stance backward without wasting the exchange attempting to overpower the Hammer directly.

Kujo landed already inside punching range.

His rear leg chopped immediately into the outside of Darius’s lead thigh.

The low kick turned the hip over completely, shin driving through the femoral nerve and lateral musculature rather than striking bone for spectacle. Kujo’s goal was systematic: damage stance first, then breathing, then balance, then the ability to generate force through the floor.

Aurelian’s future sight could perceive the next elbow.

Kujo did not throw it.

He selected the continuation in which the elbow had been a false possibility, stepped across Darius’s damaged lead line, and whipped a short right hook toward the floating ribs instead. Before the torso could finish absorbing that impact, Kujo’s left hand framed against the upper body and his right knee drove upward into the diaphragm.

“You silenced the language.”

Kujo’s golden eyes moved from inscription to consequence rather than inscription to meaning. “Excellent.” His head tilted toward the altered geometry. “I will read the accent it leaves on reality.”

[Tp] Topology froze the outcome grid before him.

Kujo allowed the available outcomes inside the grid to remain frozen.

Void Interval attacked something earlier.

Instead of shuffling the outcomes contained by the grid, Kujo removed the identification that said Kujo’s next state was required to be selected from this grid at all, leaving the frozen topology perfectly intact around a participant whose continuity no longer indexed itself to its inventory of permitted successors. The cage still contained every door it had frozen. Kujo ceased being registered as something that required one of those doors to leave.

“That is the difference between imprisoning possibility…” His foot shifted half an inch. “…and imprisoning me.”

Then [Nl] Null moved through the Plane of Fate and cancelled the black-gold glyphic propositions Kujo had left standing.

The cancellation succeeded.

Kujo watched his own prior Parunic expressions disappear without signature, without residue, and without the finished causal footprint his past-facing eyes normally used to identify completed hostile interactions. He did not attempt to Unwind it.

Instead, he laughed.

“You finally learned to leave nothing behind.”

Kujo let the cancelled glyphs remain cancelled. “So I will stop giving you glyphs.” The black-gold inscriptions surrounding him died completely. “You have been fighting my handwriting for several minutes.”

The Crevice pulsed.

“Fight my hands.”

Kujo vanished from the position Aurelian’s prediction had been observing.

There was no spatial fold to burn with Judic Flame, no moved gate, no Path expression, and no fresh glyph to Null. Kujo simply selected a different successor from the unresolved possibilities carried inside Void Interval, and his enormous speed handled the Material distance that remained. The first thing Darius received was not Void at all.

It was a teep.

Kujo’s lead foot drove straight into Darius’s centerline with the compact mechanics of Muay Thai rather than a sprawling supernatural strike. Hip, planted heel, posterior chain, and transferred bodyweight aligned through a short piston motion whose purpose was less to launch than to destroy posture. Kujo aimed through the solar plexus and lower sternum, using enough force to collapse Darius’s stance backward without wasting the exchange attempting to overpower the Hammer directly.

Kujo landed already inside punching range.

His rear leg chopped immediately into the outside of Darius’s lead thigh.

The low kick turned the hip over completely, shin driving through the femoral nerve and lateral musculature rather than striking bone for spectacle. Kujo’s goal was systematic: damage stance first, then breathing, then balance, then the ability to generate force through the floor.

Aurelian’s future sight could perceive the next elbow.

Kujo did not throw it.

He selected the continuation in which the elbow had been a false possibility, stepped across Darius’s damaged lead line, and whipped a short right hook toward the floating ribs instead. Before the torso could finish absorbing that impact, Kujo’s left hand framed against the upper body and his right knee drove upward into the diaphragm.

The Muay Thai was almost insulting in its economy.

There were no enormous Void manifestations wrapped around the strikes and no unnecessary supernatural ornamentation that a glyph could isolate and invalidate. Kujo let his speed shorten the intervals while technique supplied the damage, working the body the way a veteran fighter dismantled another veteran fighter rather than the way a sorcerer traded explosions. Each hit was small compared with what he could produce, and that was precisely why so little of the glyph architecture had anything useful to seize.

He shifted toward Aurelian before the previous exchange had become predictable.

Aurelian’s precognition could inspect the most likely entry and find a straight rush.

Kujo selected the successor in which he angled forty-five degrees instead.

His left shin cut inward against the outside of Aurelian’s knee.

Not enough to sever the leg, but enough force and placement to attack the supporting structure below the man who had already sunk into the flags under accumulated metaphysical burden. Kujo planted from the kick, stepped into clinch range, and drove the point of his right elbow toward the upper chest where breath, posture, and ritual articulation all depended upon an uninterrupted body. He did not give the Primate distance to make the exchange ceremonial.

Kujo’s hands established a brief collar tie.

His knee came upward.

It struck into the body rather than the head, hammering the upper abdomen and lower ribs with the full upward acceleration of his compact frame. Kujo released the clinch immediately afterward, denying any lasting grappling condition that could be recognized and turned into a Fate-binding.

“Your office has made you magnificent.”

Kujo’s next elbow came horizontally across the line of Aurelian’s guard. “Unfortunately, offices do not teach knees.” His golden eyes narrowed as his hips already loaded another strike. “Men do.”

Darius became the target again.

Kujo entered behind a left round kick aimed at the body and deliberately changed elevation at the last viable instant, turning it into a chopping low kick against the opposite thigh. His lead hand posted only long enough to measure distance before a rear elbow shot inward toward the ribs, followed by a short left knee delivered while the body was still reorganizing around the earlier impacts. Kujo refused to stand still long enough for the Hammer to define a reliable striking solution.

The Hammer itself received no contest.

When it threatened an active path, Kujo left the line.

When the road underneath it remained preserved by Order, Kujo used ordinary footwork rather than spatial editing.

When Nadi threatened to increase action density, Kujo’s present-facing eyes tracked the active actions while his body denied a stable target through angles, pivots, lateral steps, and his non-linear successor selection.

He was no longer trying to beat their architecture at architecture.

He was making it irrelevant to the question of whether a shin could destroy stance.

Kujo’s next combination against Darius was viciously mundane: inside low kick, left elbow, rear knee, pivot. He targeted the thigh that had already taken damage, then struck the body while weight transferred involuntarily toward the surviving leg. His speed made the combination appear simultaneous, but it remained a legitimate sequence of separate physical actions rather than an impossible multi-hit event.

Against Aurelian, Kujo became even more surgical.

A teep attacked the sternum and made maintaining rooted ritual posture more expensive; the return step became a low kick against the calf, and the low kick flowed directly into a descending elbow aimed across the shoulder line. Kujo then entered briefly into double-collar control and drove one knee toward the body before releasing again. The attacks were intended to break the practitioner down without giving his glyphic structure a single spectacular phenomenon to counter.

“The great mistake of mages…” Kujo slipped away from the centerline. “…is eventually believing every problem deserves magic.”

His black mantle trailed behind him as he turned.

“Sometimes the correct answer is a damaged liver.”

Then Haruki’s working reached him.

Kujo’s demeanor changed immediately.

Her Grace and Exile were not trying to outmuscle his higher form; they were using Saishiki to classify his states, sequence his development, and force a prior weaker arrangement into present authority. That meant the attack depended upon one especially vulnerable assumption.

That Kujo possessed a single linear history of selves.

Void Interval widened. Every golden eye opened.

Haruki’s Saishiki could still perceive every earlier Kujo correctly: the prepared Kujo, the human guise, the transformed execution-form, the Crevice-enhanced higher state, every increase in power, every acquired external force, and every transition between them. Kujo did not hide one state from her.

He removed the hierarchy.

“Past does not mean lesser.”

The states stopped arranging themselves as a ladder.

“You looked at my life and mistook chronology for rank.”

The Crevice throbbed once.

“That was careless.”

Kujo’s Void reached backward not to rewrite those states, but to strip before and after of their authority to determine which state was ontologically subordinate. The weaker configurations remained historically earlier, the stronger ones remained historically later, and all events still happened exactly where they belonged. What disappeared was the proposition that earlier necessarily meant more fundamental.

Saishiki attempted to restore the “true” prior Kujo.

It found several.

The human guise had been true. The execution-form had been true. The Crevice-form was true. The seven-foot-four higher state was true. No one of them possessed exclusive priority simply because it occupied an earlier coordinate in time.

“You cannot revert a man to his true self…” Kujo’s golden eyes burned brighter. “…when every version was genuinely him.”

The attempt to expel the power associated with later developments encountered the same problem.

Anything externally grafted could still be identified and challenged, but the Crevice was no longer an external passenger, borrowed artifact, or temporary amplification sitting atop Kujo’s architecture. He had killed Valmir, severed collapse from renewal, extracted the Crevice, and fused its law into his own structure until removing it meant defining Kujo without something Kujo had become. Saishiki could identify the historical moment before fusion.

It could not make that moment more authentic than the one standing here.

Then Grace tried to stabilize the reversion. Kujo used Void against the relationship rather than the law.

He removed the comparative statement necessary for Grace to identify the prior configuration as the healthier, cleaner, more legitimate state toward which correction should converge. Grace retained all of its authority to restore what was wounded or improperly distorted. Kujo denied that his current configuration qualified as either.

“I am not malfunctioning.”

His voice remained almost gentle.

“You dislike the result.”

One golden eye closed.

“Those are not equivalent diagnoses.”

Excision of Rot reached toward corruption.

Kujo allowed the classification to inspect him.

It found cruelty, Void, Crevice, devastation, murder, accumulated malice, and all the things Haruki expected to call corruption.

Then Kujo’s [internal] Void architecture separated moral corruption from structural foreignness.

Rot could be excised when it existed as something corrupting an otherwise prior healthy structure.

Kujo had integrated his darkness too completely for that distinction to remain clean.

“You are trying to cut the tumor out.”

His claw touched his own sternum.

“The patient became the tumor.”

The burning sword of judgment continued toward his Soul.

Kujo stepped into it.

Not because he intended to absorb the punishment, but because he used Void Interval to choose the continuation in which his Material entry occurred between the targeting recognition and the final Soul-plane assignment. The edge remained valid, the judgment remained true to its own doctrine, but Kujo ceased occupying the same three-plane correspondence the working had sampled when it committed its verdict.

Its target-profile was one Kujo-state behind.

“That is the weakness of judgment based on observation.”

Kujo appeared inside Haruki’s effective range.

“You must eventually stop looking…” His right hand opened. “…and decide.”

That instant of decision was enough.

Kujo’s lead elbow came first, not as the finishing blow but as a hard disruption aimed into the upper guard to turn structure and expose the flank. His hips then rotated as though preparing another ordinary Muay Thai strike, deliberately presenting Haruki with the same combat language he had just used upon Darius and Aurelian. The golden eyes along his mantle watched the moment her active workings committed to the physical exchange.

Kujo did not throw the second elbow. His palm opened toward her side.

The Crevice answered. Void compressed into a point barely larger than a coin.

It was not a Black Hole and possessed no broad gravitational field.

Blackness spiraled around itself in opposing directions, one layer rotating clockwise, another counterclockwise, while a third collapsed directly through the middle.

**“VOID ART, GYRE ABSOLONE.”**

Kujo fired.

The condensed spiral crossed the interval like a black drill made from missing continuity.

Rather than exploding on contact, its rotating layers tore simultaneously through Material structure, Essence conduction, and the Fate-recognition holding the struck portion together, concentrating almost all of the attack into the side of Haruki’s torso. The technique was deliberately narrower than Void Absolone had been when Zuberi died.

Kujo wanted Haruki alive.

The spiral tore through her flank and exited without traversing the center of her chest.

It left a brutal channel through the side of the torso, destroying enough muscular and structural continuity to end meaningful combat movement while deliberately avoiding the heart and central axis. Kujo cut the technique off the instant it had done the required damage.

No second blast followed. No finishing strike came.

Kujo watched the attack complete, and for once The Unwinding had nothing to do with what followed because this was a consequence he wanted history to keep.

“You wanted to return me to an earlier state.”

His claw lowered.

“So I have given you one.”

The golden eyes across his mantle settled upon Haruki’s ruined fighting posture.

“The state before you were capable of continuing this battle.”

Kujo turned away from the line of his own attack without attempting another strike against her. He had placed the damage to terminate participation rather than life, and everything about the precision of the wound made that distinction intentional rather than merciful. His attention shifted back toward Darius and Aurelian.

The Crevice beneath his sternum pulsed. Void Interval remained active.

There was still no single future in front of Kujo for Aurelian to read, only a body of unresolved successors that became real when Kujo selected them.

He raised both hands. Not like a sorcerer. Like a fighter.

His lead leg stayed light, rear heel lifted, elbows narrow, shoulders loose, and the black mantle settled behind him while the golden eyes watched every layer of the battlefield. The contrast was almost obscene: a being capable of attacking causality itself had decided that the next lesson would be delivered through knees, shins, and elbows.

Kujo smiled.

“Now.”

He shifted his weight forward.

“No more lectures.”

The future around him fractured into possibilities.

“Fight.”

The teep came up the middle at the solar plexus.

Darius read it late and read it correctly, which are two different things, and the correct read was that a push kick to the centreline is not thrown to hurt anybody. It is thrown to take a man's posture away from him, and a man without posture cannot generate anything through the floor, and everything Darius had left ran through the floor.

He did not block it.

You do not block a teep. A square block puts a shin and a hip and a bodyweight straight down your own spine and the man throwing it does not care. What you do is turn it off the line, and Darius brought his left forearm up and across in a rising sweep, catching the ball of the foot on the outside of the wrist and taking the vector wide, and at the same instant he converted through the contact and put the recoil back down the leg that had thrown it.

It worked. It was not enough.

He went back six feet on both heels with the ash ploughing up in front of his boots, and the parry had cost him the line and the line was the only thing keeping him upright, and he knew at four feet that he was going down before his knee found the ground.

He went to one knee and coughed.

Blood came up. Not much and it did not need to be much: it came up bright and frothed at the edges and that is the lung, and he had known about the lung since the second knee to the body and had filed it and had gone on filing it.

*Pulmonary contusion, lower right lobe, from the knee that came up under the ribs while I was still reorganising around the elbow. It is not the worst injury on me. It is the one that decides how long I have.*

He took inventory in the two seconds available, in the diagnostic voice, because a Vitality reading taken in a panic is worth nothing and he had been taking them on other people since he was twenty-two.

Lead thigh, outside, four kicks and the fourth had been the one. The shin had come through the vastus lateralis and into the nerve running down the outside, and the leg below the hip had gone from a limb to a weight. The common peroneal runs down the outside there under very little cover, and a shin arriving on it does not bruise a muscle so much as overload a nerve trunk, and what comes back up the leg afterward is not pain in the ordinary sense. It is absence. It would hold him standing. It would not accept a load and it would not push, and everything a man does in a fight he does by pushing off the floor.

It also meant he could no longer check. A check is the knee coming up and turning out to present the upper shin, and it is the only defence anybody has ever found against that kick, and it requires the checking leg to take the whole of the impact through a structure that is holding itself rigid. His would not hold itself rigid. So the next one landed clean and the one after that, and both of them knew it.

Cervical disc, gone since the roll. Right shoulder, gone since the roll. Three ribs on the left going the way ribs go. And the reservoir Aurelian had filled him with was already spending itself faster than it arrived, because the office pours in through a channel that cannot couple, and a man who cannot couple cannot store anything either. It runs through him. It always has.

Down the crater, in the wreck at the far end, Haruki had stopped moving the way a fighter moves.

He saw the wound from thirty yards and read it and had to put his own head down for a second, because it was a beautiful piece of work and there is no other honest word. Through the flank. Out the side. Nothing through the centre line, nothing through the heart, the channel laid exactly where enough structure lives to end a person's ability to fight and exactly clear of everything that ends a person. It had not been mercy and had not been aimed at mercy. It was a man removing a participant and keeping a witness.

Two exchanges. That was what the whole of it had come to, and Darius made himself say the number in his own head without dressing it. The Regent had put a teep, four low kicks, two elbows, a hook to the floating ribs and two knees into him inside about eleven seconds, and every one of them had been small, and none of them had carried a single ounce of Void that a glyph could have isolated and invalidated.

*That is the insult and it is deliberate. He built an arsenal of removal and then put it down and beat me with shins.*

*And he was right to. There is nothing in the Codex that answers a knee.*

Aurelian began to pour into him again.

Darius felt it arriving and felt what it was arriving from, and the Primate was nine inches into the flags with a Scale and an unsealed Turgia and a Heartflare rooted in a core that could not feel the hook, and every ounce that came into Darius was leaving a man who had no mechanism left to notice it going.

"Don't."

It came out on half a breath and he had to stop and get another one.

"I can fight him alone now, Aurelian. You have done enough."

The Primate did not answer, and the flow slowed, and stopped.

Darius set the Hammer down.

He laid it on its side in the ash with the haft toward his own foot, which is how you put down a weapon you intend to pick up, and it went on refusing there, quietly, as it had since before there was a Kharven.

Then he took the armour off.

It came away in plates. The gold had never been fitted to him in the ordinary sense, and it fell off the shoulders and the chest and the thighs and struck the flags in a sequence of flat heavy sounds, and underneath it was a fifty-six-year-old man in a torn shirt with the shoulders and the back of a lifetime and a ruined lead leg going purple from hip to knee.

He was not being theatrical.

*Nobody has ever thrown a proper round kick in plate. You cannot get the hip through. You cannot use the shin, you cannot turn on the ball of the foot, you cannot feel the floor, and every strike a man throws in armour is thrown from the shoulder, which is the weakest structure in the body for the purpose.*

*And I need to feel him.*

The strands came up on his frame.

Nothing like the tessellation. Something older and looser, laid along the arms and across the deltoids and down both flanks and around the ruined thigh: the Plane itself, standing on him in cords, following the lines of the muscle underneath as though the muscle were what it had been drawn to describe.

The Fist of God. Kami-no-Kobushi. Gimbzo configured it and Gimbzo had never given it a name in Darius's hearing, and had spent four of the nine months on it, and had said one sentence about it at the end of the fourth month and then never again.

*The seven Works go into the world. That is what everybody does with them and it is what they are for and it is why the Order has an apparatus. The Fist puts them in the body instead. Ruin in the hands. Purity in the skin. Order in the stance. Spirit at the point of contact.*

*It is worth nothing at range. It has no reach and no display and it cannot be aimed at a city.*

*It only works if you are touching him. It has no Wellspring line and no Family and Gimbzo would not give it a Physics Domain, and I asked twice and got the same answer both times, which was that a thing worked entirely inside Corporis does not need the Codex to tell it where it lives.*

He got up on the dead leg and put it forward.

Orthodox. Left foot lead, right hand rear, rear heel lifted, hands at the cheekbone, chin down, elbows in against the ribs that were already gone.

*The lead leg is the one that takes the kicks and mine is finished. Every fighter alive would switch. I would tell a student to switch. Southpaw, put the dead leg in the rear where it carries nothing and takes nothing, and buy yourself another minute.*

*A minute is not what I need.*

*I need him to stop being able to leave, and a man in southpaw is a man who is planning to retreat, and I have put the ruined leg in front because I have stopped planning to retreat, and I would like him to work that out.*
He went forward.

And the thing he had understood in the last four seconds, which was the whole of the fight and which Aurelian could not use and never would be able to, went with him.

*He has taken the future away from you.*

*He has made it so there is no privileged next. You can look ahead and see eleven things and none of them has authority, and the Primate has been reading forward all morning and has just discovered that reading forward is now an entertainment.*

*So stop reading forward.*

*A man throwing a knee has to load his hips before the knee exists. A man throwing an elbow drops his shoulder first. There is no version of any successor he selects in which the body does not have to arrange itself to deliver it, and the arrangement happens in the present tense, and the present is the one place his interval does not reach.*

*He cannot be predicted. He can be felt.*

*Proprioception does not read the future. It reads load. A hip loading is a present-tense mechanical fact occurring in a body, and it occurs before the strike whether or not the strike has been selected yet, because a body that has not loaded cannot throw and no amount of authority over successors changes what a femur has to do to make a knee arrive.*

*Spirit reads traffic. Contact is traffic. That is the only reason Gimbzo built the thing.*

*So get inside and put a hand on him and never take it off.*

He crossed the last four feet at the pace the dead leg allowed, which was slow, and the slowness was information he was handing over on purpose.

He threw the right.

Rear hand, straight, from orthodox, with Ruin in it. That was the point and it was why the fake would work: Ruin finds the ending already on its way and makes it arrive correctly, and a right hand carrying the first Fist is not a punch anybody in four worlds is permitted to ignore. It has to be answered. A man can slip a fist. Nobody slips an ending.

He let it go at ninety percent of the distance and sold the hip and the shoulder and the eyes, and he did not follow it.

His weight was already going the other way.

The low kick came off the rear leg, which was the good one, and it went down and out on the shortest arc it had, shin into the outside of the lead thigh, aimed at the same handspan of nerve that had been used on him four times in the last minute. The rotation came out of the hip and the plant foot pivoted on the ball and the whole body turned through the strike, which is the only way that kick has ever been thrown and the only way it will ever work.

*Check it and you break my shin on your knee. Take it and you get what I have. Move and you have to move somewhere.*

And in the instant the shin arrived at the place where the leg should have been, and the leg was not there, Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum did the thing he had spent four months in an ash pit learning.

He did not stop the kick.

A round kick that misses does not stop. It carries. The hip is already open and the mass is already travelling and the plant foot is already turned, and every instructor in every tradition on the Material teaches a man to recover from that and eat the lost tempo, and every one of them is teaching a man to waste a rotation he has already paid for.

He let it take him round.

The rear leg came through and past and down and became the lead, and the spin came off the missed kick as one continuous piece of momentum, and his back turned and kept turning and the strands on his frame went taut across the shoulders, and out of the far side of the revolution his left hand came up open.

A fist at the end of a spin is a fist thrown by a man with no control of his own arc, and it lands with the wrist collapsing.

An open palm, heel forward, fingers back, the strongest striking surface a hand has and the only one that does not care whether the elbow arrives at the correct angle.

He put it at the throat.

Above the gorget line and below the jaw, into the soft triangle where the larynx and the hyoid sit under nothing at all, on a body that had armoured every plate and every joint and every load path and had left that gap because everything with a neck leaves that gap, and there is no armourer in four worlds who has ever solved it.

And the whole of the Fist went into the palm on the way in. Ruin in the hand, to find whatever ending was already running in that throat. Purity in the skin, to take the permission to refuse out of whatever it found. Order in the arm, so that the road between his palm and the target stayed continuous no matter what got moved. Spirit at the point of contact, reaching for the rhythm.

*And when it lands I do not take it off.*

*Whatever comes next, whatever successor gets promoted out of that interval, it gets promoted with my hand already on him.*

The strands across his shoulders had gone from cords to something continuous, and the Attraction Force running in them was the last of what the Primate had put into him and there would be no more.

Darius came out of the revolution with his weight over the dead leg and the palm arriving at the throat, and did not close his eyes.

Haruki's palms extended out, the drill was caught before it could fully puncture her. Her hands wrapped around it as the Saishiki directed toward her palms. The grace and edict lessening the blow, but not enough to save the wound already. The drill dug through, the blood spilt onto the floor, her hands clutched against it as it continued to rotate. 

The explosion was contained, the grace keeping it's violent and erratic behavior on check as the energy evenly distributed outward. The energy from it easing and shrinking as it laid up against her hands. The harmony and Saishiki stabilizing the figure as her footing anchored herself from fully being launched back.

She was pushed behind both Aurelian and Darius, but not too far enough that she was outside the battlefield. Her hands clenched down on it as the void energy shrunk down,  the figure and form shifting, stabilizing as she pressed down. The benediction hit as the flow caused it to reduce dramatically.

The drill was too small now, the energy from it dissipated, but in return the wound on Haruki was fresh and exposed. Her hands shifted as she rested her palm up against where Kujo had attacked her. The essence sprinkling onto the wound as the flesh began to shift softly."

"Troublesome..." She muttered to herself, her eyes directing over to Darius and Kujo now, her frame shifting as she adjusted the parunes of grace and edict over herself. Her hand shifted as she used her blood to form the symbols on her palm, her stance shifting as the field assumed around her. Her hope flowed into her chest as the light began to shine brightly, vibrantly.

"Paru Number Sixteen, Dawn Anchor." Her luminalis shined through as the light blessed them with a graceful and yet renewing warmth. Darius and Aurelian both felt it, the warmth, the hope vitalizing their hearts and clearing their minds from the touches and turmoils of what the void has taxed on them. The resonance between them growing stronger, the unity no longer requiring-demand, but rather allowing the expressions to align and shape into the figure formed between them. Haruki remained there, stationary and adjusted. The parune of **Dawn** appeared etched as she focused herself on recovering and supporting her allies.


Kujo watched the armor fall away and understood immediately that Darius had stopped trying to win the same battle. The golden eyes tracked the strands forming along muscle rather than armor, reading Ruin in the hands, Purity through the skin, Order through the stance, and Spirit waiting at the point where contact became traffic. There was almost nothing external for Null to cancel, no elaborate manifestation for the Crevice to dismantle, and no distant working that could be robbed of a destination. Kujo smiled because, finally, Darius had brought him something built for touching.

The ruined lead leg went forward, and Kujo’s smile widened a fraction. He understood the declaration contained in that choice before Darius ever moved: there would be no retreat, no switch of stance, and no attempt to purchase another minute by protecting what had already been damaged. The body standing opposite him had become a commitment rather than a strategy. Kujo respected that considerably more than he respected the Hammer.

Then he noticed what Darius had actually learned.

The man had stopped trying to predict him.

Darius was reading present-tense preparation instead, watching load move through hips, shoulders, knees, spine, and planted feet before any successor could be promoted from Void Interval into actuality. That reasoning was correct for nearly every fighting body Kujo had ever inhabited, because even a future freed from linear sequence still required a present capable of producing it. The golden eyes narrowed as Kujo realized Darius had found the first genuinely useful answer to his temporal state.

“Good.”

Kujo’s hands rose into a loose Muay Thai guard, elbows narrow and weight resting lightly through both feet. “You stopped looking for tomorrow and started looking at my hips.” The Crevice beneath his sternum gave one slow pulse. “That was the correct lesson.”

Then Kujo changed the lesson.

He did not conceal his mechanical loading, accelerate it beyond perception, or remove preparation from bodily movement. Instead, every major kinetic chain in his compact frame loaded simultaneously, opposing muscle groups tightening into several equally valid launch configurations while the Crevice held their contradictory vectors apart before release. His right hip carried the preparation for a round kick, knee, and cross at once, while the left shoulder held elbow, hook, frame, and defensive shell without any one of them possessing priority. Proprioception could read every load correctly and still receive no answer about which one would become motion.

This was the bodily counterpart to Void Interval.

Kujo called it Dead Preparation, though he spoke no technique name aloud. The body performed all necessary present-tense preparation before the future was selected, and the Crevice isolated the competing mechanical commitments so they did not cancel one another prematurely. When Kujo chose a successor, every unused load simply relaxed while one already-prepared kinetic chain was permitted to complete.

“You found the flaw in prediction.”

His shoulders remained loaded in several contradictory directions simultaneously. “Now find the flaw in preparation.” The golden eyes followed Darius without seeking the future at all. “Everything you feel is true.”

Darius’s right hand came first.

Kujo saw Ruin carried in it and treated the fist seriously despite knowing the extension would never finish. His own present-facing eyes read shoulder rotation, weight transfer, and the structural line of the strike while Dead Preparation kept several defensive responses equally loaded. When the right hand died at ninety percent and the weight reversed, Kujo did not have to correct from a committed defense because he had never selected one.

The rear low kick followed.

Kujo’s lead leg was already gone from the line by the time the shin reached the space it had occupied, and the missed strike began carrying Darius through the rotation he had deliberately accepted. Kujo did not chase the back or attack during the turn because the golden eyes recognized that the rotation itself was the weapon. He watched the open palm emerge from the far side of it and finally understood the shape of the trap.

Ruin sat in the hand.

Purity sat in the skin.

Order ran through the arm.

Spirit waited for contact.

Kujo’s head tilted slightly as the palm came toward the soft triangle beneath his jaw. The higher Crevice-form had retained something resembling a throat because anatomical familiarity made movement efficient, but speech and respiration no longer depended exclusively upon the laryngeal architecture visible there. The Crevice distributed vocal resonance through the chest cavity and black mantle, while his altered respiratory exchange occurred through several internal channels instead of one fragile human passage. Darius had aimed perfectly at the throat of a man whose throat had stopped being indispensable.

Kujo did not move it anyway.

That was the part Darius had not expected.

The palm was permitted to continue toward its selected target while Kujo’s left forearm rose inside the attacking arm rather than across the hand, wedging beneath the elbow and making contact near the triceps before the heel of the palm could seat cleanly. His right hand simultaneously closed around the attacking wrist from outside, not trying to stop Order’s continuous road but adding a second geometry to the limb already committed to following it. Kujo accepted the road and began bending the vehicle traveling along it.

“Order.”

His left elbow elevated while his right hand rotated the captured wrist outward. “You made the arm insist upon reaching me.” Kujo’s hips settled underneath the trapped structure. “So I will not argue.”

He turned.

The manipulation was brutally ordinary: wrist rotation, elbow elevation, shoulder isolation, and a step that placed Kujo inside the arc rather than outside it. If Order insisted that the palm’s road remain continuous toward the throat, then the arm had to keep traveling while its joints were being placed into an increasingly hostile alignment. Kujo did not break Darius’s continuity; he made continuity carry the limb deeper into its own mechanical disadvantage.

Purity reached his skin wherever contact occurred.

Kujo gave it nothing to remove because he did not invoke refusal, resistance, negation, or a Parunic defense against the touch. The surface of his body accepted contact exactly as physics demanded while the internal Crevice treated incoming mechanical consequence as another collapse-route already native to his architecture. Purity could strip away permission to deny the impact, but Kujo was not denying it.

Ruin searched for an ending already underway within the struck throat.

It found bruisable structure, vulnerable tissue, and several functions that could indeed be damaged, but it did not find the singular catastrophic ending expected from collapsing an ordinary airway because Kujo’s current architecture had distributed those functions elsewhere. The strike could still hurt if it landed correctly. It simply no longer carried the automatic anatomical conclusion Darius had selected.

Spirit was the dangerous part.

The instant physical contact became traffic, Kujo allowed it to read him without falsifying a single signal. What came through the contact was the entire distributed load of Dead Preparation: hip rotation that could become a knee, shoulder tension that could become an elbow, planted pressure that could become a sweep, spinal torque that could become a throw, and beneath all of them the slow Crevice rhythm holding mutually valid preparations apart. Spirit received excellent information and too much of it.

“You were right again.”

Kujo’s golden eyes brightened as his grip maintained the arm trap. “Contact is traffic.” Several prepared kinetic chains remained equally real beneath his skin. “You assumed traffic had one destination.”

Then Kujo selected one.

His left forearm stopped being merely a wedge and became a short horizontal elbow driven across the inside line of Darius’s upper arm, aimed where concentrated impact could compromise the mechanics supporting the trapped limb without requiring a wide swing. Kujo’s right hand retained wrist control only long enough to guide the structure through the strike, then released before contact could become a sustained Fate-relevant bind. The movement was compact, vicious, and unmistakably Muay Thai.

The next successor had already been loaded before the elbow existed.

Kujo’s right knee came upward from the clinch distance toward the injured side of Darius’s ribs, hips driving forward while his posture remained tall enough to prevent the motion from becoming a wrestling exchange. He did not attack the head because the body had already provided a better ledger: damaged ribs, pulmonary injury, failing breath, and a reservoir being spent through a vessel struggling to retain it. The knee was intended to make every future breath cost more.

Kujo planted directly from that motion and turned his hips again.

A low kick whipped toward the already-ruined lead thigh, but this time he did not attack the outer nerve line he had damaged before. His shin traveled inward toward the adductor and supporting structures closer to the inside of the leg, attacking what remained capable of stabilizing the limb after the lateral structures had already been compromised. Darius had deliberately left the dead leg in front.

Kujo intended to make that decision expensive.

“You put the damaged leg where I could reach it.”

His rear heel lifted again as the kick completed its arc. “Not because you believed it could survive.” The golden eyes remained fixed upon Darius’s present body rather than his future. “Because you wanted me close enough to touch.”

Kujo’s stance compressed.

“So touch me.”

Dead Preparation spread through him again.

Both hips loaded without choosing direction, both shoulders held multiple striking lines, and his stance carried simultaneous readiness to check, knee, elbow, teep, pivot, or clinch without telegraphing which preparation had priority. Darius could place a hand upon Kujo and read every ounce of it through Spirit. The information would remain completely honest.

That honesty was the weapon.

Kujo no longer needed to hide what his body could do because the Crevice had changed the relationship between preparation and choice. Proprioception could tell Darius exactly which attacks Kujo was mechanically ready to perform, but every answer would remain valid until the instant Kujo selected one. By then, prediction had already become reaction again.

The golden eyes slowly narrowed.

“You solved precognition.”

His fists remained loose beside his face. “You solved my movement.” The Crevice pulsed between them. “Now solve certainty.”

The palm was a lie.

It had been a lie since the moment he committed to the spin, and the spin had been honest and the kick had been honest and the right hand had been honest, and the palm at the end of it was the only thing in the sequence Darius had never intended to land.

*Punta. The point that is offered so the hands will come up to meet it.*

*The Order's third-year manual calls it a false thrust and gives it four lines and every man who reads those four lines assumes it belongs to a sword. It belongs to whatever is at the end of your arm. You show the opponent a thing worth answering, and the answer is what you actually came for, and the whole art is in making the offer expensive enough to be believed.*

*A palm at the throat from a man carrying Ruin is expensive enough.*

The wrist got caught.

Darius felt the trap close and let it, because a limb that has been taken is a limb whose position both men now agree on, and agreement is the one thing he had come into that range to buy.

Then he dropped.

Nothing about it resembled a step or a duck. He arced the whole of his upper body forward and down over the trapped arm, spine rolling from the neck through the thoracic into the lumbar, and as he went he turned the hips through underneath, and the rotation and the descent became one motion in the way they only ever do when a man has thrown the strike enough times that his body no longer consults him about the order.

The elbow came down and across on the diagonal.

Sok chieng, in the register the Thai keep it in, and the Order's manual has it under a different name and a worse drawing. It is the strike you throw when your hand is already committed and your hand is useless, and the point of an elbow is the olecranon, which is a lever arm terminating in a spur of bone with no soft tissue over it anywhere, and it is the single hardest striking surface a human body owns.

He put it into the ribcage.

Between the plates. Over the floating ribs on the left where the armoured structure has to leave a gap because a frame that bends must leave a gap there, and the gap is a hand's width, and the whole of a fifty-six-year-old man's descending mass went through a bone spur into it.

Something gave.

Ribs eleven and twelve are called floating because they attach to nothing in front. They have a costovertebral joint at the spine and free ends at the other, and there is no sternum for the load to distribute into, so everything arriving there goes through the bone and into what the bone is covering, and on that side what the bone is covering is a spleen or whatever an anatomy that size keeps in the same place.

Darius felt it through his own arm before he heard anything, which is how you know a strike landed instead of merely arriving: the feedback comes back up the limb as a change in the character of the resistance, and the resistance changed.

He was reading the whole time, and the reading came up the arm as Attraction and never once through the eyes, which is the only reason it worked at all. Spirit is the Third Work and the Third Work is traffic, and traffic is a present-tense quantity and it does not care what the future has been permitted to do.

That was the part he could not have explained to anybody at the Order and had stopped trying to explain to himself. He had his hand on the man. Contact is traffic, and Spirit sat at the point of contact and read it, and every load in that body arrived up Darius's own arm as a present-tense mechanical fact: the right hip rotated and holding, the left shoulder set, the rear heel light, the hamstring loaded on the side that had not yet moved.

He was not fighting the future. He had given the future up in the ash at Sum-gol and had come here to fight in the moment.

He was fighting against certainty.

And that was the wrong way round, and he knew it, and it was the only advantage he had.

*I am the certainty in this crater.*

*Purity does not modulate. It is a property of a Lattice and it has no dial and I cannot make it uncertain if I want to. Order insists that a road which was there is still there, and it does not consult anybody about it. Every single thing I am is a structural fact.*

*He has made himself into a question. I have been an answer since I was thirty-five years old and I have hated it every day and I am about to find out what it is for.*


Then the loads all came up true at once.

Darius felt it through the palm still resting on the arm and stopped, internally, for the length of about a third of a second, because the reading had been clean and correct all the way through the elbow and now it said everything.

Right hip: round kick. And knee. And cross. All three, loaded, all three real.

Left shoulder: elbow, hook, frame, shell. Four. None of them subordinate.

Both feet: check, teep, pivot, step.

*Every one of those is true. I am not being deceived. There is no false load in that body and no muscle lying to me, and if I answer any one of them I will be answering a thing that was genuinely there and was genuinely not going to happen.*

*He has hidden nothing. He has made preparation stop meaning anything.*

*And I have exactly one thought about it and it had better be correct.*

It arrived out of the fourth month in the ash, in Gimbzo's flat voice, on a day Darius had spent face down in the grey with two cracked ribs and no idea what he had done wrong.

*A man who is ready for everything is throwing nothing.*

He almost laughed, and the laugh would have cost him a lung he could not spare, so it came out as a change in the shape of his mouth and nothing else.

*Co-contraction. That is all this is at the level where a body has to obey. He is holding agonist against antagonist across six chains at once, and a joint braced by opposing muscle is a stiff joint, and a stiff joint accelerates slowly and delivers less. It is the first thing anybody learns about power generation and it is the reason a tense fighter hits like a child.*

*Whatever he selects out of that, it comes out slower than it would have and it lands softer than it could have, because he has spent the tension on being ready and tension spent on readiness is tension that is not available to the strike.*

*He is paying for the state. He has been paying since he entered it. And he has not told anybody, including himself.*

*The Crevice is holding those contradictory vectors apart. That is not free either. Whatever the entry for that would say if anybody had ever written one, it would have a Cost line, and every entry in the sheet has a Cost line, and the ones that look like they do not are the ones where nobody survived to file it.*

Which was worth something and was not enough on its own, because a strike from that body at half force still ended people.

So Darius took the second half of the answer, and the second half was older than the ash and older than the Order and belonged to every wrestler who had ever lived.

*You do not have to know which one is coming.*

*You have to make most of them impossible.*

He stepped in.

Straight up the middle into the space the elbow had opened, on the dead leg, and it did not push and did not need to, because he was falling forward and had arranged to be.

A round kick needs room. An elbow needs an arc. A teep needs a foot of distance to extend into and a knee needs a hip that can travel and a pivot needs somewhere to pivot to.

He took the room.

Chest to chest. His forehead went into the sternum notch above the Crevice and stayed there, because a head is a post and a post occupying that place makes it very difficult for a man to get his hips under himself, and Darius put his weight on it and drove.

His left hand came off the trapped arm and went under. Underhook, deep, the forearm running up the inside of the arm and the hand closing at the back of the shoulder, and the underhook does one thing above all others: it takes the hip on that side away.

His right hand came across to the collar and pulled down.

No Thai plum, because a plum needs both hands and both hands was more grip than he could afford to have committed against a thing that could still elbow. One collar tie, one underhook, over and under, the oldest structure in wrestling and the one Gimbzo had put him in eleven times in the fifth month without once explaining why.

He knew why now, and the knowing arrived with the flat unwelcome clarity of every other thing the old man had put into him without explanation.

*He drilled the over-under against a partner who was allowed to attack from any position, eleven times, and would not tell me the point, and I asked on the ninth and he said HOO and nothing else. It is not a wrestling tie. It is a subtraction. It is the same shape as my own Trait and he built it into my hands before he ever taught me the seventh, so that the first thing I would reach for under pressure was the thing that removes options.*

Of the eleven loads Darius had felt come up true, the over-under killed seven outright. The round kick had no room. The teep had no distance. The pivot had nowhere.
