The cross had no arc and the hook had no arc and the check had nothing to check. What was left was a knee, an elbow on the free side, and a frame, and three is a number a man can defend against.

*Certainty is still standing.*

*I have made the question smaller. That is what a body is for.*

And the last piece went in with the underhook, and it was the piece nobody in that crater but Gimbzo would have understood.

Order went into the clinch.

It went nowhere near the ground or the field or the roads. Into the four points of contact between two bodies: the forehead on the sternum, the underhook at the shoulder, the collar tie at the neck, and the hip pressed against the hip. The Fourth Work insists that a thing which was there is still there, and Darius stopped insisting it about geometry and started insisting it about the tie.

*The contact stays. Whatever successor you promote, whatever you select out of that interval, you select it with my forehead on your sternum and my hand behind your shoulder, because those four points were there and Order says they remain.*

*You can move. You cannot leave.*

*And Purity is in the skin at all four points, which means nothing you put on me at those four points will find purchase, and there is no coupling available across the tie in either direction. You cannot bind me through it and you cannot drain me through it and you cannot reach anything of mine through it.*

*It is a wall with a man on each side of it and I am the wall.*

He drove the underhook up and rotated his own shoulder into the joint and felt the whole architecture on that side lose the ability to load, and the round kick that had been sitting in that hip for four seconds relaxed out of it and went nowhere.

A knee from the clinch is not a kick and has no arc and needs no distance. The hip travels perhaps eight inches and the whole mass of the trunk goes with it through a joint that is already the hardest thing in the lower body, and the reason it is the Thai national answer to everything is that it works at a range where nothing else fits.

Then he brought his own knee up, short, off the rear leg, into the same gap over the floating ribs that the elbow had already opened.

And the second one after it, before the ribs had finished reporting the first.

And a third, into the same handspan, because repetition into one location is the only thing in unarmed fighting that has ever reliably ended anybody. A body distributes an injury it receives once. It cannot distribute the same injury four times in six seconds, and the tissue between the second and the fourth stops behaving like tissue and starts behaving like a material with a fatigue limit, which is what it always was.

No single enormous thing anywhere in it. Nobody had ever beaten anybody with one enormous thing and Darius had spent twenty-one years writing that sentence into an Order that would not read it. Short knees from the clinch, into an existing injury, on a body that had to spend co-contraction holding six loads it was no longer able to use, over and over, in the same handspan, while a fifty-six-year-old man with a dead leg and a torn lung held four points of contact closed by a law that does not negotiate.

The strands on his frame had gone white at the shoulders, and the last of what the Primate had put into him was going out through four points of contact into a working that had no Wellspring and no Family and had never been given a Physics Domain, because Gimbzo would not assign one to a thing that lived entirely in Corporis.

*Solve certainty, he says.*

*I am not going to solve it. I am going to stand here with my head on his chest until the arithmetic finishes.*

There was blood in his mouth again and it had come up on its own and he swallowed it, because spitting costs a breath and he had a countable number of those left and had started counting them somewhere around the second knee.

Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum shortened the collar tie by four inches, turned his hips over, and brought the knee up a fourth time.

Kujo made the mistake because, for the first time since the battle had begun, he believed he had accounted for every meaningful variable on the field. Darius was directly in front of him, Aurelian remained buried within the architecture of his own impossible working, and Haruki had already been torn out of the fight by Gyre Absolone. The golden eyes across Kujo’s higher form tracked fighters, glyphs, continuities, preparations, future branches, and the instant those branches became present. Not one of them looked toward the child still kneeling beside Dawn Guard.

Asante had not moved.

The crimson-black Obsession Force that had previously filled streets with murderous pressure had gone unnaturally quiet around him, its enormous violence condensed until there was almost nothing visible except thin strands crawling across his fingers and the body he refused to release. His face remained wet against Zuberi, but the eyes staring past the corpse were no longer the eyes Kujo had seen before the Sovereign died. The Origin Eye was open.

And Origin did not begin by looking at what something was.

It looked at where something had started.

Asante saw the Essence circulating through Darius and did not perceive it as a reservoir first; he saw the first moment Aether had crossed inward, accepted the man’s identity, and become Darius rather than environment. He saw the office-power running through him, the residue of Aurelian’s transfer, the older personal Essence beneath it, and the exact recognition by which all of those currents were permitted to occupy one living architecture. The Origin Eye reduced an impossible adult system to beginnings simple enough for a child to touch.

A thread left Darius.

It was not torn from him by brute Dominion, nor did Asante attempt to capture his entire reserve and shatter the man’s Crystal through extraction. He reached through the first recognition at which circulating Aether had become personalized Essence and called a portion backward along that recognition, separating it from its current circulation without erasing where it had come from. A pale-gold current slipped through the battlefield and curled above Asante’s shoulder.

Then the Origin Eye found Aurelian.

The Primate’s Essence was harder to distinguish because so much of him had become office, Scale, Turgia, burden, and law, but those things had beginnings too. Asante saw the first internal acceptance beneath the accumulated ritual architecture, the place where Aurelian’s own Essence still answered mine before becoming fuel for anything greater. Another current left him, brighter and denser, and joined Darius’s without mixing.

Asante did not absorb either one.

That would have killed him.

Instead, the currents revolved outside his body in separate streams, each retaining its original Essence Signature while the Origin Eye held only their first common truth: all of them had once been Aether before someone claimed them. Asante had found a point of compatibility older than personality.

His gaze lowered to Dawn Guard.

That one almost broke him again.

Zuberi’s dying body still carried Essence through tissues that had not yet understood they belonged to a dead or dying sovereign, remnants of a circulation built over a lifetime of battle, rulership, vows, and impossible persistence. Asante saw where that Essence had first become Zuberi’s, and for one trembling second his hand stopped because taking even a fraction felt like stealing from the person he was begging not to leave. Then the Origin Eye saw something else beneath the grief.

The Essence was already leaving.

So Asante caught it.

Gold-white Essence rose from Dawn Guard’s body in a thin stream before dissipation could return it fully to ambient Aether, and Asante’s fingers curled around empty air as though desperately catching water through his hands. He did not consume it, overwrite it, or call it his. He simply refused to let that last portion disappear before it could matter.

Haruki came next.

Her Essence carried Saishiki, judgment, Grace, light, and the aftermath of everything she had spent trying to sentence Kujo, but Origin did not care about the names that had accumulated afterward. It found the beginning underneath them and drew out another measured portion. White-gold light curled into the growing constellation around Asante.

Four signatures revolved around him.

Then the Origin Eye finally looked at Kujo.

For the first time, Asante’s expression changed.

Kujo did not present the clean architecture the others had.

Void, Crevice, old Aether, stolen structures, fused law, the remaining consequences of Valmir, transformations, and the strange personal economy sustaining The Unwinding made his current existence look like a civilization layered upon ruins layered upon another civilization. But the Origin Eye did not attempt to understand Kujo from the top downward. It looked for the first point at which the power currently circulating through him had become available to the present Kujo.

And it found one. Several. Then hundreds.

The golden eyes across Kujo’s body suddenly moved.

Too late.

A crimson-black thread thinner than hair had already reached the Crevice.

Kujo felt the change first as a fractional weakness in one shoulder, so small that an ordinary fighter would have mistaken it for fatigue. Then another portion of his active Essence disappeared, followed by another, and the loss accelerated because Asante was not draining through the pathways Kujo had spent the battle defending. The Origin Eye was reaching for the beginning of ownership and withdrawing from there.

Kujo’s head snapped toward him.

Asante was still holding Zuberi.

*“You.”*

That was all Kujo managed.


The golden eyes exploded into motion, trying to resolve the mechanism, but Essence continued peeling out of him in black-gold strands and joining the orbit surrounding the child. Kujo attempted to collapse the relation with Void, only to discover that Asante was not maintaining one continuous conduit that could be severed. Each extraction was a separate return to origin, completed almost as soon as the Eye identified it.

Kujo lifted one hand. The hand stopped halfway. His right side vanished. There was no explosion.

No spray. No conventional severance line.

One instant Kujo possessed the right half of his torso, shoulder, arm, portions of the mantle, hip, and leg; the next, those structures ceased occupying the Material position they had held. Half of the seven-foot-four form simply disappeared into a brutally clean vertical absence, exposing a cross-section of black-gold internal architecture that should have immediately begun correcting itself.

It did not. Kujo’s remaining golden eyes widened.

The Unwinding reached backward for the completed event and found it perfectly.

It understood exactly what had happened.

It knew the sequence had completed. But recognition was not execution.

There was no usable Aether behind the command.

Asante had taken the available Essence Kujo needed to turn personal temporal authority into present correction, leaving The Unwinding aware of the wound without enough active medium to enforce its refusal. For the first time in the battle, Kujo could see a completed consequence he wanted gone and could do nothing about it.

His remaining leg shifted violently to preserve balance.

The Crevice pulsed weakly. Nothing returned.

Kujo looked down at the missing half of himself, then toward Asante, and there was no smile on what remained of his face. He had spent the entire engagement explaining the distinction between knowledge and authority to everyone around him. Now his own golden eyes possessed perfect knowledge of the past and insufficient power to exercise authority over it.

Darius had an opening. A real one.

No displaced road protected Kujo, no fresh Void topology waited to move an endpoint, no Unwinding stood ready to erase a completed strike afterward, and no overwhelming Aetheric reserve remained available to brute-force a transition. For several heartbeats, the Void Regent existed as exactly what every fighter had spent the entire battle trying to produce: a target that consequences could keep.

But Asante did not look toward Darius.

He looked only at Kujo.

The five stolen Essence signatures revolved around him, Darius, Aurelian, Zuberi, Haruki, and Kujo, each violently incompatible if treated according to what it had become. Origin looked beneath that problem and found what they had all been before bloodline, office, Wellspring, morality, hatred, Trait, or Name differentiated them. They had all begun as Aether accepted by someone.

Asante brought his hands together.

The currents screamed.

Gold from Darius tightened against Aurelian’s brilliant judicial Essence, Haruki’s white radiance pushed against Kujo’s black-gold Crevice current, and Zuberi’s remaining sovereign Essence ran through the center like a spine. Asante did not harmonize their mature signatures because their mature signatures had no reason to agree. The Origin Eye forced each current to express from the instant before disagreement became part of its identity.

For one impossible moment, five people’s Essence became compatible without becoming identical.

Asante’s Obsession Force wrapped around the outside.

Not inside.

He did not contaminate the gathered power with his hatred; he used the crimson-black force as a vessel, a shell of refusal wrapped around Essence that would otherwise tear itself apart. The attack began forming above his hands as a dense sphere whose surface could not decide whether it was gold, white, black, violet, crimson, or complete colorlessness.

It grew. Then compressed. Again. Again.

Until the entire gathered reservoir occupied something barely wider than Asante’s palm.

The Origin Eye bled.

Thin red trails ran beneath both eyes, but Asante never blinked.

He looked down once at Zuberi’s body. His mouth trembled. Then Asante carefully lowered him to the ground.

He stood.

The child who had spent the battle crying beside a corpse now rose beneath currents stolen from some of the strongest beings in the crater, his small frame surrounded by pressure it should never have been capable of containing. Yet none of that pressure actually entered him. The Origin Eye kept the attack outside his architecture, assembled from beginnings while Asante served only as the one deciding what those beginnings would become.

Kujo finally understood what he had ignored. Not Obsession. Not rage. Not the child.

Origin.

Asante raised the compressed sphere. The High Vaeloris came quietly at first.

“Daor va Rȳbās.”

The words carried through the crater. Asante’s voice cracked.

Then his grief caught up with it.

**“DAOR VA RȲBĀS!”**

**Down with the Regent.**

The sphere unfolded. Not outward. Forward.

Five Essence signatures collapsed into a single directed column as the Origin Eye formulated their shared beginning into one attack, while crimson-black Obsession gave that attack only one refusal: Kujo would not be permitted to stand untouched at the end of it. The blast tore away from Asante with the accumulated force of everyone Kujo had spent the battle fighting, including the very Essence Kujo had expected to use to defend himself.

And at its center was Dawn Guard.

Not his body.bNot his soul. The last Essence his dying body had been able to give.

Asante screamed as the attack left his hands.

**“DAOR VA RȲBĀS!”**

The crater became light.

For the first time since Kujo had declared himself the end, the power advancing toward him contained a portion of himself, and he did not have enough left to tell the past that it no longer mattered.

The fourth knee never landed.

Darius felt the tie go slack under his hands and understood two things in the same quarter-second, and the first was that something had come out of him and the second was that it had not been taken by the man he was holding.

It went out through the middle. Nothing crossed the four points, because nothing ever crosses those four points in either direction and nothing ever will, and this had gone from somewhere further in than the skin. He felt it leave the way a man feels a room go quiet behind him.

*That is my Essence and it has been called and I did not feel a hand on it.*

*It did not go out through a conduit. Something reached the place where circulating Aether first agreed to be me and asked for a portion back along that agreement, and the agreement is older than the Trait and the Trait has no opinion about it, because Purity subtracts coupling and that was never a coupling. That was an acceptance.*

*Nothing in the Order has a defence for that. Nothing in the Codex has a row for it, and I have read all one hundred and nine.*

He let go of the collar tie and got his weight off the post and came out backward on the dead leg, and the leg took it and did not fold, which surprised him. The shin had overloaded the common peroneal without severing it, and an overloaded nerve trunk returns in stages if it is left alone, and his had been left alone for about forty seconds. It would carry him. It would not carry him twice.

The half of the thing in front of him that had been on the right was gone.

Cleanly. A vertical absence from the shoulder through the hip with a cross-section of black-gold internal architecture standing open along the cut, and it was not closing, and Darius had spent an hour watching that body discard and replace and restore and correct, and it was not correcting.

*He knows. He can see the completed event and he cannot afford it.*

*Knowledge and authority. He has spent the entire morning explaining the difference to everybody in this crater and he has just found out what it feels like from the inside.*

Down the wreck, at the far end, there was a child on his feet with a compressed thing above his hands and five currents turning around him that had no business being compatible, and one of them was Darius's own.

He did not look at it for longer than he had to.

*Take it. Take all of it. I have never been able to keep anything anyway.*

He had a window, and it was not a long one, and he knew exactly what he was going to do with it because he had known since the gate and had been refusing to look at it.

He turned his palm up and reached for the seam.

The Pillar was no part of it. Aurelian's Pillar stood along the join and drew from it and had been drawing from it for six minutes, and the Primate did not know whose join it was and had never been told and was not going to be told now.

Darius knew.

He had felt the wall standing since he came through the gate. A Palatine of the Holy Sea had gone into a seam at the ninth hour and become the material of a Custodia and had been lifted out of the world by a hand, and the seal had gone on standing afterward, and there was a hairline fault running through it that had never closed.

*Verinus.*

*I met you four times. You were at the table when they put the chain on Charles and you signed the minute and you were the only man in that room who looked me in the face while he did it. I have thought about you perhaps eleven times in ten years and I have never once thought about you kindly, because you were the man who kept the Court's arithmetic and I have spent my life refusing to be that.*

*And you spent your last hour in the world declining to stop.*

*I am about to spend you.*

He mended it outward into his hand.

That was the only word for the operation and it was Aurelian's word rather than his, and Darius took it because he had none of his own. He did not draw the seam into himself. **He could not.** Nothing external integrates into his Lattice, not the office, not a district, not a wall, and twenty-one years of that had made him the loneliest instrument in four worlds.

So he did the other thing.

*A hand holds.*

*That is the whole office and I worked it out in a cold chapel at thirty-five and wrote it in a letter I never sent. It does not weigh and it does not choose and it does not pass anything. It closes on a thing and keeps that thing in place while somebody else does the work, and it is not required to be right.*

*I cannot receive you. I can hold you.*

The seam came up out of the join and into his palm, and it stayed itself, and it stayed a wall, and Darius Ignatius Sanctus Sanctorum closed his hand around a boundary made out of a dead man and did not take one ounce of it inside himself.

Aetheric Density around his right arm went past the point where a reading means anything, and it did it inside four seconds, and it did it in a crater where saturation had been falling since the Pillar closed because bleed had been declared impermissible. All of it was in his fist and none of it was in him.

It began to convulse.

He was, in the strict sense, holding a phase boundary in his fist. A surface across which two states meet and neither is permitted into the other, and the energy of such a surface is proportional to its area, and it wants to minimise that area the way every interface in the world wants to minimise it. Compressed into a hand it had an area it could not reduce and a tension it could not shed.

A Custodia is not built to be carried. It is built to stand along a join and be a fact, and a fact in a fist has nowhere to be, and the whole mass of it went into a beating that came up his forearm and into his shoulder and out through his ribs and made the torn lung report in a way it had not yet managed. The light off it was the wrong colour for anything and it was throwing no shadow.

"POWER YOUR ENERGY INTO ME."

He got it out at volume with the last of the breath he had been counting.

It was the wrong instruction and he knew it was the wrong instruction as it left him and there was no time to correct it. Nothing they sent would reach him. Purity would refuse it at the skin and it would run off him and ground in the flags.

Aurelian understood anyway.

The Primate was a true Mahuo of the true line with the organ, and the organ reaches Fate directly without waiting for a sentence, and he did not need the words. What Darius saw was the Scale under the street turn once, and the light that came across the crater did not go into Darius's body at any point.

It went into the wall in his hand.

Everything the Primate had left, which was very little and which was leaving a man nine inches into the flags who could not feel it going, poured into the seam and not into the man holding it. Down the crater, white came off the wreck and joined it, from somebody with a channel through her flank who should not have been able to give anything to anybody.

*I am the arm. I have always been the arm. Charles carried and could not spend and I spend and cannot carry, and it took the two of us being broken in exactly opposite directions for the office to work, and he is dead and I am the last of three, and the third one is in my fist.*

Totality came up.

The seventh Work is the six held at once and the Plane delivering the finding, and Gimbzo had spent the last of the nine months on it and had never once let Darius reach it, and had said, in the fourth month, one sentence about the Fist that Darius had not understood until this moment.

*It is worth nothing at range. It only works if you are touching him.*

He was going to be touching him.

*And the Fist has no Wellspring line and no Family and no Physics Domain, and Gimbzo would not give it one, and I asked twice. A thing worked entirely in Corporis does not need the Codex to tell it where it lives. I have spent twenty-one years assigning Physics Domains to other people's workings and the only one that has ever mattered to me is unfiled.*

Ruin went into the hand. Purity went into the skin. Order into the stance and Spirit at the point of contact, and then the three the Fist had never been permitted to carry, because a body cannot hold seven and Darius was not holding them in a body any more. Temporality went into the tie. Knowledge into the read. Balance last, which was his, which had come back to him in the ash the way a language comes back to a man who spoke it as a child.

And the seventh is the six agreeing to hold still long enough to be looked at.

His frame took the shape of it.

He could not have described what happened to him afterward and never tried. The strands stopped being strands. The gold went out of the filaments and into the whole of him and he stopped having an outline that a person could have drawn, and what was standing in that crater with a convulsing boundary in its right fist had a fifty-six-year-old man somewhere inside it holding on with both hands.

*I have one arc. The same as before. That is all a body ever gets.*

There was blood coming out of his nose and out of his left ear and the vision had gone at the sides and stayed gone, and the Vitality reading he took on himself came back in a class he had only ever written about.

He went forward on the dead leg.

The presence arrived before he did.

He felt it hit and felt what it hit, and it went into the threads. It went nowhere near the body, the plates, or the architecture standing open along the cut. Into the threads of him, at the Plane, the whole bundle of small unique things that a person is when the Fate stratum looks at them, and Totality did not cut one of them and did not intend to.

It weighed on them.

And the weight was the finding. Six Works held at once and the Plane returning a verdict about the object in front of them, and the verdict had a magnitude, and the magnitude pressed on every strand in that bundle at once and none of them could hold it and the whole of the thing began to go back.

The threads did not tear and Totality did not want them to. A bundle under uniform load across every strand at once behaves the way any tensioned array behaves, which is that it translates instead of failing, because failure requires a differential and there was no differential anywhere in the finding.

Back through the crater. Back through the burning quarter and the wreck and the cracked flags and the place where the road had been. Back toward the tear it had come out of, at the rate of a man walking, because Darius was walking and the boundary in his fist was doing the pushing and the boundary did not hurry.

*You are not going to be killed here. I understand that now and I should have understood it at the gate.*

*You cannot be killed by any of us. You have taken a Titan's organ into the relationship between yourself and the Void and there is no arrangement in four worlds that ends that, and every one of us has spent this morning finding an elegant way to lose to it.*

*So you are not going to be killed.*

*You are going to be put outside.*

Every step cracked the flags under the dead leg and the cracking ran ahead of him in a line, and behind him the whole quarter had gone quiet in the way a room goes quiet when the thing that has been making all the noise has been carried out of it.

He drove it through the tear and kept walking until his arm was through to the shoulder.

The cold on the far side of the tear went through the sleeve and into the skin of the forearm and stopped there, at the boundary of him, because it could not couple and nothing ever has. He noticed it and had the strength to be grateful and no strength to say so.

Then he set the wall.

It went into the join with the whole of the Primate's last output and the white from the wreck behind it, and it closed, and it closed as a Custodia closes, which is to say it did not become a door with a lock on it. It became a fact. A boundary of Totality, made out of a man who declined to stop, standing across the place where the Void gives onto the Material, with the entire finding of the seven Works standing in it as its authorisation.

The Judger's own boundary, and the thing on the far side of it put outside the world it had come to edit, and the perpetual nothing on the other side of the seal to keep it company.

It went in along the whole length of the join at once, and Coagulatio settled it at the level of the bond, which is the level where matter agrees to remain matter, and the agreement was made permanent because that is the only kind of agreement Coagulatio has ever made.

The hairline fault was still in it.

Darius saw that as it closed and had no capacity left to do anything about it, and he entered it, in the small clear place at the back of his head that noticed things and wrote them down and had never once stopped operating in fifty-six years.

Then he took his arm out of the seam and fell over onto his side in the ash of the Vaeloris quarter, with his right hand still closed, and did not open it.

Haruki's flare illuminated brightly, the boundary between Kujo's figure and the void began to blurr, change and even shift. He was no longer welcome here, the boundary required correction, and correction required the absence of Kujo. Haruki watched closely, her eyes seeing Kujo's figure. The opening was clear, the Dawn produced by the light and heat of luminalis shined brightly, her expression sharpening onto Kujo's vulnerable frame.

With moment's notice, she focused the remainder of her aether into the single point of her palm. Her figure blurring between the lines, the silence broken between the land as she appeared beside her master, her hand extended outward. The energy collected into the palm, and expulsing outwardly from it's center point.

"Paru Number Seventy-Two, EXILE SANCTION!" She focused her Saishiki onto Kujo, the purity and light began to envelop him, the authority with the mix of grace, exile, edict, and excision began to shift and mix into a wild card of a mix, the glyphs working together in the chain. The force pressing against Kujo's frame as the brand as placed onto his chest. The mark burning and etching into his vessel and spirit, the mixture of expression forming a compound that definied the purity of Haruki's judgement.

The law pass through, the boundary between the void and the material world began to shift and open, Kujo's frame dragging across the ground as the space around him began to close in and crunch down onto him. The radiance was bold, unique, but yet fair. The harmonic pulses pushed against as the counter-frequency pressed against him, the harmonic force enchaining Kujo's figure, setting the ritual's seal and principal.

The boundary shifted, Kujo's frame absorbing back into the darkness and frigid cold of the void. The gate closing behind him, Haruki's palm lowered as she caught her breath. her arm dropping limp as the harmonic field dropped around her, the power silencing itself, as the peace returned into a simple form. A-soft smile formed across her face.

"Peace and Silence, finally..." She spoke as a soft exhale left her lips, looking over at the rising sun in the background. The exhaustion hit her like a tidal wave, her frame collapsing over as she remained on the ground silently, her heart still beating, but her body numb and sore from the battle's previous hardships.

Asante carried Dawn Guard himself.

Zuberi’s body was heavy against him, far heavier than Asante remembered him ever seeming in life, because the weight no longer adjusted, braced, or met him halfway. One of Asante’s arms was hooked beneath Zuberi’s shoulders while the other supported him lower across the back and waist, forcing the boy to lean beneath the size of the man he refused to leave behind. His steps were slow, uneven, but he never asked anyone else to take him.

The crying had not stopped.

It had simply become quiet.

Tears continued sliding down Asante’s cheeks without sobbing now, gathering beneath the blood dried beneath his Origin Eye and falling from his jaw onto Zuberi’s clothes. Every few steps his face tightened as though another wave of grief had reached him, but he swallowed it and kept walking. His fingers occasionally clenched against Zuberi’s clothing, small unconscious movements that looked almost like he was checking whether there might still be something there to answer.

The battlefield had become strangely distant around him.

Soldiers moved between shelters, wounded practitioners were carried past on stretchers, and farther away the sounds of fighting against Val Nihil’s creatures still rolled through the city. Asante barely looked toward any of it. For those few minutes, the only thing in his world was getting Zuberi somewhere clean.

Then Ara saw them.

She had been moving between the shelters when her eyes found Asante first, and something about the way he was walking made her stop before she had even understood what he was carrying. Her gaze lowered from the boy’s tear-streaked face to the long body held against him, to the familiar clothes, the gold, the dark hair, and finally the complete absence of movement. Recognition struck her all at once.

“Zuberi…”

The name barely came out.

Ara’s knees folded beneath her before she seemed aware she was falling, and she caught herself badly against the ground with both hands. Her breath broke into a sound that was too sharp to be called speech, and then she began crying with the kind of helplessness that had no dignity left to protect. She stared at Zuberi as though looking long enough might force the image to become something else.

Asante stopped.

He looked at Ara, but he did not know what to say to her.

The tears continued silently down his own face as he stood there holding Zuberi between them. For a moment neither grief resembled the other, Ara’s came out broken and audible while Asante’s had become almost frighteningly still.

“He saved me.”

Asante’s voice was soft.

His eyes dropped toward Zuberi’s face, and his mouth trembled before he managed the rest. “Kujo fired at me, and he got in front of it.” Asante swallowed, then whispered, “He wasn’t supposed to.”

Ara broke harder.

She lowered her head until her forehead nearly touched the ground, one hand covering her mouth while the other dug into the dirt beneath her. Every breath became another attempt to speak his name, and most of them failed before sound could properly form. The Sovereign she had known as something almost impossible to remove from the world was lying motionless in the arms of a crying child.

Asante started walking again.

He did not do it because Ara’s grief meant nothing to him, but because standing there with Zuberi exposed beneath the ruined sky suddenly felt wrong. He adjusted the body carefully against himself, struggling beneath the weight until he found a position he could carry again. “I’m taking him inside,” he said quietly.

The medical tent was already crowded.

Healers and attendants moved between beds carrying water, cloth, Essence instruments, splints, and whatever remained usable after the battle. Their movement slowed when Asante entered with Dawn Guard in his arms. Nobody needed to ask what had happened.

Asante carried him to an empty cot.

He lowered Zuberi carefully, one arm remaining beneath his shoulders until the very last possible moment before he finally let him rest against the bedding. Even then, Asante kept one hand against Zuberi’s upper arm as though removing both hands at once would make the separation too final. Silent tears fell onto the white cloth beneath them.

“He needs to be cleaned.”

Asante spoke to the nearest attendant without raising his voice.

There was something almost practical in the sentence, the small desperate practicality of someone finding one task that could still be done after everything important had become impossible. His gaze moved across the blackened blood around Zuberi’s mouth, the dried trails beneath his eyes, the ruined clothing around the wound, and the dirt that had collected across him during the battle. “Please clean him.”

The attendants began carefully.

They brought warm water first, then clean cloths, moving with the gentleness normally reserved for someone still capable of feeling discomfort. Blood was wiped from Zuberi’s face in slow passes, then from his neck, hands, and the exposed portions of his skin. Nobody hurried.

Asante stood beside the cot the entire time.

He watched every movement, his breathing quiet and shallow while tears continued appearing faster than he bothered wiping them away. When one attendant reached toward the gold around Zuberi’s body, Asante’s hand moved instinctively before stopping himself. He stared for a moment, then slowly lowered it again.

“Leave that on him.”

His voice remained calm.

“The gold.”

Asante looked down at Zuberi, remembering how naturally the ornaments had always belonged against him, how much they had seemed like part of the person rather than decoration. “He should still look like himself.”

Ara reached the tent sometime later.

Her face was swollen from crying, and she had needed help simply making it across the distance after her legs had given out beneath her. She stopped just inside the entrance when she saw the cleaned portion of Zuberi’s face against the pillow. Whatever fragile control she had recovered disappeared again.

She approached slowly.

Every step looked like something she had to negotiate with herself.

When Ara finally reached the side of the cot, her hand hovered above Zuberi’s chest before settling lightly against him. There was no rise beneath her palm.

“No…”

Ara shook her head.

Her fingers tightened against his clothing while her tears fell freely again. “No, Zuberi.” She bent over him, shoulders trembling as the reality of the stillness refused to change.

Asante remained on the opposite side.

He did not tell her to stop crying.

He did not tell her that Zuberi had died bravely, or that he had protected someone, or that his death meant something, because none of those things made the body between them warmer. He simply stood beside her while his own tears continued falling in silence.

After a while, Asante reached for Zuberi’s hand.

The hand was enormous compared with his.

He wrapped both of his around it and stood there looking down at the man who had stepped between him and death without hesitation. The Origin Eye could show him beginnings, causes, first recognitions, and the exact moment an event became inevitable, but none of that sight offered him a road back to before Kujo fired.

Asante lowered his head.

“I’m sorry.”

There was no scream in it this time.

Only a child speaking quietly beside a cleaned body while a woman cried across from him.

Outside the medical tent, Val Nihil was still falling from the sky.

Inside, for just a little while, Asante allowed himself to stop fighting.
